| [[2607.07459\|EmbodiedGen V2]] | sim-ready scene-generation substrate with part-level *affordance autolabeling* (functional segmentation + VLM semantics + physics-validated grasps) + LLM world editing | 96.5% asset human-acceptance / 98.6% collision success; affordance autolabeling +19 pp end-to-end pass rate at 94 s/asset; mobile-manip transfer 21.7%→75.0%, cube-stack 43.1%→88.9% | the affordance-autolabeling substrate A1's H3 needs (auto-labeled semantics closing the physical-annotation bottleneck the survey flags), sibling to [[2602.09153\|SceneSmith]]'s physical-feasibility grounding, but assigns affordance/material semantics once at generation time, never re-sampled per episode in a physics loop |
| [[2512.18396\|AOMGen]] | physics-consistent *articulated-object* demo generation from one real scan + one demo: part-level segmented 3DGS recovers joint motion from contact-point trajectories, then swaps in same-category assets via contact-mapped geometry optimization + relighting | 88.66% VLA success from 0% on 150 generated demos; 98% simulator-replay fidelity across 5 replacement assets; mixed-object training 65% vs 15% single-object on an unseen object | demonstration-*generation*-time asset variation (like [[2604.10579\|AffordGen]]/[[2410.07408\|Digital-Cousins-ACDC]]) for *articulated* objects specifically — same-category swap is a semantic axis, but assigned once per generated demo, not re-sampled per training episode in a physics loop |
| [[2608.11876\|D3D-GEN]] | robot-aware *domain-grounded* scene-generation substrate: a web-research domain agent builds a provenance-tagged constraint database (codes, robot-clearance rules), an LLM/RAG pipeline turns it into a scene graph + floorplan + collision-checked asset placement | 450 worlds across 3 domains beat floorplan/population baselines on density (54.56 objects, 7.06/room) + VQA realism (0.400); hospital domain covers 82 room types across 9 jurisdictions | the domain-*grounded* sibling of [[2602.09153\|SceneSmith]]/[[2607.07459\|EmbodiedGen V2]] — constraints come from real building-code research rather than a generic prior, but semantics/affordance are still assigned once at world-generation time, not resampled per episode |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (randomizing affordance/material *semantics* beats appearance-only DR), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Per-episode in-loop semantic resampling beats every fixed-timing baseline.**
   - *Prediction*: coupling [[2604.25459|GS-Playground]]'s renderer to a physics engine and randomizing affordance labels + material parameters per episode lifts [[2604.11674|AffordSim]]-class real SR >20 pp over each of three fixed-timing conditions — appearance-only DR ([[2506.18088|RoboTwin-2.0]]-style), fixed-cousin asset randomization ([[2410.07408|Digital-Cousins-ACDC]] / [[2606.28276|SimFoundry]]), and no-photoreal task diversity ([[2603.16861|MolmoBot]]) — at matched render/diversity budget, with the gain concentrated on the hardest affordance task (mug-hang ~10%).
   - *Test*: train four policies — {per-episode semantic resampling} vs {appearance-only DR} vs {fixed-cousin asset randomization} vs {no-photoreal task diversity} — at equal render/diversity compute; report real SR per affordance-task tier for all four conditions.
   - *Row*: AffordSim (affordance in data) / GS-Playground (appearance substrate) / Digital Cousins (fixed-cousin) / RoboTwin 2.0 (appearance-DR) / MolmoBot (no-photoreal diversity).
   - *Falsifier*: any fixed-timing condition matches per-episode resampling at matched budget → timing is not the lever, only "randomize the cause" is (already consensus).
2. **H2 — Semantic randomization adds real-SR *on top of* the fidelity gain Grounding-Study found.**
   - *Prediction*: in a 2×2 {PBR low/high} × {semantic-randomization off/on}, the semantic main-effect is non-zero *even at high PBR* — i.e. it survives the fidelity-helps finding of [[2603.22876|Grounding-Sim-to-Real]] rather than being absorbed by it.
   - *Test*: run the 2×2 at matched render budget on [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s standardized per-axis perturbation harness (distractor/color/texture/lighting — its worst factors, 30–50% single-perturbation SR drop), grounding VISER's "drops drastically under distractors" on a named protocol; report semantic main-effect and the semantic×PBR interaction, not just whether semantics beats realism.
   - *Row*: VISER (realism floor) / Grounding Sim2Real Study (fidelity-helps factorial) / THE COLOSSEUM (per-axis OOD-perturbation harness) vs AffordSim (semantic gap).
   - *Falsifier*: the semantic main-effect vanishes at high PBR → semantics is redundant once fidelity is high and the gain was confounded with rendering.
3. **H3 — The gain is carried by material-parameter resampling, not affordance-label resampling (or both, additively).**
   - *Prediction*: ablating affordance-label resampling and material-parameter resampling independently shows the two contribute unevenly — material resampling (which changes the physics rollout, not just the label) carries more of the real-SR gain than affordance-label resampling alone, since [[2604.11674|AffordSim]]'s own diagnostic shows affordance-aware *data* alone caps at ~24% zero-shot.
   - *Test*: train three per-episode conditions — {affordance-label only} vs {material-parameter only} vs {both} — at matched budget; report real SR per condition and whether the combination is additive or dominated by one factor.
   - *Row*: AffordSim (affordance in data) / GaussianProperty (material semantics) vs GS-Playground (appearance substrate).
   - *Falsifier*: affordance-label resampling alone matches the combined gain → material resampling is decorative and the mechanism is purely label-driven.
4. **H4 — The gain concentrates on geometrically-ambiguous tasks and vanishes where affordance is geometrically determined.**
   - *Prediction*: per-episode semantic resampling's real-SR gain is largest on tasks where the affordance is visually/geometrically ambiguous (mug-hang ~10% baseline) and shrinks toward zero on tasks where the affordance is unambiguous from geometry alone — so the bet is scoped to the ambiguous regime, not a universal lift.
   - *Test*: stratify [[2604.11674|AffordSim]]'s task tiers (Easy/Medium/Hard) by affordance ambiguity; report the per-episode-resampling SR gain per tier.
   - *Row*: AffordSim (affordance in data, Easy/Medium/Hard tiers).
   - *Falsifier*: the gain is uniform across ambiguity tiers → ambiguity is not the boundary condition and the bet is mis-scoped.
5. **H5 — Resampling frequency shows a dose-response relationship, extending Grounding-Sim-to-Real's frame-wise-beats-episode-wise finding to the semantic axis.**
   - *Prediction*: resampling affordance/material semantics every episode beats resampling every N>1 episodes, which beats a single fixed assignment — a monotonic frequency-to-SR relationship mirroring [[2603.22876|Grounding-Sim-to-Real]]'s frame-wise>episode-wise pattern on the appearance axis, now on the semantic axis.
   - *Test*: sweep resampling frequency (every episode / every 5 episodes / every 50 episodes / fixed-once) at matched total render budget; report real SR vs frequency.
   - *Row*: Grounding Sim2Real Study (frame-wise>episode-wise, appearance axis) / AffordSim (affordance in data).
   - *Falsifier*: SR is flat across resampling frequency → granularity is not the lever and only "randomized vs fixed" matters, not "how often."

> [!warning] Risks
> - **The >20 pp claim sits near the real-robot noise floor** — a >20 pp per-task SR margin at REALM-class N~50 trials is barely above the binomial standard error (a 50%-SR cell has SE ≈ 7 pp, so a 20 pp difference is ~2–3 SE before multiple-comparison correction across affordance-task tiers). → Gate H1/H2 on a pre-registered bootstrap-CI / minimum-detectable-effect (MDE) power check *before* running the semantics-vs-appearance ablation: fix the per-tier trial budget so the targeted margin clears the binomial SE at the chosen confidence, or report the experiment as underpowered rather than as a positive result.
> - **Neural rendering in the loop is compute-heavy** — physics + 3DGS per step may blow the training budget. → [[2604.25459|GS-Playground]]'s point-pruning + batch rendering already demonstrates 10,000 FPS is tractable at appearance-only scale; discard auxiliary heads at deploy and re-profile once H1's per-episode semantic pass is added.
> - **Semantic randomization needs semantic ground truth the field lacks at scale** — affordance labels are scarce. → Bootstrap from [[2604.11674|AffordSim]]'s VoxAfford auto-labeling on [[2604.26509|3D-Generation-for-Embodied-AI-Survey]]-style generated assets rather than hand annotation; H3's affordance-label-only condition is the direct test of whether auto-labeled semantics carry real signal.
> - **Gains may be confounded with realism** — a richer renderer could improve SR for reasons unrelated to semantics. → H2's semantics-vs-appearance ablation at fixed PBR is the go/no-go; report the semantic main-effect separately.

### A2 — Reward-Signal Sim-to-Real: Transferring PINN-Estimated Physics Rewards, Not Actions

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Forward Sim-to-Real |
| **Thesis** | Sim-to-real almost always transfers *actions* — a policy or its distilled student. Transferring the *reward* instead is no longer novel: learned reward functions cross embodiments and tasks already. But every learned reward is fit to a data distribution and drifts under dynamics shift, whereas a reward grounded in a physical law is a function of physical *state*, so it scores any trajectory correctly on hardware the policy never saw. The field assumes a transferable reward must be *learned*. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]] (method), [[2511.15200\|VIRAL]] (method), [[2603.15956\|ExpertGen]] (method), [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] (survey), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | GRF-predictor R²=0.99/0.99 sensor-free ([[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]], up from 0.39/0.67 supervised; 82–86% error reduction from the inverse-dynamics constraint); −7.17 dBA mean / −4.98 dB peak noise transferred across 4 footwear types + outdoor terrains; cross-condition reward stability where DR action policies plateau |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: most sim-to-real pipelines harden the *action* mapping — [[2511.15200|VIRAL]] distills a teacher into a vision student, [[2603.15956|ExpertGen]] distills experts into visuomotor policies, [[2210.13702|DeXtreme]] transfers a PPO policy — so the thing crossing the gap inherits the *simulated* dynamics it was hardened on. Transferring the *reward* instead is a mature alternative ([[2106.03911|XIRL]] cross-embodiment reward since 2021, [[2206.00238|DARL]] dynamics-agnostic discriminator, [[2405.19988|Video-Language-Critic]], [[2406.01967|DrEureka]]) — but each reward is *learned* from data, so it drifts under the dynamics shift it was meant to survive.
- **Today's answers**: [[2604.23702|QuietWalk]] grounds the reward in a *law* instead of data — an inverse-dynamics PINN estimates per-foot ground-reaction force from proprioception alone (R²=0.99/0.99), the *frozen predictor* is dropped into the reward as a critic, and the policy then generalizes across barefoot / skate-shoes / sneakers / high-heels and outdoor terrains it never trained on. The PINN encodes a physical law, so its output is invariant to the appearance and contact-dynamics changes that drift a learned reward like [[2405.19988|Video-Language-Critic]]'s.
- **The opening**: the inverse-dynamics *constraint* is the lever, not the network — enforcing it cuts GRF error 82–86% vs a purely supervised predictor and lifts R² from 0.39/0.67 to 0.99/0.99, exactly the fidelity a reward needs to hold off-distribution. [[2605.00080|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] frames the rationale: a model's value is "its utility for action and physical consistency, not visual realism."

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A reward grounded in a physical law (momentum, Newton's third law, contact mechanics) is a function of physical *state*, not of training distribution — it scores any trajectory correctly, on hardware the policy never saw. Actions are distribution-bound; physics-grounded rewards are distribution-free. [[2604.23702|QuietWalk]] supplies the load-bearing evidence: the inverse-dynamics constraint — a physical law, not data — is what drives the 82–86% error reduction and the R²=0.99, so the reward is invariant precisely because the constraint is.
- **Assumption being challenged**: That a transferable reward must be *learned* — the learned-reward orthodoxy ([[2106.03911|XIRL]], [[2206.00238|DARL]], [[2405.19988|Video-Language-Critic]], [[2406.01967|DrEureka]]) that crosses the gap with a visual-embedding / discriminator / video-language / LLM-designed reward. Each is fit to a distribution, so it drifts under appearance and dynamics shift; a PINN-estimated force reward is a function of physical state, so the high-heels contact-dynamics shift that drifts a learned reward leaves the law-grounded reward intact. (The "reward not policy" framing itself is already consensus — the novelty is *distribution-free*, not *reward-instead-of-action*.)
- **The bet**: A physics-law-grounded reward (GRF, contact wrench) retains its objective across *contact-dynamics* shifts where a *learned* transferable reward drifts — holding [[2604.23702|QuietWalk]]'s **R²=0.99 sensor-free** accuracy and the **−7.17 dBA** objective across **4 footwear types** and outdoor terrains, while a [[2405.19988|Video-Language-Critic]]-style learned reward and a [[2511.15200|VIRAL]]-style DR action policy both lose retention under the same shift. The lever is the inverse-dynamics *constraint* (H2): removing it collapses R² toward 0.39/0.67. Cross-*embodiment* portability (robot A's PINN as robot B's reward) is the speculative extension H3 probes — no existence proof yet. Falsifiable: if a learned transferable reward matches the physics-law reward on cross-footwear retention, the law-grounded signal is not the more distribution-free object.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what crosses the gap, and whether it is a physical law* (physics reward / action policy / hand-designed reward / physics-as-input / transferable representation) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | What crosses the gap | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]] | a frozen PINN *force reward* (inverse-dynamics-constrained) | R²=0.99/0.99 sensor-free, −7.17 dBA, 4-footwear + outdoor robust | single-robot cross-*condition* only — cross-embodiment reward portability untested |
| [[2511.15200\|VIRAL]] | the *action* (teacher→vision student) | 54/59 real loco-manip cycles; RSI ablation 95% vs <10% | the canonical action-transfer pipeline — inherits the simulated dynamics it hardened on |
| [[2603.15956\|ExpertGen]] | the *action* (behavior prior + DSRL + distillation) | 90.5% [[2407.08028\|AutoMate]] | action transfer; reward stays sparse, never re-grounded in real physics |
| [[2210.13702\|DeXtreme]] | the *action* (VADR + PPO), hand-designed reward | 27.8 vs 14.8 reorientations (VADR vs manual DR) | reward is hand-engineered, not a physical law — not transferable as a signal |
| [[2106.03911\|XIRL]] | a *learned reward* (visual-embedding IRL), cross-embodiment | self-supervised reward transfers across embodiments | reward is learned from video embeddings, not a law — drifts under appearance/dynamics shift; the foil for "distribution-free" |
| [[2206.00238\|DARL]] | a *learned reward* (dynamics-agnostic discriminator ensemble) | transfers reward across dynamics gaps | discriminator is fit to data — invariance is empirical, not law-grounded |
| [[2405.19988\|Video-Language-Critic]] | a *learned reward* (contrastive + sequential-ranking video-language) | 72% avg SR unseen tasks, 2× sample efficiency cross-embodiment | dense learned reward, transferable but distribution-bound — the closest learned-reward competitor |
| [[2406.01967\|DrEureka]] | a *learned reward* (LLM-designed) + DR config for sim2real | LLM-authored reward enables zero-shot sim2real | reward designed by an LLM from task text, not a physical law — no invariance guarantee under contact shift |
| [[2511.07416\|PhysWorld]] | the *reward*, but grounded in a *reconstructed twin* | 82% real, object-centric residual RL | reward is twin-bound (per-object), not a portable physical law |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | physics *as policy input* (CoM, friction) | 57% vs 23% (weight-top T-block) | physics conditions the policy; it is not a transferable reward |
| [[2601.02778\|Force-Based-Sim2Real]] | force *via the policy* (tactile feedback) | 25.1 vs 1.1 rotations with/without contact sensing | force is the transferable quantity but rides the policy, not the reward |
| [[2605.28812\|CoP-Tactile]] | a physics-grounded contact *representation* (Center-of-Pressure) | 0.78 peg-in-hole zero-shot, latents cluster by mass | a transferable *representation*, not a reward — the input-side cousin |
| [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] | a prediction-residual adaptation *trigger* | [[2301.04104\|DreamerV3]] residual → online fine-tune | the online loop a transferable reward would drive — not itself a transfer signal |
| [[2605.22123\|FLORA]] | a *learned reward* invariant by *abstraction* (object-centric motion-flow + symbolic PBRS-MS, ≤5 demos) | 0.97 Spearman ρ on train tasks, ~100% Peg-Insert/Box-Open + only method to solve Bulb-Unscrew (45%), zero-shot real-world OOD | pursues invariance by abstracting away pixels, not by a physical law — the sharpest learned-side competitor to "distribution-free"; if its flow-abstraction reward holds across the *contact-dynamics* shift (not just appearance/object OOD), the law-grounding lever is not unique. The FP falsifier homes here |
| [[2603.02115\|Robometer]] | a *learned reward* (VLM progress + preference), scaled cross-embodiment over 21 robots incl. suboptimal/failed | VOC Pearson r=0.95 / Kendall τa=0.66 OOD (vs 0.88/0.47 RoboReward-8B), 2.5× online-RL SR, F1=0.81 zero-shot failure detection on unseen robot | the largest-scale *learned* cross-embodiment reward — strengthens H1's learned comparator and H3's cross-embodiment claim, but invariance is fit from 1M trajectories, not a law; drifts under the contact-dynamics shift [[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]]'s constraint survives. Distinct from [[2405.19988\|Video-Language-Critic]]'s 4-platform scale |
| [[2605.08774\|ProcVLM]] | a *learned reward* grounded in *semantic procedural structure* (subtask stages, not temporal proxies) | VOC_succ +85.7% / MAE_fail −38.0% one-shot on unseen RoboFAC tasks; +25.0 pp real-robot Stack-Bowls SR | grounded in learned *procedural* structure, the explicit "grounded-but-not-in-physics" foil to [[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]]'s law-grounding — procedural structure is still distribution-bound; it crosses *task* boundaries, not the appearance/contact-dynamics shift the physics reward is built to survive |
| [[2605.11020\|TRIRL]] | a *learned reward* (MCE-IRL via explicit dual ascent) claimed dynamics-disentangled by construction | 2.4× aggregate IQM performance factor; transfers reward across altered gravity / disabled joints, beating AIRL/NEAR | the IRL-theory statement of the orthodoxy A2 challenges — disentanglement is an empirical guarantee of the recovered reward, not a physical law; the theory-side cousin of [[2206.00238\|DARL]]'s discriminator, still fit to the demonstrator's distribution rather than scoring physical state |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a physics-grounded reward transfers across conditions where action policies don't), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Physics-law reward beats a *learned* reward (and action transfer) on cross-condition retention.**
   - *Prediction*: training one contact task three ways — a [[2604.23702|QuietWalk]]-style PINN-force reward, a [[2405.19988|Video-Language-Critic]]-style *learned* reward, and a DR+distillation action policy ([[2511.15200|VIRAL]]-style) — and evaluating across held-out conditions (footwear/terrain/payload), the physics-law reward retains the most real SR, with the gap over the *learned* reward widest under the hardest contact-dynamics shift (high heels).
   - *Test*: matched task + base RL, swap only the transferable object across all three; report cross-condition SR retention by shift severity, homing the learned-reward comparator on [[2602.19313|TOPReward]] (130 real-world manipulation tasks across 4 robot platforms, subtask progress + completion/failure annotations, VOC 0.942–0.954 — a reward-quality suite with explicit cross-embodiment robustness, though manipulation/learned-reward not locomotion GRF).
   - *Row*: QuietWalk (physics reward) vs Video-Language Critic (learned reward) / TOPReward (learned-reward benchmark) vs VIRAL (action transfer).
   - *Falsifier*: a learned reward matches the physics-law reward across conditions → law-grounding is not the distribution-free lever, only "reward not policy" is.
2. **H2 — The inverse-dynamics constraint, not the network, is the transfer lever.**
   - *Prediction*: removing the inverse-dynamics constraint (purely supervised GRF predictor) collapses R² from 0.99 toward 0.39/0.67 *and* the resulting reward fails to hold the −7.17 dBA objective off-distribution — so the constraint is what makes the reward distribution-free.
   - *Test*: ablate the inverse-dynamics term; measure GRF R² and cross-footwear objective retention with vs without it.
   - *Row*: QuietWalk (physics reward).
   - *Falsifier*: the supervised predictor's reward transfers equally → the physical constraint is decorative.
3. **H3 — Reward portability across embodiments (the speculative extension).**
   - *Prediction*: a GRF/contact-wrench PINN trained on robot A's proprioception gives a usable reward on robot B without re-collecting demos, within a measurable morphology-distance envelope beyond which R² degrades below a usable threshold.
   - *Test*: transfer the frozen PINN reward A→B; map cross-embodiment SR vs morphology distance on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s morphology-distance protocol (18 robot variations — 8 procedural + 10 real — with explicit interpolation / extrapolation / composition splits supplying the measurable morphology-distance envelope, though it measures policy cross-embodiment transfer, manipulation reach/push, not reward portability).
   - *Row*: QuietWalk (physics reward) / AnyBody (morphology-distance protocol) / CoP Tactile (transferable representation).
   - *Falsifier*: the cross-embodiment reward is unusable at any morphology distance → portability is single-robot only, as QuietWalk currently shows.
4. **H4 — Sensor-free reward holds where the policy lacks force sensors.**
   - *Prediction*: using the PINN-estimated force as the *only* reward on hardware without force sensors recovers most of the on-sensor SR, with a measurable trust envelope where R² stays above threshold and beyond which the reward misleads.
   - *Test*: deploy sensor-free PINN reward vs ground-truth-force reward; map R² degradation off-manifold and the SR cliff.
   - *Row*: QuietWalk (physics reward) / Force-Based Sim2Real (force via policy).
   - *Falsifier*: sensor-free reward degrades immediately off the training distribution → the reward needs the sensor it was meant to replace.
5. **H5 — Physics-reward generalizes only on conservation/inverse-dynamics tasks.**
   - *Prediction*: the transfer advantage holds on contact/force-dominated tasks where a conservation law or inverse-dynamics constraint exists, and vanishes on semantic tasks where no physical law grounds the reward — so the bet is bounded to the physics regime.
   - *Test*: contrast cross-condition retention on force-dominated vs semantic tasks; report where the physics-reward advantage disappears.
   - *Row*: QuietWalk (physics reward) vs DeXtreme (hand-designed reward).
   - *Falsifier*: the physics-reward transfers on semantic tasks too → the conservation-law boundary is not real and the claim under-scopes.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **PINN rewards exist for few physical quantities** — GRF is clean; arbitrary task rewards are not physical laws. → Bound the claim to contact/force-dominated tasks where a conservation law or inverse-dynamics constraint actually exists (H5); do not over-claim to semantic tasks.
> - **Reward transfer ≠ policy transfer** — a transferable reward still needs an on-hardware optimization loop. → Pair with [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting-RL]]'s online fine-tune so the transferable reward drives fast real adaptation rather than from-scratch RL.
> - **PINN degrades off-manifold** — R²=0.99 holds on the training distribution; far OOD it may mislead the reward. → H4 measures the trust envelope explicitly; gate reward use on residual magnitude, and fall back where R² drops below threshold.

### A3 — Controller-Gain-Aware Sim-to-Real: Co-Optimizing Dynamics and Control

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Forward Sim-to-Real |
| **Thesis** | Randomizing PD gains is already routine DR practice, and runtime adaptive-gain prediction already beats fixed gains on contact tasks. What no one tests is whether the gain and the dynamics *interact* — whether co-optimizing them jointly captures a super-additive term that the standard "tune gains for sysID, then randomize dynamics around them" recipe structurally misses. The field assumes gain and dynamics randomization are separable. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.02523\|Tune-to-Learn]] (method), [[2602.23253\|SPARR]] (method), [[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]] (method), [[2505.24068\|DiffCoTune]] (method), [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] (survey), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2603.15469\|RoCo-Challenge]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | Recover the transfer SR stiff gains destroy *despite* lowest sysID error ([[2604.02523\|Tune-to-Learn]]); reach RL's 99%+ regime across gain settings without per-gain hand-tuning; beat best-fixed-gain DR on [[2407.08028\|AutoMate]]-class contact tasks (95–100% via [[2602.23253\|SPARR]]), anchored to [[2603.15469\|RoCo-Challenge]]'s sub-mm planetary-gearbox assembly with paired Simulation + Onsite (real) tracks measuring the Sim-to-Real Cliff (300+ demos, 60+ teams) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the field treats gains as a fixed robot property, or tunes them for low tracking error during sysID — but the gain is really an unrecognized sim-to-real hyperparameter, setting the dynamics distribution the policy trains against, the action-space smoothness, and the deployment oscillation spectrum all at once.
- **Today's answers**: [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]] is the result almost nobody has internalized — the gains with the *lowest* sysID error (stiff, overdamped) give the *worst* transfer, amplifying high-frequency oscillation on hardware; RL can reach 99%+ across all gain regimes, but only with per-gain tuning. The competing fix is *runtime* adaptive gains: [[2505.00991|DexCtrl]] jointly learns action + adaptive gains and beats fixed-gain baselines on contact-rich dexterity "without excessive randomization", [[2311.07499|Dynamic-Compliance-Tuning]] tunes compliance online for industrial insertion, [[2502.14457|Watch-Less,-Feel-More]] adapts motion + impedance for articulated objects — all *predict gains at runtime* rather than co-optimizing them into the training distribution.
- **The opening**: [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]]'s own pairing of per-gain sysID error against sim2real SR *and* oscillation spectrum is the legible metric — the low-sysID-error gains are precisely the high-oscillation, worst-transferring ones, so the objective the field minimizes is anti-correlated with the goal.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The controller is part of the plant the policy controls — gains set the closed-loop dynamics, action smoothness, and high-frequency response, so they belong in the transfer distribution, not a separate pre-tuning step. Tuning gains for tracking error optimizes the wrong closed-loop. [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]] demonstrates it directly: lowest-sysID-error gains are the worst-transferring, so the gain is a *transfer* variable, not a fidelity one.
- **Assumption being challenged**: [[2509.06342|Towards-bridging-the-gap]] (PACE — Bjelonic, Tischhauser, Hutter at ETH's Robotic Systems Lab, the ANYmal group) argues the opposite of joint co-optimization, and backs it with real hardware evidence, not just a default: "because PACE identifies the dynamics end-to-end, we do not use dynamics randomization" at all — task and terrain are still randomized, but dynamics are not — and the PACE-fitted simulator held near-overlap with real in-air trajectories "even when validating with different PD gains and unseen random joint step trajectories," i.e. the *same* identified parameters stayed faithful under gain settings other than the ones used to identify them. Precise identification at known, fixed gains is offered as a *replacement* for randomization, not merely a separate pre-tuning step. That is a genuinely contested position, not an inherited default — and it collides directly with [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]]'s own finding in this same table: gain choice interacts with the DR-trained dynamics distribution strongly enough that the lowest-sysID-error (stiffest) gains transfer *worst*, which only makes sense if gain and dynamics distribution are coupled. So the open disagreement in A3's own literature is which regime wins: PACE's precision-replaces-randomization route (no DR needed once identification is exact) vs. Tune-to-Learn's evidence that within a DR-trained distribution, gain and dynamics already interact. [[2606.22062|Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation]] now supplies a controlled (if narrower) data point for PACE's side: in a sim-to-sim pendulum sweep of identification rollouts against randomization width, training at the point estimate beats every wider band by a paired margin of 165–198 return, and a band that merely *brackets* the true parameter without narrowing to it performs no better than skipping identification altogether — direct evidence that coverage is not a substitute for measurement. But the sweep is single-DOF, contact-free, free-space dynamics — precisely the regime A3's own H5 predicts a null in ("the effect is contact-specific; free-space shows a null") — so it sharpens, rather than resolves, which regime wins: it is adverse to a *free-space* gain×dynamics interaction, not to the contact-task interaction H2 bets on. A3's bet targets neither PACE's nor Tune-to-Learn's route directly — [[2505.24068|DiffCoTune]] co-optimizes gains and dynamics jointly by gradient, but only per-system against a *fixed* real-world deployment target, never against a *randomized* distribution — so the open question is narrower than "separable vs joint": whether co-optimizing gains *against a randomized* dynamics distribution captures an interaction term neither PACE's precision route, DiffCoTune's fixed-target route, nor Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation's free-space identification route reaches.
- **The bet**: As the first test of co-optimization against a *randomized* dynamics distribution (not [[2505.24068|DiffCoTune]]'s fixed per-system target): co-optimizing $(K_p, K_d)$ *jointly with* the dynamics distribution captures a non-empty **gain×dynamics interaction term** — joint beats (gain-only + dynamics-only) by **more than their sum** on [[2407.08028|AutoMate]]-class contact tasks, *and* the co-optimized DR variable beats a [[2505.00991|DexCtrl]]/[[2606.06218|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]]-style runtime gain residual at matched SR (preventing the mismatch beats patching it). Falsifiable: if joint = gain-only + dynamics-only (additive) **or** the runtime residual matches co-optimization, the interaction term is empty and co-optimization buys nothing.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how control gains are treated relative to the transfer distribution* (fixed/sysID-tuned / residual-absorbed / co-optimized / implicit-via-action-structure / named-but-unaddressed) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How gains relate to transfer | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.02523\|Tune-to-Learn]] | studied across BC/RL/sim2real; lowest-sysID-error = worst transfer | stiff gains worst transfer; RL 99%+ only with per-gain tuning | diagnoses the inversion but never *co-optimizes* gains with DR — the gap this fills |
| [[2505.24068\|DiffCoTune]] | co-optimized: gradient co-tunes differentiable-sim params *and* differentiable-controller PD gains jointly (LQR/MPC/TO/DeepRL controllers; cart-pole/quadruped/biped) | +60% sim-to-sim, Go1 +1.04 m in 5 s, 34 cm biped hardware jump where controller-only tuning falls | the closest realization of the co-optimization mechanism — but co-tunes gains+sim *per-system from real rollouts to match deployment* (offline-iterative sysID objective), never randomizes a dynamics distribution nor measures the gain×dynamics interaction; the strongest baseline H2's joint co-optimization must beat, not a preemption |
| [[2507.23445\|Physics-Guided-Gain-Regularization]] | gain-aware *training*: penalizes deviation of a neural controller's local input-output *sensitivities* (equivalent gains) from *empirically measured* hardware proportional gains, AND conditions the controller on current plant parameters | **GC-DR-RNN** keeps equivalent gains < **2.0** (vs DR-RNN's unbounded **4.0**); on a **110:1**-gearbox balancer, kills the DR-RNN's persistent **5 Hz** oscillation where naive DR shows a persistent gap | a *distinct* cell the table lacks — gain-as-transfer-variable via **sensitivity-matching + plant-conditioning**, not residual-absorbed ([[2602.23253\|SPARR]]/[[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]]), implicit-via-action-structure ([[2312.03673\|Action-Space-Sim-to-Real-Study]]), or pure runtime-adapt ([[2505.00991\|DexCtrl]]); it lands squarely on H3 (regularized gains suppress the oscillation spectrum stiff gains amplify) and on the gain×dynamics *conditioning* A3 argues for — the constructive counterpart of [[2604.02523\|Tune-to-Learn]]'s sysID-vs-transfer inversion and the strongest standalone gain-aware-*training* method A3 was missing; but it matches measured gains rather than *co-optimizing* gains with a randomized dynamics distribution, so it strengthens H3 without preempting H2's interaction-term claim |
| [[2602.23253\|SPARR]] | residual absorbs gain/dynamics mismatch after the base policy | 95–100% [[2407.08028\|AutoMate]], 74.5% NIST assembly | the residual patches mismatch post-hoc; gains never enter the randomization |
| [[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]] | policy-agnostic 1 kHz torque residual (multi-robot pretrain) | Franka pushing 47.6%→76.2%, zero-shot Google Robot (1.05° vs 4.69°) | actuation-residual absorbs gain mismatch, not a co-optimized gain variable |
| [[2505.00991\|DexCtrl]] | *runtime* adaptive gains jointly with action, "without excessive randomization" | beats fixed-gain baselines on contact-rich dexterity | the H4 alternative — predicts gains at *deploy-time*, never co-optimizes them into the training distribution |
| [[2311.07499\|Dynamic-Compliance-Tuning]] | *runtime* online compliance tuning for industrial insertion | bridges sim2real on insertion via online compliance | runtime gain adaptation, not a training-distribution variable — the residual-side competitor |
| [[2502.14457\|Watch-Less,-Feel-More]] | *runtime* motion-adaptation + impedance control (generalizable articulated) | sim-to-real RL on articulated objects via adapted impedance | adapts impedance online; gain never enters the DR distribution as a co-optimized variable |
| [[2210.13702\|DeXtreme]] | action-smoothness reward (implicit gain proxy) | 27.8 vs 14.8 reorientations | smoothness penalty acknowledges gains matter but never makes it a first-class co-optimization |
| [[2511.15200\|VIRAL]] | delta action space flagged critical in ablation | RSI 95% vs <10% | action structure governs transfer, gains not co-optimized |
| [[2603.15956\|ExpertGen]] | DSRL preserves the motion manifold while optimizing reward | 90.5% [[2407.08028\|AutoMate]] | manifold-preservation is gain-adjacent, not a gain×dynamics co-optimization |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | conditions on friction/CoM (the dynamics half) | 57% vs 23% OOD weight-top | the dynamics side of the coupling, no control-gain term |
| [[2305.17110\|IndustReal]] | PLAI integrator targets unmodeled-dynamics / steady-state error (the regime gains shape) | NIST-board transfer sim 82–99% / real 80% pegs, 97.5% gears, 100% NEMA; ~2 mm steady-state error, 600 real trials | the canonical NIST-board contact-rich assembly transfer benchmark (behind SPARR's NIST number) — a standardized transfer-SR substrate, but a fixed-gain integrator, no gain×dynamics co-optimization |
| [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] | names control-loop closure + physical inconsistency | survey | names the gap, doesn't spot gains as the lever |
| [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] | open-loop vs closed-loop divergence | survey | the closed-loop is where gains bite, but unnamed as a variable |
| [[2312.03673\|Action-Space-Sim-to-Real-Study]] | implicit-via-action-structure — controlled 13-action-space study (joint/Cartesian × position/velocity/torque × delta), the gain proxy made systematic | Joint Velocity best transfer (low OTE, high real ACC, low ECV); Joint Torque unsafe/unreliable for contact, across 250 PPO agents | the systematic version of DeXtreme's smoothness reward + VIRAL's delta-action flag — studies action structure as the gain lever but never co-optimizes gains with the dynamics distribution, and torque control (closest to a raw gain command) is exactly where it breaks, the contact regime A3 targets |
| [[2602.14174\|Direction-Matters]] | *runtime* force-aware admittance — regulates stiffness online along task-relevant directions, policy predicts dynamics-invariant force direction | 91% overall SR across 4 contact tasks (microwave/peg/wipe/door) vs 67% best baseline; 25.0 mm peg insertion depth | the H4 stiffness-adaptation competitor alongside DexCtrl/Dynamic Compliance Tuning/Watch Less Feel More — its finding that force *direction* survives the sim-to-real gap but *magnitude* does not sharpens H4, yet it still adapts stiffness at deploy-time rather than co-optimizing gains into the training distribution |
| [[2509.23075\|In-Hand-Articulated-Tools]] | *runtime* tactile+torque residual (CATFA) patching contact/gain mismatch online, with training-time curriculum force-torque perturbations | 100% SR across 5 articulated tools; 0.0 mm residual tip gap (surgical clamp), attenuated high-frequency oscillation vs baselines | the dexterous-articulated counterpart to [[2602.23253\|SPARR]]/[[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]]'s rigid-assembly residuals — adds an articulated-tool instance to the H4 residual set, but the gain mismatch is patched online (and only perturbed, not co-optimized, in training) rather than entering the DR distribution as a first-class gain variable |
| [[2607.02205\|Actuator Reality Shaping]] | *shapes the plant, not the gains*: modifies the real actuator's closed-loop response (cascaded 2-DoF controller + disturbance observer) to emulate the idealized second-order reference dynamics used in sim training | 96.3% mean position-deviation reduction (single-axis testbed) / 96.5% end-effector reduction (7-DoF arm) vs factory PID, beating a learned actuator-model baseline (ASAP); zero-shot to a 22-DoF humanoid + wheeled-legged slope climbing | a *distinct cell*: *prevent* the actuation gap by making the hardware match the sim's assumed dynamics, vs co-optimizing gains ([[2505.24068\|DiffCoTune]]), residual-absorbing ([[2602.23253\|SPARR]]/[[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]]), or runtime-adapting ([[2505.00991\|DexCtrl]]); beats the learned-actuator-model line but standardizes a *fixed* reference, so gains never enter as a co-optimized DR variable (H2's interaction term untouched) |
| [[2607.04940\|Dexterous Force-Based Grasping Sim-to-Real]] | *randomized actuator model* (backlash + velocity-dependent saturation) + data-driven current-to-torque calibration absorbs the actuation gap for sensor-free dexterous force control | zero-shot sim-to-real on a real multi-finger hand: force-adaptive grasping to 93.92 N on novel objects, up to 25.1 consecutive in-hand reorientations | the dexterous-hand instance of actuation-gap DR (backlash/torque without direct torque sensors) the rigid-arm residual rows leave open, but randomizes the actuator model rather than co-optimizing gains with dynamics, and the contact information rides the policy observation, not a co-optimized gain variable |
| [[2606.31043\|Warp RL]] | *expressive residual*: an invertible, state-conditioned RQ-spline-flow transformation reshapes the base policy's action distribution (scale/shape/geometry), generalizing additive residual RL | up to 70.6% PegInsertionSide SR vs 57.4% Residual-PPO; full recovery on ~50% of task-seeds; real peg-insertion −30% median completion time (4.65→3.25 s) at matched SR | makes the runtime-residual competitor A3's H4 must beat strictly more expressive than [[2602.23253\|SPARR]]/[[2606.06218\|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]]'s additive residual (warping shape/variance, not just the mean), so "co-optimized gains beat a runtime residual" must clear an expressive-residual bar; but it patches dynamics shift post-hoc, never entering gains into the training distribution |
| [[2607.11734\|NeuralActuator]] | *models the actuator itself*: a Transformer learns history-dependent non-idealities (friction/hysteresis/backlash) on low-cost servos, embedded end-to-end in a differentiable simulator to output simulator-equivalent effort surrogates + sensorless external-force estimates | joint-angle MAE 2.5°, force MAE 0.12 N (5.5× better than classical baselines), +10 pp lift-and-hold SR | a distinct cell from co-optimized gains ([[2505.24068\|DiffCoTune]]) or hardware-reshaping ([[2607.02205\|Actuator Reality Shaping]]): corrects the *simulator's actuator model* to match real non-idealities rather than tuning gains against a fixed actuator or reshaping hardware to match sim — but no gain×dynamics interaction test, single low-cost-servo platform |
| [[2606.22062\|Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation]] | *neither co-optimizes nor randomizes gains* — a controlled sim-to-sim identification-vs-randomization-width sweep (SAC on a hidden-parameter pendulum), no controller-gain axis at all | training at the identified point estimate beats every wider randomization band by 165–198 paired return; a truth-bracketing (non-narrowing) band performs no better than n=0 | the adverse H5-null instance A3 must reconcile against — single-DOF, contact-*free* dynamics, so it bears on the free-space regime H5 already predicts a null in, not on H2's contact-task gain×dynamics interaction; no gain variable, no contact, no co-optimization mechanism to test |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (co-optimizing gains with DR beats best-fixed-gain DR), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Gain-randomization beats dynamics-only DR on contact tasks.**
   - *Prediction*: sampling $K_p, K_d$ from a learned/randomized range during training beats fixing them and randomizing only dynamics, on [[2407.08028|AutoMate]]-class contact-task real SR, recovering the transfer [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]] shows stiff gains destroy.
   - *Test*: gain-randomized vs dynamics-only DR, matched everything else; report contact-task real SR on [[2603.15469|RoCo-Challenge]]'s Onsite (real) assembly track (sub-mm planetary-gearbox, named contact-rich real-SR suite with a paired sim track).
   - *Row*: Tune to Learn (gains-vs-sysID) / RoCo Challenge (sim+real assembly tracks).
   - *Falsifier*: dynamics-only DR matches gain-randomization → gains add nothing to the transfer distribution.
2. **H2 — There is a non-empty gain×dynamics interaction term fixed-then-randomize misses.**
   - *Prediction*: jointly co-optimizing $(K_p, K_d, \psi)$ via differentiable sim or bilevel search beats both gain-only and dynamics-only randomization by more than their sum — an interaction term the sequential recipe cannot capture.
   - *Test*: full-factorial {gain rand} × {dynamics rand} × {joint}; test for super-additivity, with [[2505.24068|DiffCoTune]]'s per-system differentiable-sim gain+param co-tuning as the matched co-optimization baseline (it co-tunes but never randomizes a distribution, so it cannot capture the interaction term).
   - *Row*: Tune to Learn (gains-vs-sysID) / DiffCoTune (per-system differentiable co-tuning baseline) / Phys2Real (dynamics side).
   - *Falsifier*: joint = gain-only + dynamics-only (additive) → there is no interaction and co-optimization is unnecessary.
3. **H3 — Co-optimized gains suppress the deployment oscillation spectrum stiff gains amplify.**
   - *Prediction*: a deployment-time oscillation-spectrum metric shows co-optimized gains have lower high-frequency energy than the low-sysID-error stiff gains [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]] flags, and the SR gain tracks the oscillation reduction.
   - *Test*: measure oscillation spectrum + SR for co-optimized vs stiff vs compliant gains; regress SR on high-frequency energy — with [[2507.23445|Physics-Guided-Gain-Regularization]] as the constructive precedent (its GC-DR-RNN kills the DR-RNN's 5 Hz oscillation by penalizing equivalent-gain deviation from measured hardware gains, the exact H3 mechanism on a 110:1-gearbox plant).
   - *Row*: Tune to Learn (gains-vs-sysID) / Physics-Guided Gain Regularization (sensitivity-matched gain training, oscillation-suppression precedent).
   - *Falsifier*: oscillation energy is uncorrelated with SR → oscillation is not the transfer mechanism.
4. **H4 (deciding) — Co-optimized DR-variable gains beat a runtime adaptive-gain residual.**
   - *Prediction*: making the gain a co-optimized DR variable recovers more transfer SR than a [[2505.00991|DexCtrl]]-style *runtime* adaptive-gain predictor (or a [[2602.23253|SPARR]]/[[2606.06218|TAM-Torque-Adaptation]] residual) at matched SR — preventing the mismatch in the training distribution beats predicting/patching it at deploy-time. This is the front-line falsifier now that runtime adaptive gains are established.
   - *Test*: head-to-head {co-optimized gains, no runtime adapter} vs {fixed gains + DexCtrl-style runtime gain prediction} vs {fixed gains + torque residual} on [[2603.15469|RoCo-Challenge]]'s paired Simulation + Onsite tracks (matched-SR transfer substrate AutoMate's sim-only numbers and RoboChallenge's no-sim-track eval cannot supply); report SR and adaptation/residual magnitude.
   - *Row*: DexCtrl (runtime adaptive gains) / SPARR (residual) / TAM (torque residual) / Warp RL (expressive RQ-spline-flow residual, the strongest residual variant the co-optimization must beat, not just an additive one) / RoCo Challenge (paired sim+real tracks) vs Tune to Learn (gains-vs-sysID).
   - *Falsifier*: the runtime adaptive-gain predictor (or [[2606.31043|Warp RL]]'s expressive residual) matches co-optimization → the mismatch is better adapted at runtime than prevented in training, and A3 reduces to DexCtrl.
5. **H5 — The effect is contact-specific; free-space shows a null.**
   - *Prediction*: the co-optimization advantage concentrates on contact-rich tasks (where gains shape the contact-force response) and is near-zero on free-space reaches, so the claim is scoped to contact — consistent with [[2606.22062|Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation]]'s single-DOF, contact-free pendulum sweep, where identification alone (no gain axis at all) already dominates randomization breadth.
   - *Test*: stratify by contact vs free-space; report the gain-co-optimization margin per regime.
   - *Row*: Tune to Learn (gains-vs-sysID) / SPARR (contact residual) / Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation (contact-free null precedent).
   - *Falsifier*: free-space tasks show the same margin → gains are not the contact-specific lever the bet claims.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Gain co-optimization explodes the search space** — adding $(K_p, K_d)$ to DR multiplies training cost. → Use [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]]'s finding to seed a narrow compliant/overdamped prior rather than searching the full grid.
> - **Hardware gain limits** — real controllers cap achievable gains; co-optimized values may be infeasible. → Constrain the co-optimization to the hardware's admissible gain box and validate on the real controller.
> - **Effect may be task-specific** — gains matter most for contact; free-space tasks may show little gain. → Scope the claim to contact-rich tasks ([[2407.08028|AutoMate]]/NIST) and report the free-space null result honestly (H5).

---

## Cluster B — Real-to-Sim-to-Real: Grounding the Simulator in Deployment

*Invert reality into the simulator first — recover appearance and dynamics from real data — but forward transfer is capped, not free: a simulator predicts reality forward no better than it captured reality backward, and no better than its own engine reproduces reality at the true parameters.*

### B1 — Closing the Real-to-Sim Gap: Reconstruction Fidelity as the New Bottleneck

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Real-to-Sim-to-Real |
| **Thesis** | [[2512.19562\|REALM]] is the first to name the *real-to-sim* gap as a distinct object. Once named, the arrow flips: forward sim-real correlation is capped by how faithfully reality was inverted into the simulator *and* by the simulator's own engine floor — inversion fidelity sets the ceiling, but even perfect inversion cannot lift it above the engine's own floor. Joint appearance+physics inversion is now a crowded engine, so the open science is not *whether* to invert jointly but *how reconstruction fidelity maps to forward transfer* — a law nobody has measured, and one the navigation literature shows is not even monotone. The field still treats this as solved by better engines. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] (method), [[2512.19562\|REALM]] (benchmark), [[2505.17966\|Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics]] (benchmark), [[2508.11117\|Robot-Policy-Eval-Sim2Real]] (benchmark), [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]] (method), [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] (method), [[2207.10821\|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]] (method), [[2604.26509\|3D-Generation-for-Embodied-AI-Survey]] (survey) |
| **Key targets** | A monotone fidelity→forward-r law per object class, with a *floor-adjusted* deformable Δr (rope 0.901 vs [[2511.04831\|Isaac-Lab]] 0.237) exceeding rigid Δr (push-T 0.915 vs 0.649) once [[2608.05948\|GAUGE]]'s regime-specific engine-floor error is subtracted — [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] supplies the joint-inversion substrate; the law must hold where [[2207.10821\|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]]'s navigation counter-example does not; [[2512.19562\|REALM]] r=0.92 overall / 0.88 default; [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] 75%→95% from reconstruction fidelity; operationalize the graded reconstruction-error→transfer dependent variable on [[2505.17966\|Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics]] (graded Chamfer 2 mm target / ~5 mm YCB-Video / ~1 cm Aria → ~50% grasp-transfer SR across 12 recon models, 5 robotics-reconstruction desiderata) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the field invests in *forward* realism — better engines, more domain randomization — but once you name a real-to-sim gap distinct from the forward one, the causal arrow flips: forward correlation is capped by inversion fidelity, and no forward training can lift a lossy inversion.
- **Today's answers**: [[2512.19562|REALM]] is the first benchmark to name the real-to-sim gap ("low visual fidelity and misaligned control dynamics"); [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]] proves the consequence — joint 3DGS-appearance + physics optimization hits r=0.915 on push-T (0.901 on soft-body rope) where [[2511.04831|Isaac-Lab]] manages 0.649 (0.237 on rope), and its ablation collapses correlation when *either* color alignment or physics optimization is removed.
- **The opening**: the gap closes only when *both* appearance and dynamics are inverted together (the joint-ablation result), and [[2605.26638|HyperSim]] confirms the leverage — better reconstruction moves zero-shot SR from 75% to 95% with 35 demos. But the joint-inversion *engine* is now consensus ([[2506.04120|Splatting-Physical-Scenes]] and [[2412.00259|One-Shot-Real-to-Sim]] both jointly optimize geometry+appearance+physics end-to-end; [[2512.19390|TwinAligner]] adds visual+dynamic alignment; [[2603.01151|D-REX]] adds a policy) — so the bottleneck is not building the engine but establishing the *law* that maps reconstruction fidelity to forward r, which [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]] shows is not even universally monotone (lower fidelity *helped* navigation transfer). Cluster A's [[2606.28276|SimFoundry]] adds a further r-comparison data point (r=0.911 sim-real over 7 tasks × 5 policies) — but by generating "unlimited digital cousins" at scale rather than inverting reconstruction fidelity, so it lands beside REALM/Real-to-Sim-GS's numbers without testing the same joint-inversion mechanism this cluster's law is about.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A simulator predicts reality forward no better than it captured reality backward, and no better than its own solver reproduces reality at the true parameters — $|\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}| \geq \text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}} + \text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ qualitatively (see Formal Framing). The forward map is a function of the recovered parameters; a lossy inversion is a floor no forward training lowers, and even a perfect one only lowers that floor to $\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$, not zero. [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s ablation is the proof of the $\text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}}$ term: removing color *or* physics alignment collapses the correlation, so that half of the bound is *joint*-reconstruction-bound, not appearance- or physics-alone; H4 below flags where [[2608.05948|GAUGE]]'s $\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ term can confound the deformable-vs-rigid comparison.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "invert jointly" — that mechanism is consensus ([[2506.04120|Splatting-Physical-Scenes]], [[2412.00259|One-Shot-Real-to-Sim]], [[2512.19390|TwinAligner]], [[2603.01151|D-REX]] all jointly invert geometry+appearance+physics). The live assumption is that reconstruction fidelity *monotonically* predicts forward transfer, so "reconstruct better" is the recipe. [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]] breaks it head-on — lower-fidelity simulation gave *higher* sim2real transfer in navigation — so the fidelity→r map is task-dependent and must be *measured*, not assumed, before it can bound anything.
- **The bet**: Across rigid/articulated/deformable object classes there is a **monotone, measurable fidelity→r law** — degrading reconstruction error in controlled steps predicts forward r per object class — and the joint-inversion advantage is **widest on deformables, floor-adjusted** ([[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]] 0.901 vs [[2511.04831|Isaac-Lab]] 0.237 on rope, vs 0.915 vs 0.649 push-T, net of [[2608.05948|GAUGE]]'s regime-specific engine-floor error), the regime [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]]'s navigation counter-example does *not* cover. Falsifiable: if forward r is insensitive to reconstruction error (flat law), *or* the floor-adjusted deformable Δr equals the rigid Δr, the arrow does not flip and one number generalizes.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what is inverted and how jointly* (joint appearance+physics / continuous correction / twin via alignment / generative single-view / deformable-specialized / video-to-asset / feedforward joint inversion / feedforward-init-then-refine / VLM-selected constitutive law / control-dynamics residual / ground-truth diagnostic) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | What is inverted (how jointly) | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] | 3DGS appearance + soft-body physics, *jointly* | r=0.915 push-T / 0.901 rope vs [[2511.04831\|Isaac-Lab]] 0.649 / 0.237; ablation collapses on removing either | per-scene cost; control-dynamics misalignment is a separate term it doesn't close |
| [[2512.19562\|REALM]] | [[2403.12945\|DROID]]-aligned sysID, names the real-to-sim gap | r=0.92 overall / 0.88 default; unseen objects drop most | names + measures the gap but supplies sysID alignment, not joint appearance+physics inversion |
| [[2508.11117\|Robot-Policy-Eval-Sim2Real]] | *measurement protocol*, not an inversion engine: names sim-to-real performance-*alignment* metrics as the eval, over a 4-level task taxonomy + 5 perturbations + graded-success/failure-mode metrics | the named transfer-matching protocol H1/H2 regress forward r on (rank twins by predicted transfer) and H3's perturbation/unseen-object robustness leans on | supplies the alignment-metric + perturbation protocol but reports no empirical r itself — weaker than [[2512.19562\|REALM]]'s realized r=0.92, complementary not a data point |
| [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]] | geometry + physical params + appearance from video | generalizes to unseen interactions | the inversion *engine* [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] builds on — not itself a forward-correlation benchmark |

| [[2504.03597\|Real-is-Sim]] | dynamic twin ([[2406.10788\|Embodied-Gaussians]]) corrected at 60 Hz by real RGB | 57%→80%, 82% best PushT | continuous *correction*, not an offline joint inversion — the runtime-grounding alternative |
| [[2512.16881\|PolaRiS]] | interactive sim from wrist-camera real-video scans | r=0.9 avg / 0.98 best vs RoboArena, <20 min/scene | reconstruction → forward correlation at scale, but appearance-led; physics inversion lighter |
| [[2604.15805\|WorldComposer]] | generative real-to-sim from one panorama → 3DGS + collision mesh | r=0.91 sim-real SR | generative single-view inversion — the cheap route to r>0.9, fidelity bounded by the generator |
| [[2604.08544\|SIM1]] | deformable data engine, sub-mm geometric + AVBD-stable dynamic alignment | 76% zero-shot from de-novo init where real-data baselines hit 0% | joint inversion *where the gap is widest* (deformables) — but a data engine, not a correlation benchmark |
| [[2512.14696\|CRISP]] | real-to-sim from monocular human-scene video (planar fit + contact completion) | 93.1% real-to-sim vs VideoMimic 44.8% at 23K FPS | the video-to-asset inversion route; scene-level, not object-physics joint inversion |
| [[2404.09833\|Video2Game]] | single-video → NeRF + mesh + physics twin | 100+ FPS browser | appearance-heavy, lighter physics — the unbalanced-inversion contrast |

| [[2412.00259\|One-Shot-Real-to-Sim]] | geometry + appearance + physics, *jointly* via differentiable sim+rendering, one shot | one-shot end-to-end differentiable inversion | the joint-inversion mechanism B1's bet rests on — but no fidelity→r law, no per-object-class sweep |

| [[2502.08645\|Re3Sim]] | hybrid real-to-sim: foreground ray-traced + background 3DGS, geometry+appearance recovered together | Pearson r=0.924 sim↔real, >58% avg zero-shot real SR | the appearance-led correlation number [[2512.16881\|PolaRiS]] and [[2604.15805\|WorldComposer]] also report (r≈0.91–0.9), but physics inversion is lighter than [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s joint soft-body term — no per-object-class fidelity→r sweep |

III chained: Type 0.000 / Angle 0.849° / Pos 0.028m vs AA 22.545°/1.367m, ScrewSplat 62.820°/0.636m); 7 min 23 s total, 42 s active human interaction | recovers joint *kinematics* alongside appearance+geometry — the articulation axis every other B1 row leaves implicit or fixed (a hinge or drawer is inverted as a rigid or fixed mesh) — but from one static configuration, no dynamics, so it supplies the URDF/kinematic layer B1's soft-body/rigid rows don't touch, not a physics-parameter inversion |
| [[2608.06164\|BendTwin]] | *deformable* physics parameters, jointly with geometry — augments [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]]'s axial-only spring-mass with explicit bending stiffness + viscous bending damping over local node triplets | 19.0% Chamfer reduction (reconstruction) / 16.8% (future prediction) over PhysTwin; 26.5%/18.3% at a 0.05 sparse-graph sampling ratio; surface-only nodes match/beat PhysTwin's interior-node axial model | a direct mechanical-fidelity upgrade to the B1/B3 PhysTwin baseline itself, not a new inversion pipeline — explains *why* axial-only spring-mass under-constrains sparse physical graphs and sharpens the deformable Δr the H4 sub-hypothesis targets, but still parameter-only (spring stiffness, not a learned law) and reports no forward sim-real correlation r |
| [[2608.05948\|GAUGE]] | *neither appearance nor dynamics reconstructed from video* — a ground-truth instrument: 22 real task families with millimeter motion-capture + independently calibrated physical properties (friction, Young's modulus), scored against physics engines given the *correct* parameters and against video world models | Isaac Sim/Genesis/Newton show errors an order of magnitude above real-world baselines on dynamic contact (Newton's cradle) and volumetric deformation, even with GT-calibrated parameters; world models hit R²=0.99 fitting a pendulum's oscillation form while inferring a period 73–93% too long | isolates the floor term the Formal Framing's bound now makes explicit: *even perfectly measured* $(\phi^{\text{GT}},\psi^{\text{GT}})$ may not forward-simulate correctly, because the engine/generator itself has domain-dependent solver error — sharpens the Formal Framing's inversion-bound (H1's within-object slope) without itself running a reconstruction-from-video → forward-r sweep |
| [[2303.05512\|PAC-NeRF]] | geometry + physics, *jointly*, the actual root of the joint-inversion line — differentiable MPM + NeRF recovers *both* without assuming known geometry (distinct from the per-object [[2104.02646\|gradSim]] root, which fits physics given known geometry) | best in 14/17 physical-property categories across 9 synthetic material scenarios (elastic/plastic/granular/fluid), beating privileged geometry-known baselines; applied to real multi-view video | the NeurIPS'23 root every joint-inversion engine below descends from — [[2506.06440\|Vid2Sim]] benchmarks against it head-to-head (PSNR 30.17 vs 22.06); [[2604.07882\|ReconPhys]] positions against it in its landscape prose but tables Spring-Gaus/4DGS instead — voxel-NeRF appearance (not 3DGS), multi-view-only, no forward sim-real correlation r |
| [[2604.07882\|ReconPhys]] | geometry + appearance + physics, *jointly*, but **feedforward** (not per-scene) — a frozen 3DGS predictor + a physical-attribute network bound by a spring-mass system, trained self-supervised via a differentiable sim-render loop | 8.37 dB future-prediction PSNR gain over Spring-Gaus (21.64 vs 13.27); Chamfer 0.001 vs 4DGS's 0.593; **<1 s** inference vs **>1 hr** for per-scene baselines | the *feedforward* instance of every per-object B1 row ([[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]]/[[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]]/[[2506.04120\|Splatting-Physical-Scenes]]/[[2603.01151\|D-REX]] all optimize per scene) — but all numbers are on a synthetic held-out set of 46 objects (Objaverse-XL → TRELLIS → simulated free-fall); real-video results are qualitative only, no forward robot-SR or sim-real correlation r |
| [[2506.06440\|Vid2Sim]] | geometry + appearance + physics, *jointly*, via a **feedforward initial estimate** (VideoMAE + LGM) refined by a **light per-scene** mesh-free LBS simulation (a Neural Jacobian module accelerates the differentiable refinement) | PSNR 30.17 vs [[2303.05512\|PAC-NeRF]]'s 22.06; refinement in ~15 min vs 54–120 min for PAC-NeRF/Spring-Gaus/GIC; future-state PSNR 25.07 vs 20.11 | the *hybrid* feedforward-init-then-refine pattern an amortized-inference pipeline would fall back to when its estimate is low-confidence — sits between ReconPhys's fully-feedforward and the fully-per-object rows, but reduced-order LBS not full 3DGS-physics, no robot-SR endpoint |
| [[2510.21447\|PhysWorld-Deformable]] | geometry + physics, jointly, via a digital twin built with **VLM-assisted constitutive-*model* selection** (Qwen3 picks among candidate laws — Neo-Hookean, Anisotropic Hyperelastic — rather than fitting one preset) + global-to-local property optimization, then distilled to a GNN surrogate via synthetic motion+property augmentation | **799 FPS**, **47×** faster than [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]] (17 FPS); CD 0.010 vs PhysTwin 0.012; MPPI-based manipulation planning on the distilled surrogate | a third option beyond B3's fix-a-law-and-fit-params vs. learn-the-form-from-scratch split — *select* the law via VLM, then fit+distill it (cross-referenced into B3's H1 below); still single-object, no forward robot-SR correlation |
| [[2504.13159\|Digital-Twin-Survey]] | survey, organized directly on B1's own axis — geometry / appearance / dynamics / physics / semantics capture, with 3DGS named as the unifying representation | names interoperability across representations, unified physically-consistent rendering, and robust semantic generalization as the open problems | a second survey anchor beside [[2604.26509\|3D-Generation-for-Embodied-AI-Survey]], closer to B1's own "digital twin" vocabulary — framing only, no empirical results |
| [[2502.01143\|ASAP]] | *not* appearance or geometry — corrects the **control/dynamics discrepancy** directly via a learned "delta action model" residual on a pre-trained humanoid whole-body motion-tracking policy | beats SysID and Delta-Dynamics baselines head-to-head on open-loop dynamics matching; real Unitree G1, tracking error 61.2→50.2 mm (kick) / 159→112 mm ("Silencer") | a second H5 control-alignment datapoint beside [[2606.30268\|ConCent]] (cross-referenced into H5 below) — but corrects the *action/dynamics* term directly rather than the *rendered* twin like [[2504.03597\|Real-is-Sim]]'s 60 Hz RGB correction, and in a different embodiment (humanoid whole-body vs. tabletop manipulation) |
| [[2601.21282\|WorldBench]] + [[2510.02311\|IDPP]] (its **PhysVid** dataset) | *neither* recovers geometry/physics from video — two diagnostics testing whether generative world models / video foundation models recover the *parameters*, not just plausible-looking motion | WorldBench: models fit visually-plausible parabolic trajectories yet infer wrong gravitational constants, worst on viscosity/extreme-friction; PhysVid: oracle/classical-CV baselines (ROC-AUC 0.99–1.00 synthetic) beat pretrained video/MLLM models (DynamiCrafter/V-JEPA-2/GPT-4o) on real elasticity/viscosity/friction | complements [[2608.05948\|GAUGE]]'s engine-given-correct-parameters failure mode with the upstream one — video→parameter recovery itself fails before the engine ever runs, sharpening the same inversion-bound-isn't-free point from the recovery side rather than the simulation side |
| [[2607.06699\|RoboSnap]] | geometry + appearance, *jointly*, one-shot from a **single RGB image** (VLM+SAM3/SAM3D object layer + generative-world-model Gaussian background, SDF-physics pose refinement) | r=0.887 sim-real correlation, MMRV 0.0066; **100%** DROID-trajectory replay without interpenetration vs **40%** for RoLA; +13.4 pp real policy SR (π0 29.3%→42.7%) | confirmatory, not novel — the single-image real2sim sub-axis is already dense ([[2604.15805\|WorldComposer]] r=0.91, [[2512.16881\|PolaRiS]] r=0.9 avg/0.98 best, [[2502.08645\|Re3Sim]] r=0.924, [[2606.28276\|SimFoundry]] r=0.911); adds a stability metric (73% fewer falling objects than RoLA) but no new mechanism |
| [[2601.08454\|Real2Sim-Active-Perception]] | *not* appearance or scene geometry — a VLM decomposes a task into the *minimal missing physical parameters* (mass, height, friction) and synthesizes a reactive Behavior Tree that only probes those, on a torque-controlled Franka | mass 0.257±0.022 kg vs GT 0.254; height 0.7642±0.00004 m vs GT 0.765; 47% fewer actions than exhaustive probing (12 vs 29, 33 s vs 62 s) | a distinct *how much to invert* cell every other B1 row skips — task-conditioned, *active*, parameter-minimal identification rather than passive full-scene capture; complements [[2606.22062\|Sim2Real-Budget-Allocation]]-style identification-vs-randomization sweeps (A3/Survey Landscape) but at the perception-planning level, not the training-objective level |
| [[2602.11575\|ReaDy-Go]] | geometry + appearance, jointly, *plus a dynamic actor* — static-scene 3DGS (PGSR) unified with animatable human Gaussian avatars driven by motion-diffusion priors along planned trajectories | real dynamic-nav success 90%/70%/80% (Outside/Lobby/Library) vs Vid2Sim 60/40/60 and ViNT 30/20/40, at 2M vs ViNT's 30M params | every other B1 row inverts a *static* scene — ReaDy-Go is the first to reconstruct a *moving, animated* actor into the twin, an empty cell the fidelity→r law (H1/H2) has never been tested against; reports navigation SR, not a fidelity→correlation regression, so whether the same law holds for dynamic-actor inversion is untested |
| [[2602.12633\|Real2Sim-Cluttered]] | geometry + *dynamics*, jointly, for multi-object *clutter* — a contact graph of support/proximity edges gates a two-stage SDF-then-hierarchical-differentiable-rigid-body optimization that refines pose, mass, friction, and CoM together | stability ratio 85.7% (GSO) / 89.3% (YCB) vs ~51%/49% for SAM3D+ICP/HoloScene; real decluttering 70–80% vs 0% | extends [[2602.08058\|Picasso]]/[[2602.20150\|SPARCS]]'s cluttered-scene geometry+stability inversion with the *dynamics* parameters (mass/friction/CoM) those two leave un-recovered — but scores stability/declutter-SR, not a forward sim-real correlation r, so it still doesn't touch H1/H2's fidelity→r law |
| [[2608.06827\|R2S-EGO]] | geometry + appearance, jointly, from **sparse capture along robot-executable views** — a simulator-derived *robot proxy* (rolling out a controller to find behavior-admissible camera queries) pairs with a capture-anchored *geometry proxy* to guide generative view-filling, assimilated as lower-weight pseudo-observations | 19.062 dB PSNR from 6 captures vs GaussGym's 14.226 dB; real-G1 sitting 82.5%±6.8% (33/40) vs GaussGym's 10.0%±10.5% (4/40) despite comparable in-sim SR (97.6% vs 87.2%) | a genuinely distinct *what to capture* cell — every other B1 row assumes dense capture or accepts whatever sparse capture gives; R2S-EGO's robot-proxy view-selection is active-reconstruction on the *capture* side (the geometric-input cousin of [[2601.08454\|Real2Sim-Active-Perception]]'s active parameter-probing), and its 82.5%-vs-10% real-SR gap under matched sim-SR is the sharpest evidence yet that capture-view *choice*, not just reconstruction fidelity, gates forward transfer — but a single-humanoid-task result, no fidelity→r regression |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (joint appearance+physics inversion is the forward-correlation ceiling), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (headline causal test — within-object, not cross-object) — Degrading *the same object's* reconstruction in controlled steps causally lowers its forward r.**
   - *Prediction*: the load-bearing claim is *causal*, so the test must hold object identity fixed and intervene only on fidelity: for a *single* object, degrading its reconstruction error in controlled steps (the only thing varied) *monotonically* lowers that object's forward r — the within-object causal slope. A cross-object regression of r on reconstruction error (the easy observational version) conflates fidelity with per-object difficulty and measures correlation, not the slope the arrow-flip claim needs; the headline is the within-object intervention. The corollary is that [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]]'s lower-fidelity-helps result is navigation-specific and the within-object slope is negative on contact-rich manipulation, where the joint-inversion engines ([[2506.04120|Splatting-Physical-Scenes]], [[2412.00259|One-Shot-Real-to-Sim]]) operate.
   - *Test*: within-object controlled-degradation sweep — fix the object (pose clamped to ground truth so pose error does not re-confound the slope), step its reconstruction error (Chamfer 2 mm/5 mm/1 cm) via [[2505.17966|Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics]]'s graded reconstruction-error → grasp-transfer-SR substrate (~50% SR over 12 recon models on YCB-Video + Aria, DV is grasp-transfer SR not the full forward-r), regress forward r on the *within-object* error step, and only then aggregate the per-object slopes across the cross-object panel; compare the within-object slope sign to the navigation result.
   - *Row*: Real-to-Sim GS (joint) / Single-View Mesh for Robotics (graded reconstruction-error → grasp-transfer SR) / Robot Policy Eval (Sim2Real) (the named sim-to-real transfer-matching metric to regress forward r on) vs Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real (non-monotone navigation).
   - *Falsifier*: the *within-object* forward r is flat or *rises* as that object's fidelity drops → fidelity is not a within-object cause of transfer, the cross-object correlation was confounded by difficulty, and the arrow does not flip.
2. **H2 — The monotone law is an offline twin-ranker (predicts forward r per object class).**
   - *Prediction*: the fidelity→r regression from H1 generalizes across rigid/articulated/deformable well enough to *rank* twins by predicted forward transfer before any forward rollout — so $\text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}}$ predicts forward transferability offline.
   - *Test*: fit the law on [[2505.17966|Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics]]'s reconstruction-quality→grasp-transfer data (Chamfer/stability/occlusion vs ~50% SR across 12 models) as a held-out object subset; predict forward r on new objects and measure rank correlation against the realized forward r.
   - *Row*: Real-to-Sim GS (joint) / Single-View Mesh for Robotics (reconstruction-quality→transfer fitting substrate) / SIM1 (deformable).
   - *Falsifier*: predicted forward r does not rank twins → the law is descriptive, not predictive, and offline twin selection fails.
3. **H3 — Improving unseen-object inversion beats forward DR for novel objects.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2512.19562|REALM]] shows unseen objects drop most; generative-prior reconstruction ([[2604.15805|WorldComposer]]-style) closes that drop *more* than forward domain randomization does, because the drop is an inversion failure, not a forward-coverage failure.
   - *Test*: on unseen objects, compare {better inversion} vs {more forward DR} at matched compute; report the recovered correlation, scoring robustness over [[2508.11117|Robot-Policy-Eval-Sim2Real]]'s 5 controlled perturbations + unseen-object track with its graded-success metrics (beyond binary SR).
   - *Row*: REALM (unseen-object drop) / Robot Policy Eval (Sim2Real) (perturbation + unseen-object robustness protocol) vs WorldComposer (generative inversion).
   - *Falsifier*: forward DR closes the unseen-object drop as well → the drop is a coverage problem, not an inversion one.
4. **H4 — The widest inversion advantage is on deformables, not rigid.**
   - *Prediction*: the joint-inversion advantage over [[2511.04831|Isaac-Lab]] is larger on soft-body rope (0.901 vs 0.237) than rigid push-T (0.915 vs 0.649), because forward engines model deformables worst — so the r>0.9 bet must be reported per object-class.
   - *Test*: report the inversion advantage (Δr vs Isaac Lab) stratified by rigid / articulated / deformable.
   - *Row*: Real-to-Sim GS (joint) / SIM1 (deformable).
   - *Falsifier*: rigid and deformable show equal Δr → the deformable-specific advantage is illusory and one number generalizes.
   - *Confound flagged by [[2608.05948|GAUGE]]*: volumetric-deformable is exactly the regime where GAUGE measures engine forward error an order of magnitude above baseline *even given ground-truth-calibrated* physical parameters — so a wide deformable Δr could reflect Isaac-Lab's engine floor being worse in that regime, not [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s inversion being better in it. Subtract (or report separately) the GAUGE-measured engine-floor error per regime before attributing H4's deformable Δr to inversion quality. **Second-order confound**: GAUGE's own foam/rubber Young's-modulus reference is calibrated *quasi-statically* (compression tests + DIC, fitting the small-deformation linear region) but scored against *high-acceleration* deformation tasks (twisting, flinging, rapid textile motion) — a rate-regime mismatch, since viscoelastic materials have rate-dependent stiffness (dynamic modulus exceeds static modulus). So GAUGE's reported floor is itself partly inflated by a mismeasured reference, not pure solver error; subtracting it as-is may over-correct H4's deformable Δr if [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s reconstruction also under-estimates dynamic stiffness, or under-correct if it doesn't. H6 tests this directly.
5. **H5 (commit gate) — Control-dynamics misalignment is a residual term inversion alone leaves open, and whether the control term recovers it is the go/no-go for the direction.**
   - *Prediction*: even at r>0.9 from joint appearance+physics inversion, [[2512.19562|REALM]]'s control-dynamics misalignment caps the remaining correlation — co-optimizing control alignment (links to A3) recovers the residual that appearance+physics inversion cannot. This is the *commit gate*: if the within-object causal slope (H1) holds *and* the control term closes a measurable residual past joint inversion, the "reconstruction fidelity is the new bottleneck" thesis commits; if either fails, the direction de-risks to the observational law and does not over-claim a causal ceiling.
   - *Test*: measure forward correlation with appearance+physics inversion alone vs + control-alignment; report the residual closed by the control term, with a pre-registered MDE so a "non-zero residual recovered" claim is powered.
   - *Row*: REALM (real-to-sim gap) / Real-to-Sim GS (joint) / ConCent (contact-geometry alignment as a control-dynamics-term instance, recovers contact-rich SR 20%→80% by inverting local contact dynamics a joint appearance+physics fit leaves coarse) / ASAP (a second control-alignment datapoint — a learned delta-action-model residual closes the humanoid whole-body dynamics gap, beating SysID and Delta-Dynamics head-to-head, in a different embodiment than ConCent's tabletop contact geometry).
   - *Falsifier*: control alignment adds nothing past joint inversion → appearance+physics inversion fully closes the gap, the control term is redundant, and the commit gate fails on this leg.
6. **H6 — GAUGE's reported engine floor is partly a rate-regime artifact, not pure solver error.**
   - *Prediction*: independently re-measuring GAUGE's foam/rubber/textile assets' Young's modulus and stiffness at *dynamic* strain rates — matched to the loading rate of GAUGE's own high-acceleration test tasks (twisting, bending, flinging, rapid textile motion) — rather than reusing the quasi-static compression-test/DIC values it currently reports, shrinks the reported order-of-magnitude engine-floor error on volumetric-deformable and rapid-textile tasks by a measurable fraction. Part of what GAUGE currently attributes to solver error is a rate-regime mismatch in its own reference measurement, not the solver.
