   - *Test*: re-run GAUGE's compression/DIC calibration protocol at strain rates matched to the deformation rate actually observed in the high-acceleration tasks (rather than the "initial linear region... small-deformation regimes" GAUGE's Method section reports), re-score the same physics engines (Isaac Sim, Genesis, Newton) against the dynamically-recalibrated $(\phi^{\text{GT}}, \psi^{\text{GT}})$, and compare the resulting $\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ to GAUGE's originally-reported floor on the same tasks.
   - *Row*: GAUGE (ground-truth diagnostic — the recalibration target) / Real-to-Sim GS (joint) (the H4 comparison the corrected floor feeds back into).
   - *Falsifier*: the dynamically-recalibrated floor does not change appreciably from GAUGE's quasi-static-derived floor → the floor really is solver-only, rate regime is not a confound, and the original subtraction recipe (H4 and the Risks below) was fine as reported.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Reconstruction is per-scene expensive** — joint 3DGS + physics fitting per object/scene may not scale to open worlds. → Amortize with generative reconstruction priors ([[2604.26509|3D-Generation-for-Embodied-AI-Survey]], [[2604.15805|WorldComposer]]) and reuse twins across tasks.
> - **The gap to [[2511.04831|Isaac-Lab]] is widest on deformables** — the r=0.915 headline is rigid push-T, but the largest advantage is soft-body rope (0.901 vs 0.237). → Scope the >0.9 bet across rigid and deformable, reporting the per-object-class delta separately (H4) rather than assuming one number generalizes.
> - **Inversion fidelity may not be the *only* bound** — control-dynamics misalignment ([[2512.19562|REALM]]) is a separate term. → Co-optimize control alignment (links to A3, H5) alongside appearance+physics rather than assuming reconstruction alone closes the gap.
> - **$\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ is nonzero** — per the Formal Framing's bound ($|\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}| \geq \text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}} + \text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ qualitatively, [[2608.05948|GAUGE]]), even perfectly measured $(\phi^{\text{GT}},\psi^{\text{GT}})$ forward-simulates wrong. → Treat H1's within-object slope, and H4's deformable Δr, as floor-adjusted: subtract the engine's own GAUGE-measured regime-specific error before attributing the residual to inversion quality; report which regime (rigid contact vs. deformable) the tested object falls into.
> - **The reported floor number is not itself calibration-protocol-orthogonal** — GAUGE's foam/rubber/textile assets are calibrated quasi-statically but scored under high-acceleration deformation, so part of the "engine floor" GAUGE reports may be rate-regime mismatch in its own reference measurement, not solver error. → Subtracting GAUGE's number as-is may over- or under-correct H1/H4 depending on whether the reconstruction method under test shares the same rate-regime blind spot; report both the raw and floor-adjusted Δr until H6 measures the dynamically-recalibrated floor directly.
> - **Amortizing the per-object parameter-recovery step is an engineering choice, not an open research question** — if the pipeline needs a cheap observation→parameter map instead of a per-scene gradient fit, that map is already solved: [[1702.02453|UP-OSI]] (2017) trains a feedforward net that predicts dynamics parameters from a short interaction history in one forward pass and generalizes to parameters 100% outside its training range, and [[2410.20357|Dynamics-as-Prompts]], [[2406.12769|Latent-Intuitive-Physics]], [[2603.23973|SLAT-Phys]], [[2508.17437|Pixie]], and [[2502.18615|Distributional-Treatment-Real2Sim2Real-Object-Centric]] extend the same amortized-inference mechanism to explicit constitutive vectors, in-context multi-episode histories, and likelihood-free posteriors. → Reuse one of these nets for a fast parameter-recovery step rather than re-deriving amortization as a research bet; what remains is swapping in a robotics-specific (diff-sim-rollout) supervision source and validating clutter robustness — a scaling/engineering exercise, not a new mechanism.

### B2 — Bidirectional Sim↔Real Co-Training: The Twin as a Data Engine, Not a Sandbox

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Real-to-Sim-to-Real |
| **Thesis** | A reconstructed twin generates training-valid data, and closing a real→sim→real loop that folds deployment data back beats one-shot training — both already shown at the lifecycle scale. What no one has measured on a *per-task object-grounded* twin is whether folding back keeps improving *monotonically across rounds* rather than drifting, and how the per-round gain scales with reconstruction fidelity. The first principle: an ungated fold-back loop can amplify reconstruction error, so monotone improvement requires a fidelity gate. The field assumes the closed loop is unconditionally self-improving. **A sharper counter-signal has since appeared**: [[2607.29172\|CLIFT]] gets genuine multi-round monotone improvement on a real humanoid (93→100%, 70→98%, 53→96% over 2 flywheel cycles) by *discarding the simulator entirely* — deploy, reward-score, refit, redeploy, no asset re-grounding at all. That reopens the thesis's own premise: is twin re-grounding the mechanism that makes fold-back work, or is it deployment-scored refitting *of any kind*, with the twin only load-bearing for round 0? The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2403.03949\|RialTo]] (method), [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]] (method), [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] (method), [[2504.03597\|Real-is-Sim]] (method), [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] (survey), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2506.18088\|RoboTwin-2.0]] (benchmark), [[2412.01770\|CASHER]] (method), [[2607.29172\|CLIFT]] (method, twin-free counter-example), [[2605.21458\|Fisher-SEP]] (theory) |
| **Key targets** | A fidelity-gated per-task fold-back loop that is **monotone across rounds** where an ungated loop drifts; the reconstruction-fidelity *exchange rate* ([[2504.03597\|Real-is-Sim]] 30 sim ≈ 30 real, 57%→80%) rising with twin fidelity (link to B1); reproduce [[2403.03949\|RialTo]]'s 90% (target twin) vs 10% (generic) grounding advantage and [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] 75%→95% on a per-task object twin, beating [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]]'s single feedback-on/off lifecycle delta (LIBERO 88.5 vs 86.9) over successive rounds, with the unseen-object generalization-SR target anchored to [[2506.18088\|RoboTwin-2.0]] (50-task / 731-object / 147-category object-twin generation, +24.4% real few-shot / +21% zero-shot on unseen backgrounds); [[2412.01770\|CASHER]] +55% from unsupervised deployment-scan fold-back where SR ≤20% (the in-KH deployment-grounded fold-back gain B2's per-round loop must beat), with R²=0.92 sim-real correlation and 62% zero-shot on 8 unseen kitchens |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: most of the corpus uses twins as sandboxes — places to evaluate policies cheaply ([[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]], [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]] are evaluation frameworks) — which discards the twin's generative capacity entirely.
- **Today's answers**: the highest-leverage results use twins as *data engines* — [[2403.03949|RialTo]] reconstructs a twin from real data, runs RL inside it, distills back (91% pose-rand real), and its decisive ablation shows a policy trained on the *target-specific* twin hits 90% on the real drawer while a generic-asset one manages 10%; [[2605.26638|HyperSim]]'s co-training (abundant synthetic + 35 real demos) lifts 75% to 95%; [[2506.18088|RoboTwin-2.0]] gets +24.4% real few-shot. Cluster A's [[2606.28276|SimFoundry]] belongs on this list too — its "unlimited digital cousins" framing is the at-scale automation of [[2604.15805|WorldComposer]]'s single-panorama data-engine row below (r=0.911 sim-real over 7 tasks × 5 policies), generated once per scene rather than folded back from deployment, so it sharpens the one-shot-vs-closed-loop distinction this direction turns on without itself closing the loop.
- **The opening**: the surviving question is whether per-round fold-back improves *monotonically* on a per-task object-grounded twin over *successive* re-grounding rounds. The closed loop is now *claimed* — [[2512.00076|Arcadia]] re-grounds assets + policy from deployment feedback and states B2's H1 falsifier verbatim ("removing any stage reverts to one-shot training"), feedback-on > feedback-off (LIBERO 88.5 vs 86.9, BridgeData V2 52.4 vs 47.3) — but only on a *single* scene/lifecycle pass, leaving the per-round monotone-improvement question untouched. The reachable lever is the exchange rate: [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]] measures 30 sim demos ≈ 30 real on PushT (57%→80%), so a grounded twin's data value *should* rise with twin fidelity (link to B1) — the per-round law [[2605.00080|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] frames as world-models-as-"data amplification."

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A twin grounded in real reconstruction generates data from the *correct* distribution $p_{\text{sim}}(\cdot \mid \phi^\star, \psi^\star) \approx p_{\text{real}}$ — so its samples are training-valid, not just test-valid. Evaluation discards the twin's generative capacity; data generation uses it. [[2403.03949|RialTo]]'s 90%-vs-10% (target-twin vs generic) is the proof that only a *grounded* twin produces target-distribution training data.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "the closed loop beats one-shot" — [[2512.00076|Arcadia]] already claims and (qualitatively) shows that. The live assumption is that fold-back is *unconditionally* self-improving — that you can re-ground every round without a gate. Because each fold re-reconstructs from imperfect deployment data, an ungated loop can lower sim-real fidelity round-over-round and drift; [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]]'s continuous 60 Hz *correction* exists precisely because uncorrected twin error compounds. The conditional version — fold back *only when a B1 fidelity check passes* — is what no one has tested. [[2605.21458|Fisher-SEP]] supplies the formal reason a gate can't be the whole story even when it's well-posed: any *passive* fold-back loop's error splits into a local component (states the deployed policy visits, closed by more rounds) and a reachability component (states it doesn't visit, **provably bounded away from zero at any horizon** under passive learning alone, its Theorem 6) — so which part of the sim-real gap a given fold-back round can even address depends on whether the round explores beyond the current policy's support, not just on whether the twin it re-grounds from is high-fidelity.
- **The bet**: A per-task object-grounded fold-back loop **gated on a B1 reconstruction-fidelity check** improves *monotonically across N rounds* on unseen-object generalization, where the same loop *ungated* drifts (a round-over-round SR trajectory that diverges) — and the per-round gain *scales with the reconstruction-fidelity exchange rate* ([[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]] 30 sim ≈ 30 real rising with twin fidelity). Reproduce [[2403.03949|RialTo]]'s 90%-vs-10% grounding advantage and [[2605.26638|HyperSim]] 75%→95% per round. Falsifiable: if the *ungated* loop never drifts (gate redundant), or the exchange rate is flat in fidelity, the conditional-fold-back mechanism buys nothing over [[2512.00076|Arcadia]]'s one-pass result.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the twin is used* (data engine / co-training source / continuous-correction sandbox / generated-video twin / planning sandbox / distribution-alignment) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How the twin is used | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2403.03949\|RialTo]] | data engine: RL in twin + inverse distillation back | 91% pose-rand real; 90% (target twin) vs 10% (generic) | open-loop (no deployment-data fold-back) — the closed loop B2 proposes |
| [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] | co-training source: synthetic + 35 real demos | 75%→95%, +35% first-attempt from adversarial twin data | one-shot co-train, not a closed loop folding deployment data back |
| [[2504.03597\|Real-is-Sim]] | continuous-correction sandbox; data-substitution evidence | 30 sim ≈ 30 real (57%→80%) | used as evaluator/corrector; the exchange-rate evidence the loop scales |
| [[2506.18088\|RoboTwin-2.0]] | twin data generator + 5-axis DR | +24.4% real few-shot, +21% zero-shot, 71.3% auto-codegen | twin data as augmentation, not a deployment-grounded closed loop |
| [[2511.07416\|PhysWorld]] | twin built from *generated* video → residual RL | 82% real | twin grounded in generated, not real, video — the synthesis-side variant |
| [[2509.24948\|RehearseVLA]] | world-model post-training + VLM reflector | 79.6% LIBERO from 5 demos, real 20%→30% | world-model-as-data-engine for VLA — learned model, not a reconstructed twin |
| [[2601.02078\|Genie-Sim-3.0]] | LLM-driven sim + 3DGS-reconstruction *platform* as a data engine | 1,500 synthetic episodes beat real-data baselines zero-shot; R²=0.94 sim-real | synthetic-beats-real proof at platform scale (humanoid), but scenes are LLM-composed, not deployment-grounded per task — no real-fold-back loop |
| [[2606.12604\|EgoEngine]] | object-centric twin from *egocentric human video* as a data engine | synthetic-only training beats teleop (60% vs 25% Hammer); 83% TACO / 90% Aria | turns free human video into target-distribution training data, but open-loop per video — no deployment-grounded fold-back |
| [[2606.08828\|Video2Sim2Real]] | single-human-video twin → *decoupled* IL (geometry) + residual-RL (physics) data | 95.7% real avg over 7 tasks vs pure IL/RL 3–45.7% | full-stack single-video data engine, but per-video and open-loop — the closed re-grounding loop B2 proposes |
| [[2604.08544\|SIM1]] | grounded deformable data engine | synthetic-only +50%/+13%/+47% over real baselines at 27× lower cost | data-engine for deformables; no closed real-fold-back loop |
| [[2604.11386\|ComSim]] | DiT neural sim turns classical-sim videos into "pseudo-real" data | 10 real + 200 pseudo-real → 28/30 vs 17/30 from 20 real | classical+neural one engine, but not deployment-grounded co-training |
| [[2604.15805\|WorldComposer]] | "Digital Cousins" from one panorama for augmentation | 50 real + 1,000 sim → 85% real SR (r=0.91) | generative-cousins augmentation, not a single grounded closed loop |
| [[2509.18631\|Sim-Real-OT-Co-Training]] | distribution-alignment co-train (unbalanced optimal transport) | 0.73 image / 0.77 point-cloud real SR, unseen-texture | aligns the sim:real distribution but no twin-grounding or fold-back |
| [[2604.13645\|CFG-ADDA]] | co-training-*mechanism* analysis (generic MuJoCo/robosuite sim, not a reconstructed twin): explains *why* sim+real co-training works and sets the optimal mixing-ratio range | structured representation alignment ~50% of loss variance (primary) + importance reweighting ~20%; SR peaks within mixing ratio (0.016, 0.3) and degrades outside both ends; ~74% (21/30) real SR | turns H4's hand-waved balance claim into a mechanism-backed ratio sweep with an empirical optimum — but uses generic sim + camera calibration, *not* a grounded twin, so it supplies the co-training-ratio backbone (H4/H2), not a twin-data-engine, and replaces no twin-grounding row |
| [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]] | *closed lifecycle loop*: deployment feedback re-grounds scene assets + a shared VLN+VLA backbone | feedback-on > off (LIBERO 88.5 vs 86.9, BridgeData V2 52.4 vs 47.3) | the topThreat — states "removing any stage reverts to one-shot" but only a *single* feedback pass at *scene/lifecycle* level; no per-task object twin, no successive-round trajectory, no fidelity gate |
| [[2503.10118\|RSR-Loop]] | closed loop tuning *sim parameters* (differentiable sim) per round | generalizable policy transfer via real-sim-real param tuning | re-grounds *parameters* not assets+policy, and no per-round monotone-improvement or fidelity-gate analysis |
| [[2510.20813\|GSWorld]] | reconstructed twin data engine + DAgger policy-only loop (*fixed* twin) | closed-loop photo-realistic sim suite, policy-only DAgger | the twin is fixed (un-re-grounded) — isolates the policy half of the loop, not asset re-grounding or drift |
| [[2602.12628\|RL-Co]] | *single-pass* RL-based sim-real co-training for VLA | RL co-train beats imitation-only sim-real co-train | one SFT-RL pass, not an iterated re-grounding loop — the one-shot baseline B2's rounds must beat |
| [[2412.01770\|CASHER]] | *closed real-to-sim-to-real data engine*: crowdsourced digital twins + a continually-improving generalist policy generates demos for new twins, and unsupervised scanned-*deployment* fine-tuning folds deployment data back | 62% zero-shot on 8 unseen real kitchens (R²=0.92 sim-real); +55% from unsupervised deployment-scan fold-back where SR was ≤20%; super-linear data scaling | the in-KH instance of B2's deployment-grounded fold-back — but the fold-back is shown as a single unsupervised pass at *scene* scale, not a per-task object twin run over *successive* rounds with a fidelity gate (the per-round monotone-vs-drift question), so like [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]] it concedes H1's direction without the trajectory |
| [[2502.08645\|Re3Sim]] | data engine: per-object real-to-sim-to-real reconstruction (3DGS background + ray-traced mesh foreground) generating sim training demos | >58% real zero-shot avg; sim-real Pearson r=0.924; reconstruction ~51.5s background / 60.5s per object | a pure grounded-twin data generator with a *measured* sim-real correlation — but open-loop per object: no deployment fold-back ([[2412.01770\|CASHER]], [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]]), so it supplies the H2 exchange-rate / H3 grounding substrate without closing the loop |
| [[2502.08643\|IKER]] | closed real-to-sim-to-real loop with *VLM-keypoint-reward* re-grounding: real→sim via BundleSDF mesh + FoundationPose, RL in sim, iterative VLM-reward replanning + deployment-disturbance recovery | 0.7 'Shoe Place' SR (vs 0.3 pose-based); spontaneous recovery from human disturbances + on-the-fly re-planning | a distinct fold-back mechanism — re-grounds the *reward* via VLM keypoints rather than [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]]'s deployment-feedback assets or [[2503.10118\|RSR-Loop]]'s differentiable params; but per-task and reward-side only, no asset re-reconstruction across rounds and no monotone-improvement / fidelity-gate analysis |
| [[2603.25725\|SoftMimicGen]] | co-training source on the *deformable* axis: non-rigid-registration twin data engine (1,000 demos from 1–3 examples) | zero-shot 63.3% → sim-real co-train 93.3% (YAM Bag Loading); +25–97% over source-only across tasks | the deformable instance of the H4 co-training-balance delta — an explicit sim-real co-train gain the cited deformable row [[2604.08544\|SIM1]] (synthetic-only, no co-train) and rigid [[2605.26638\|HyperSim]] do not show; but one-shot co-train, not a closed loop folding deployment data back |
| [[2503.22634\|Empirical-Analysis-Sim-and-Real-Cotraining]] | co-training-*mechanism* on a *grounded* contact-rich planar-pushing task: empirical mixing-ratio (α) sweep + sim-and-sim ablation isolating visual/physics/task gaps | 2–7× improvement with limited real data (10/20 → 19/20 at \|D_R\|=50); cotraining beats pretrain-finetune; small physics gaps degrade contact transfer sharply | substantiates H4 (the sim:real ratio is a tunable optimum) and H2 (twin-data value scales with physical fidelity) on the *physics-gap* axis where [[2604.13645\|CFG-ADDA]]'s representation-alignment analysis (generic MuJoCo) is silent — but one-shot co-train on a contact task, not a closed deployment-grounded fold-back loop |
| [[2607.13154\|WANDA]] | data engine from a *single* RGBD demo: Gaussian-splat + BundleSDF reconstruction, VLM-guided spatial re-configuration, and factorized (splat background + Isaac Sim foreground) rendering, for *open-world mobile* manipulation | 75.6% sim SR from 100 generated demos (matches 40–60 teleop demos); 54.8% real progress across 16 environments; first reported zero-shot cross-embodiment transfer (40% on an unseen robot) | extends B2's single-demo data-engine lineage ([[2604.10579\|AffordGen]], [[2504.13175\|Novel-Demonstration-Generation-Gaussian]]) to *mobile* manipulation and adds a cross-embodiment data point no B2 row has — but open-loop generation, not a deployment-grounded fold-back round |
| [[2607.26903\|Pegasus]] | data engine from *passive human video*: task-graph extraction + hierarchical affordance latent + cross-embodiment *graph* translation + iterative physics-constrained verification (not pixel/geometry reconstruction) | +11.3 pp SR augmenting real-robot data; 93.5% IK-error / 90.9% collision-rate reduction via closed-loop physics verification | a structured-*graph* alternative to the pixel/geometry human-video engines ([[2606.12604\|EgoEngine]], [[2606.08828\|Video2Sim2Real]]) — abstracts to embodiment-invariant task semantics before re-grounding, with an explicit physics-verification reject loop, but single-author/no released benchmark and no deployment fold-back |
| [[2602.09023\|TwinRL]] | twin as *dual-role*: a one-shot smartphone-video 3DGS+segmentation twin both synthesizes trajectory data (imitation warm-up) *and* runs parallel online RL to seed the real replay buffer, then scores which real-world start configurations are failure-prone to target scarce human-in-the-loop resets | 4-task avg 95% SR in ~20 min vs ConRFT's 31 / HiL-SERL's 30; twin-trajectory augmentation lifts SFT 27%→70%; seeding with 20 successful twin rollouts reaches 100% at 3.5k steps vs 90% at 5.0k unseeded (adding 40 *failure* rollouts drops SR to 70%) | not a re-grounding loop — the twin is built once and never re-reconstructed from deployment data, so it doesn't test H1's per-round fold-back; instead it is a third *use* of the twin (curriculum-director for real interaction, on top of data-engine) B2's own axis list doesn't yet name — and its failure-rollout ablation is a direct, if narrower, data point for H3's "grounding, not quantity" (synthetic *failures* actively hurt, not just fail to help) |
| [[2607.29172\|CLIFT]] | *no twin at all* — closed real→real flywheel: deploy, score rollouts with a reward model, refit an API-only foundation VLA from scratch on the relabeled data, redeploy, on a real Unitree G1 humanoid | 2 flywheel cycles, monotone: 93→100% (Box Packing), 70→98% (Cup Insertion), 53→96% (Bimanual Plate Handover); weaker base model (π0.5) improves too but *plateaus* well below the stronger one under the identical pipeline | the H1 counter-example the cluster lacked — genuine multi-round *monotone* closed-loop gain, reported round-by-round, but by discarding the simulator/twin entirely; reframes H1 from "does per-task fold-back beat one-shot" to "is twin re-grounding *necessary* for the monotone gain, or does any deployment-scored refit produce it" (H6 below) |
| [[2508.12252\|RTR]] | sim-pretrained dynamics-latent policy (domain-randomized, FiLM-conditioned), then a *single* real-world pass fine-tuning only the latent — with a physical robot-arm "teacher" rig for humanoid safety (support, curriculum, auto-reset) | RTR's latent-only real tuning beats freezing the policy and beats full-policy fine-tuning (which collapses); beats RMA's reconstruction-based adaptation module head-to-head (Table 4) | the deployment-time-adaptation instance of the twin-free lineage (Cluster D territory, not B2) — sim gives the *initial* policy and the latent's structure, but the real-world step never touches the simulator or asset; one pass, not iterated, so it doesn't test monotonicity either, but sharpens the "what survives from sim after round 0" question CLIFT raises |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a closed deployment-grounded co-training loop beats one-shot and pure-source training), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — On a *per-task object* twin, fold-back beats one-shot over rounds (Arcadia only showed it at lifecycle scale, one pass).**
   - *Prediction*: at the per-task object-grounded twin scale (not [[2512.00076|Arcadia]]'s room+backbone lifecycle), folding deployment data back over *successive* rounds beats a one-shot [[2605.26638|HyperSim]]-style co-train and [[2602.12628|RL-Co]]'s single SFT-RL pass on unseen-object generalization, the gap widening each round — replicating Arcadia's feedback-on/off direction (88.5 vs 86.9) as a *trajectory* rather than a single delta.
   - *Test*: closed-loop vs one-shot over N rounds on [[2506.18088|RoboTwin-2.0]]'s per-task object-twin generation + 50-task / unseen-object generalization-SR protocol (+24.4% real few-shot / +21% zero-shot — grounds the dependent variable, the same substrate backing H3's grounded-vs-generic and H4's sim:real-ratio sweep); report unseen-object SR per round. NOTE: RoboTwin 2.0 covers the SR-substrate leg only — the round-over-round monotone-vs-drift trajectory (H1's per-round widening, H5's drift) remains a field gap with no named benchmark.
   - *Row*: Arcadia (one-pass lifecycle) / RoboTwin 2.0 (object-twin unseen-object generalization SR) / HyperSim (one-shot co-train) vs RialTo (data engine).
   - *Falsifier*: the closed loop ties one-shot at per-task scale → fold-back's lifecycle result does not transfer to object twins and a single co-train suffices.
2. **H2 — Twin-data value scales with reconstruction fidelity (the exchange rate).**
   - *Prediction*: [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]]'s 30 sim ≈ 30 real exchange rate improves with twin fidelity (link to B1) — a higher-fidelity twin makes each sim demo worth more real demos.
   - *Test*: vary reconstruction fidelity; measure the sim:real exchange rate (sim demos to match N real).
   - *Row*: Real-is-Sim (exchange rate) / RialTo (data engine).
   - *Falsifier*: the exchange rate is flat in fidelity → twin data value is independent of grounding and B1's fidelity is irrelevant here.
3. **H3 — Grounding, not quantity, drives the target-twin advantage.**
   - *Prediction*: replicating [[2403.03949|RialTo]]'s 90%-vs-10% across object classes, *grounding* the twin in real reconstruction (not the *amount* of twin data) is what carries the advantage — generic-asset data at any volume stays near 10%.
   - *Test*: vary {grounded vs generic twin} × {twin-data volume}; report which main-effect carries SR.
   - *Row*: RialTo (data engine).
   - *Falsifier*: enough generic-asset data closes the gap → quantity substitutes for grounding and the twin need not be target-specific.
   - *Cross-cluster tension flagged by [[2410.07408|Digital-Cousins-ACDC]]* (Cluster A): ACDC's own 90%-vs-25% zero-shot result looks like this falsifier realized — *variety* of semantic cousins beating a single fixed digital twin — but the two results test different axes. [[2403.03949|RialTo]]'s 90-vs-10 is *reconstruction fidelity* (how well the twin matches the *specific* real object); ACDC's 90-vs-25 is *semantic variety* (how many different-but-functionally-similar objects the policy sees). A "quantity substitutes for grounding" falsifier needs a same-object ablation (vary the volume of generic-but-unrelated data against one target-grounded twin), not a cross-object comparison against a fixed single twin — so ACDC sharpens what the falsifier experiment must control for rather than closing H3 outright.
4. **H4 — Co-training balance is a tunable, not a fixed ratio.**
   - *Prediction*: there is a sim:real ratio that maximizes unseen-object SR, and too much twin data swamps the few real demos — the optimum tracks [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]]'s ~1:1, [[2605.26638|HyperSim]]'s 35-demo regime, and [[2604.13645|CFG-ADDA]]'s mechanistically-derived optimal mixing-ratio band (SR peaks within (0.016, 0.3), degrades outside both ends).
   - *Test*: sweep the sim:real ratio; report unseen-object SR vs ratio, and check the [[2604.13645|CFG-ADDA]] mechanism — does representation-alignment (its ~50%-of-variance primary driver) track SR across the sweep?
   - *Row*: Real-is-Sim (1:1) / HyperSim (35-demo) / CFG-ADDA (mechanism + ratio band).
   - *Falsifier*: SR is flat in the ratio → balance is not delicate and any mix works.
5. **H5 (primary endpoint — gate-metric validity) — Sim-real reconstruction-fidelity r predicts per-fold SR gain, so the gate it defines is valid before any drift claim.**
   - *Prediction*: the whole fidelity-gate mechanism rests on an unmeasured premise — that the B1 reconstruction-fidelity score actually tracks downstream value. So the *primary* endpoint is the rank-correlation between **Δr (per-fold change in sim-real reconstruction fidelity)** and **ΔSR (per-fold change in unseen-object SR)**: if higher Δr reliably predicts higher ΔSR (positive Spearman rank-correlation), the gate is well-posed; only then does the gated-vs-ungated drift comparison (the secondary endpoint) carry weight. Pre-register the null (Δr and ΔSR are rank-uncorrelated, so the fidelity gate is arbitrary) and the rank-correlation MDE at the planned number of folds/rounds.
   - *Test*: across folds, log (Δr, ΔSR) pairs; report the Δr–ΔSR Spearman rank-correlation with a bootstrap CI as the primary result; *then*, conditional on a positive correlation, run the closed loop with vs without the per-fold fidelity gate and report the SR trajectory and drift over rounds.
   - *Row*: RialTo (data engine) / Real-is-Sim (correction).
   - *Falsifier*: Δr and ΔSR are rank-uncorrelated (the pre-registered null holds) → the fidelity gate is arbitrary and gating on r is unjustified, regardless of whether the ungated loop drifts.
6. **H6 (new, prompted by CLIFT) — Twin re-grounding is not necessary for the monotone multi-round gain; deployment-scored refitting alone reproduces it.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2607.29172|CLIFT]] gets a monotone multi-round SR trajectory (93→100%, 70→98%, 53→96% over 2 cycles) with *zero* asset/twin re-grounding — just reward-scored deployment rollouts refit each cycle. If a matched twin-free ablation of B2's own fold-back loop (same task, same round budget, fold-back *without* re-reconstructing the twin, using only a reward/advantage-labeled replay of deployment rollouts) reproduces the gain, twin re-grounding is not what H1's monotone improvement was measuring — the *scored refit* is the active ingredient, and the twin's role collapses to seeding round 0.
   - *Test*: on the same per-task object-twin setup as H1, run three arms — (a) fidelity-gated twin re-grounding fold-back, (b) twin-free deployment-scored refit (CLIFT-style), (c) one-shot co-train baseline — over matched rounds and compute budget; compare SR trajectories and the *marginal* gain of (a) over (b).
   - *Row*: CLIFT (twin-free flywheel) / RTR (twin-free single-pass latent tuning, the deployment-time-adaptation cousin) vs Arcadia / RialTo (twin re-grounding).
   - *Falsifier*: (b) plateaus well below (a) at matched budget → twin re-grounding *is* the active ingredient and CLIFT's result doesn't transfer past VLA-manipulation-with-a-strong-foundation-prior to twin-dependent regimes (e.g. locomotion, or weaker base models — CLIFT's own π0.5 arm already plateaus lower than GROD's, a within-paper hint this may not transfer cleanly).

> [!warning] Risks
> - **The per-round monotone trend sits below the real-robot noise floor** — a round-over-round SR-gain trajectory at REALM-class N~50 per round cannot resolve a small per-fold ΔSR from binomial sampling noise (SE ≈ 7 pp at 50% SR), so a "monotone improvement vs drift" curve can be an artifact of trial scarcity. → Pre-register the per-round ΔSR MDE and the Δr–ΔSR rank-correlation gate (H5) *before* claiming monotonicity; size the per-round trial budget so the targeted gain clears the binomial SE, or report the trajectory as power-limited.
> - **Closed-loop can drift** — folding deployment data back may amplify reconstruction errors over rounds. → Gate each fold on a B1 reconstruction-fidelity check (H5); reject folds that lower sim-real r.
> - **Co-training balance is delicate** — too much twin data swamps the few real demos, and [[2604.13645|CFG-ADDA]] shows SR degrades outside the (0.016, 0.3) mixing band on both ends (worst on physics-only gaps). → Tune the sim:real ratio per [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]]'s 1:1 finding, [[2605.26638|HyperSim]]'s 35-demo regime, and CFG-ADDA's mechanistically-derived optimal band (H4); treat the ratio as a hyperparameter.
> - **Grounding cost per object** — every new object needs reconstruction before it can be a data source. → Amortize via amortized parameter-inference nets ([[1702.02453|UP-OSI]]-style; see B1's Risks) + generative reconstruction priors; reuse twins across tasks.

### B3 — Generalizable Constitutive-Law Inversion: Learning the Physics, Not Just the Parameters

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Real-to-Sim-to-Real |
| **Thesis** | Parameter-only system identification recovers the *parameters* of a constitutive law the engineer chose; learning the functional *form* from video is now also done — material-agnostic neural constitutive sysID exists. But every such result is shown for *recovery generalization* (more materials, longer sequences), not for the two things that would make a learned law matter for robotics: that it extrapolates to *unseen geometry* a parameter fit cannot, and that it survives a *closed robot real→sim→real loop*. The first principle: the constitutive form, not its parameters, is the binding constraint on geometry extrapolation. The field assumes learning the form from video is itself the contribution. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]] (method), [[2505.16971\|UniPhy]] (method), [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]] (method), [[2604.27367\|DOT-Sim]] (method), [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] (method), [[2506.02794\|PhysGaia]] (benchmark), [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] (survey) |
| **Key targets** | Match [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]]'s reconstruction loss <1e-3 + generalization to unseen geometries up to 1M particles from real 2D video; beat parameter-only sysID ([[2604.27367\|DOT-Sim]], [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]]) on held-out material/geometry; supply a generalizable dynamics model to [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s r=0.915 joint-inversion loop; measure recovery fidelity on [[2506.02794\|PhysGaia]] (GT 3D particle trajectories + GT material params across liquids/gases/viscoelastic/textiles via MPM/FLIP/Pyro/Vellum solvers, physical-realism metrics, SOTA DyNVS PSNR <30) and the parameter-fit baseline on [[2510.22975\|VoMP]] (166-object volumetric material-estimation benchmark, Young's-modulus ALRE 0.0409 vs NeRF2Physics 0.1346) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: parameter-only sysID recovers the *parameters* of a law the engineer picked — Young's modulus and Poisson's ratio for a neo-Hookean solid ([[2604.27367|DOT-Sim]]), stiffness and friction for a spring-mass deformable ([[2503.17973|PhysTwin]]) — which works when the chosen law matches the material but caps generalization at the functional form.
- **Today's answers**: [[2304.14369|NCLaw]] shows the move — embed a *neural* constitutive law inside a differentiable Material Point Method simulator, let the simulator enforce conservation (momentum, mass) structurally, and have the network learn only the material-specific stress-strain map under physics-aware priors (rotation equivariance, undeformed-state equilibrium); it generalizes by *orders of magnitude* over data-driven baselines, real 2D dough video, loss <1e-3. [[2508.01112|MASIV]] makes the *learn-the-form-from-video* half consensus — the first material-agnostic neural-constitutive sysID, learning both elasticity and plasticity from video with *no* material prior, explicitly inspired by NCLaw — so "learn the functional form" is no longer the open frontier the card once treated it as.
- **The opening**: NCLaw's generalization to unseen boundary conditions, geometries up to 1M particles, longer horizons, and multi-physics — from a *learned law* not a parameter fit — is the existence proof that the functional form, not its parameters, is the binding constraint. But [[2508.01112|MASIV]]'s generalization is across material *types* and added training sequences of the same object, *not* the unseen-geometry-to-1M-particles extrapolation, and like NCLaw/[[2505.16971|UniPhy]] it has no robot sim2real loop. So the two unattacked deltas remain: (1) isolating the learned-law-vs-parameter-fit advantage *on held-out geometry* (H1), which MASIV never does, and (2) the closed robot loop (H2/H3) — where the robot-side dynamics-learning work ([[2407.07889|AdaptiGraph]], [[2601.17251|EMPM]], [[2506.15680|Particle-Grid-Neural-Dynamics]], [[2512.13214|Differentiable-MPM-Control]]) all sit on black-box dynamics or parameter-fit axes, *not* a learned constitutive law in the loop.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The cause of a material's motion is its constitutive law — the stress-strain map — not the scalars of any one parameterization. Conservation laws are universal and belong in the simulator; the constitutive law is material-specific, the only degree of freedom that varies across materials, so it is what must be *learned*. [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]'s orders-of-magnitude generalization from a learned law is the evidence that the functional form is the binding constraint on extrapolation, not the parameter values.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "learn the functional form from video" — [[2508.01112|MASIV]], [[2511.06299|Physics-Informed-Deformable-GS]], [[2406.04155|Lagrangian-Particle-Optimization]] and [[2505.16971|UniPhy]] already do that. The live assumption is that learning the form is *itself* the contribution, so a learned-law paper stops at recovery generalization (more materials, longer sequences). That leaves untested whether the learned form actually beats a parameter fit *on unseen geometry* (the form's whole claimed advantage) and whether it survives a *robot* loop — the two deltas MASIV's material-agnostic recovery does not touch.
- **The bet**: A learned constitutive law beats a parameter-only fit (e.g. [[2503.17973|PhysTwin]]) **on held-out geometry up to 1M particles** — reproducing [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]'s order-of-magnitude extrapolation advantage and <1e-3 loss — *and* survives a **closed robot real→sim→real loop**: dropped into [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s pipeline in place of the fixed soft-body model, it holds forward correlation across material variation a single parameter fit cannot cover (the missing proof neither NCLaw nor MASIV supplies). Falsifiable: if parameter-only sysID matches the learned law on held-out geometry, the functional form is not the binding constraint and MASIV-style recovery generalization is the whole story.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what is recovered* (the law itself / parameters of a chosen law / parameters jointly with appearance / fixed rigid physics / the law+appearance jointly via feedforward generation) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | What is recovered | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]] | the constitutive *law* (neural stress-strain in differentiable MPM) | <1e-3 loss, generalizes to 1M particles / unseen geometry / multi-physics, real dough video | demonstrated for *recovery* generalization, not a closed sim2real loop on a robot |
| [[2505.16971\|UniPhy]] | a *unified* neural law (latent-conditioned MPM functions, frozen; per-scene latent inferred) | elastic reconstruction 5.2e-6 vs [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]] 2.4e-4; generalizes to unseen geometry / velocity / horizon | one law spanning *all* materials via a latent, no preset material type — beats [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]] on recovery but still no robot sim2real loop |
| [[2508.01112\|MASIV]] | the constitutive *law*, *material-agnostic* (elasticity + plasticity, no prior, from video) | first material-agnostic neural-constitutive sysID, inspired by [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]] | the topThreat — makes "learn the form from video" consensus, but generalizes across material *types* not unseen *geometry*, and no robot loop |
| [[2511.06299\|Physics-Informed-Deformable-GS]] | a *unified* constitutive law over a time-evolving Gaussian material field | physics-informed deformable GS, unified constitutive laws | learned-law-from-video for deformable GS — recovery generalization, no unseen-geometry isolation, no robot loop |
| [[2406.04155\|Lagrangian-Particle-Optimization]] | *geometry-agnostic* continuum sysID (physics-augmented NeRF + particle optimization) | improves geometry-agnostic physics-from-video sysID | confirms geometry-agnostic recovery is solved — but recovery, not the learned-law-vs-parameter-fit extrapolation test |
| [[2407.07889\|AdaptiGraph]] | a *black-box* learned graph dynamics model (no constitutive law) | material-adaptive graph dynamics for manipulation | robot-side dynamics learning, but black-box GNN not a constitutive law — the robot-loop contrast on the wrong axis |
| [[2506.15680\|Particle-Grid-Neural-Dynamics]] | a learned *particle-grid* neural dynamics model (data-driven) | learns deformable dynamics for manipulation | learned dynamics in a robot loop, but not a conservation-structured constitutive law — black-box, caps extrapolation |
| [[2603.22039\|RAFL]] | a learned *element-level residual acceleration field* over a base differentiable sim (geometry-transferable via shared local features, *not* a constitutive law) | zero-shot accuracy gains on **unseen morphologies** in sim-to-sim + sim-to-real, while **parameter sysID frequently exhibits negative transfer**; reports geometry-dependent effects get absorbed into identified parameters | the **only** robot-side / sim-to-real result running B3's central head-to-head — a geometry-transferable learned correction *beats parameter sysID on held-out morphology*, with parameter sysID showing **negative transfer** — supplying the robot-side evidence H1's falsifier and H2's loop lack (every other robot-side row [[2407.07889\|AdaptiGraph]]/[[2506.15680\|Particle-Grid-Neural-Dynamics]] runs no learned-vs-parameter geometry comparison); its abstract states B3's first principle near-verbatim ("identified parameters absorb geometry-dependent effects rather than intrinsic material behavior, more pronounced when geometry is a design variable") — but it recovers a *black-box residual dynamics field*, the very black-box route the card argues *caps* extrapolation, not the conservation-structured learned LAW the bet proposes; add-not-replace that *strengthens* B3's contrarian position (proves the learned-correction route works) on the capping axis, and tightens the Risk that a residual is the black-box competitor a learned constitutive law must beat on extrapolation-vs-interpretability |
| [[2601.17251\|EMPM]] | learned *elastoplastic* MPM dynamics for manipulation | elastoplastic dynamics model for robot deformable manipulation | closer to MPM but parameter/dynamics-fit, not a learned constitutive *form* generalizing across geometry |
| [[2512.13214\|Differentiable-MPM-Control]] | differentiable MPM for *control* (fixed/parameterized law) | differentiable-MPM control of deformables | uses differentiable MPM in the loop but with a fixed law — the robot-loop slot B3's learned law would fill |
| [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]] | *parameters* of a chosen spring-mass law from video | generalizes to unseen interactions | the parameter-only baseline B3 generalizes past — caps at the functional form |
| [[2604.27367\|DOT-Sim]] | *parameters* (Young's modulus, Poisson's ratio) of a fixed form | 96.55% zero-shot, 1.71 mm Chamfer | fixed form, point-parameter recovery — the per-object fit B3 replaces |
| [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] | *parameters* of a soft-body law jointly with 3DGS appearance | r=0.915 | the joint-inversion loop a learned law would slot into (B1) |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | *priors* over a fixed law (VLM-inferred) + online estimation | 57% vs 23% OOD | priors over a fixed law, not a learned functional form |
| [[2404.09833\|Video2Game]] | fixed rigid physics + NeRF/mesh appearance | 100+ FPS browser | fixed rigid physics — the contrast for learned-deformable-law generalization |
| [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] | — (names physical-consistency + latent-identifiability gaps) | survey | the integration-layer gaps a learned-law inversion fills |
| [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] | — (world models as learned simulators needing physical consistency) | survey | the rationale for learning the law, not just parameters |
| [[2606.16202\|EgoPhys]] | a *generalizable prior over parameters* of a chosen law (codebook-distilled dense spring-stiffness field for a spring-mass deformable, from single egocentric RGB video) | CD **0.015** / TE **0.025** resim, zero-shot to 11 held-out objects, **77.6%** config-error reduction on a physical xArm6 *without* real fine-tuning | the robot-loop-with-generalization slot the table's robot-side rows ([[2407.07889\|AdaptiGraph]]/[[2601.17251\|EMPM]]/[[2506.15680\|Particle-Grid-Neural-Dynamics]]) leave empty — but it generalizes a *learned prior over the parameters* of a fixed spring-mass form, not the constitutive *form* itself (H1's lever), so it closes H2's loop on the parameter-fit side, not the learned-law side |
| [[2510.24673\|Differentiable-Rheometry]] | the constitutive *law* (frame-invariant Tensor Basis Neural Network embedded in a differentiable fluid solver) *plus* a distillation of it into interpretable classical models | near-perfect velocity reconstruction, generalizes to an unseen porous-medium geometry, robust to **10×** lower resolution + **4%** noise, **70%** identification of Linear PTT across **5** classical models via BIC | runs the unseen-*geometry* extrapolation H1 isolates and adds a learned-form→closed-form bridge no row covers, but for fluids only and never in a robot loop — the geometry-extrapolation leg done, the H2 sim2real loop still open like [[2304.14369\|NCLaw]]/[[2508.01112\|MASIV]] |
| [[2406.12769\|Latent-Intuitive-Physics]] | a *probabilistic latent* physics representation (no explicit parameters), inferred from a single 3D video and transferred to a physics engine | novel-geometry avg Euclidean distance **34.54** vs CConv **52.49** / PAC-NeRF **51.10**; long-term ¯d **38.37** vs DPI-Net **90.21**, with physical parameters *unknown* | directly backs H1's geometry-extrapolation leg with a *parameter-free* recovered representation — a distinct row vs the explicit-neural-law rows ([[2304.14369\|NCLaw]]/[[2505.16971\|UniPhy]]/[[2508.01112\|MASIV]]) — but fluids-only, single-video, and no robot loop, so it sharpens the unseen-geometry test without the H2 closed-loop proof |
| [[2501.18982\|OmniPhysGS]] | a *mixture-of-expert* learnable constitutive law (per-Gaussian neural model assigned from a 12-expert ensemble: elastic/viscoelastic/plastic/fluid) in a differentiable MPM | **+3–16%** CLIPSIM text-alignment for single objects, **+~4%** for multi-object heterogeneous scenes, **75%** less training memory than Warp-based MPM | adds the expert-ensemble *what-is-recovered* axis vs the single-unified-law row ([[2511.06299\|Physics-Informed-Deformable-GS]]), but supervised by text-to-video SDS (generation, not real-video recovery) and scored on CLIPSIM not physics GT — so it sits on the learn-the-law-*for-generation* side, neither H1's geometry-extrapolation nor H2's robot loop |
| [[2508.13911\|PhysGM]] | the constitutive law + appearance, *jointly*, **feedforward** from a single image — transformer-based, one-to-one MPM-particle↔Gaussian correspondence, two-stage training with **DPO fine-tuned using the non-differentiable simulator itself as reward** | **<1 min** 4D generation (vs per-scene optimization baselines); beats [[2501.18982\|OmniPhysGS]]/DreamerPhysics on CLIP_sim (0.2748) and User-Preference-Rate (42.8%, DPO lifts from 30%) | the same generation-side slot as [[2501.18982\|OmniPhysGS]] (not a robotics-grounding result) but a genuinely new mechanism — RL-from-simulator-feedback via DPO; evaluated on CLIP-sim/user-preference, **not** physics-recovery accuracy or robot SR, so it does not touch H1's geometry-extrapolation test or H2's robot loop — this limitation is explicit, DPO preference is not a physics-accuracy result |
| [[2605.22597\|MoSA-Continuum]] | a *learned residual* on a *chosen isotropic constitutive prior* — bounded residual-stress corrections (pre-linear / microplane-redistribution / post-linear) + an implicit heterogeneity field η(x), supervised by motion-constrained higher-order 3DGS losses | lowest Chamfer 13.5 / EMD 8.8 on synthetic; 31.35 real PSNR (vs GIC 30.02); lifts elastic-rabbit placement 42%→68% real | the *learned-residual-over-a-physics-prior* sibling of [[2603.22039\|RAFL]] on the continuum-dynamics axis — captures mild anisotropy/heterogeneity a fixed isotropic fit misses *and* closes a real-robot SR loop (42→68%), strengthening the contrarian "learned correction beats parameter fit" leg — but it residualizes a *chosen* prior rather than learning the constitutive *form* ([[2304.14369\|NCLaw]]'s lever), and shows no held-out-geometry extrapolation, so it sharpens H1/H2 without supplying the form-learning proof |
| [[2606.04206\|DLO-Lab]] | — (a *differentiable deformable simulator + benchmark* substrate, not a recovery method): Discrete-Elastic-Rods differentiable physics with bending plasticity + two-way rigid/soft coupling, 10-task DLO suite | ~58% zero-shot sim-to-real (Wiring-ring), 60.0% fine-tuned multi-task VLA; CMA-ES beats first-order RL on non-smooth DLO landscapes | the differentiable-deformable-sim *substrate* a learned constitutive law would be embedded in (the [[2604.27367\|DOT-Sim]]/[[2512.13214\|Differentiable-MPM-Control]] DLO analog) and a named sim-to-real DLO benchmark beside [[2506.02794\|PhysGaia]] — but ships a *fixed* DER law with parameters, and its own finding that DLO gradients are non-smooth (CMA-ES > first-order) is the caution H2's BPTT-into-the-loop bet must clear |
| [[2607.17132\|BoxTwin]] | *parameters of a chosen non-linear form* — per-hinge elastoplasticity (nonlinear elastic torque, a yield-threshold plastic flow rule shifting the rest angle, and a damage variable driven by accumulated plastic slip) fit from video onto an articulated rigid-link kinematic tree | joint-angle trajectories track hardware over long horizons through repeated actuation cycles despite accumulated hysteresis/wear (qualitative overlays, no quantitative error reported) | extends the deformable-parameter axis past [[2503.17973\|PhysTwin]]'s spring-mass and [[2608.06164\|BendTwin]]'s bending-stiffness to *permanent* deformation (yield + damage) — still a chosen functional form with fit parameters, not a learned law (H1), and reports no quantitative recovery error or robot-loop SR, so it strengthens B1/B3's deformable-parameter substrate without moving either open bet |
| [[2602.18707\|CLASH]] | *neither* a constitutive law nor its parameters — a MuJoCo collision solver is *distilled* into a frozen MLP surrogate (pre-collision state + contact params → post-collision state), then the contact parameters (not the surrogate weights) are gradient-identified from ~10 real collisions plus 10 early-stopped residual fine-tune steps | prediction accuracy 0.76 vs MuJoCo's 0.64 / real-data-only network's 0.42; real CMA-ES search 42–48% faster; hybrid-sim SAC policies double Route-1 SR (8/10 vs 4/10 MuJoCo) | the rigid-*contact* answer to B3's own stated Risk that "differentiable MPM is mature for soft bodies, not rich contact" — sidesteps differentiability entirely (distill, then a few gradient/fine-tune steps on parameters + a frozen surrogate), splitting the gap into an interpretable identified-parameter part plus a small learned residual; distinct axis from every deformable-law row (BoxTwin/BendTwin/NCLaw/MASIV) — B3's learned-law-vs-parameter-fit split has, until now, had no rigid-contact instance at all |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (the learned law, not its parameters, is the extrapolation lever), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (front line) — A conservation-structured learned law beats BOTH a black-box residual and a residualized-prior on held-out geometry — the live wedge now that learned-beats-parameter is settled.**
   - *Prediction*: learned-beats-parameter-fit is now conceded baseline, not the bet — [[2603.22039|RAFL]] already shows a learned residual beating parameter sysID on unseen morphology (parameter sysID even shows negative transfer), and [[2605.22597|MoSA-Continuum]] lifts real-robot placement 42→68% over an isotropic fit. The live open bet: a *conservation-structured* neural law ([[2304.14369|NCLaw]]-style, momentum/mass enforced structurally in the differentiable MPM) beats *both* RAFL's black-box residual *and* MoSA's residualized-prior on *unseen* geometry — reproducing NCLaw's order-of-magnitude extrapolation advantage (<1e-3 loss) *and* a strictly lower conservation-law residual at matched held-out geometry up to 1M particles.
   - *Test*: fit a conservation-structured neural law, a RAFL-style black-box residual, and a MoSA-style residualized-prior on the *same* real video; test all three on held-out geometries up to 1M particles; report *both* extrapolation error and conservation-law residual for the three arms, scoring recovery fidelity on [[2506.02794|PhysGaia]] (GT trajectories + GT material params, so a recovered law's correct-dynamics reproduction is measurable — though PhysGaia does not itself run the geometry-extrapolation-to-1M-particles sweep, which stays a field gap; it backs the recovery-fidelity leg) and the conceded parameter-fit baseline on [[2510.22975|VoMP]]'s 166-object material-estimation benchmark + ALRE metric (vs only method self-numbers today).
   - *Row*: NCLaw (conservation-structured law) / RAFL (black-box residual, unseen-morphology beat over parameter sysID) / MoSA-Continuum (residualized-prior, 42→68% real SR) / PhysGaia (GT-physics recovery fidelity) / VoMP (conceded parameter-fit ALRE baseline).
   - *Falsifier*: the conservation-structured law's extrapolation error and conservation-residual tie or exceed RAFL's black-box residual and MoSA's residualized-prior at matched held-out geometry → the conservation structure buys nothing over a learned correction, and RAFL/MoSA-style residual learning is the whole story (learned-beats-parameter, the conceded leg, is doubly settled and no longer the wedge).
   - *A further arm flagged by [[2510.21447|PhysWorld-Deformable]]*: B1's PhysWorld-Deformable *selects* a constitutive model via VLM reasoning over a library of candidate laws (Neo-Hookean, Anisotropic Hyperelastic, …), then fits and distills it — a distinct option beyond H1's conservation-structured-law-vs-black-box-residual-vs-residualized-prior test: choosing among discrete named forms rather than fixing one (RAFL/MoSA-Continuum) or learning a continuous one from scratch (NCLaw). It reports 47× inference speedup and CD 0.010 (vs PhysTwin 0.012) on the distilled GNN, but never runs H1's held-out-geometry extrapolation test itself, so it widens the arm-space without yet resolving which wins.
2. **H2 — A learned law survives the full real→sim→real loop (the missing proof).**
   - *Prediction*: dropping an [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]-recovered law into [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s pipeline in place of the fixed soft-body model holds forward correlation across material variation a single parameter fit cannot cover — supplying the closed-loop evidence NCLaw alone lacks.
   - *Test*: swap the learned law into the joint-inversion loop; report forward r across materials vs the fixed-law baseline, using [[2603.22039|RAFL]]'s residual-field-over-base-sim as the black-box-correction control arm (it already closes a sim-to-real loop on unseen morphology, so the learned *law* must beat both the parameter fit *and* RAFL's residual to justify the conservation structure).
   - *Row*: NCLaw (the law) / Real-to-Sim GS (joint inversion) / RAFL (closed sim-to-real loop, black-box residual control arm).
   - *Falsifier*: the learned law does not preserve forward correlation across materials → recovery-generalization does not transfer to a sim2real loop; or RAFL's black-box residual matches the conservation-structured law in the loop → the structure buys nothing over a residual correction.
3. **H3 — Real-video-only law recovery works for manipulation deformables.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]'s recovery runs on cloth, cable, or food from a single RGB-D interaction, supplying B2's co-training engine a transferable dynamics model per material class.
   - *Test*: recover laws for cloth/cable/food from single interactions; report reconstruction loss and downstream co-training value, scoring per-material recovery loss on [[2506.02794|PhysGaia]]'s textile + viscoelastic + liquid scenes with GT physics (exactly the deformable classes H3 targets).
   - *Row*: NCLaw (the law) / PhysGaia (per-material GT-physics recovery).
   - *Falsifier*: single-view recovery is under-constrained for manipulation deformables → multi-view/RGB-D is required and the cheap-recovery claim narrows.
4. **H4 — Structural priors are load-bearing for off-distribution physicality.**
   - *Prediction*: keeping [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]'s rotation-equivariance + undeformed-equilibrium priors as hard constraints is what keeps the learned law physical off-distribution — dropping them yields non-physical extrapolation despite low reconstruction loss.
   - *Test*: ablate the structural priors; gate on conservation-law residuals, not just reconstruction loss; report off-distribution physicality using [[2506.02794|PhysGaia]]'s explicit physical-realism-vs-photorealism separation (it shows SOTA DyNVS hits low PSNR <30 yet fails physical realism — the physicality-vs-reconstruction metric split this ablation needs).
   - *Row*: NCLaw (the law) / PhysGaia (physicality-vs-reconstruction split).
   - *Falsifier*: the law stays physical without the priors → the inductive biases are decorative.
5. **H5 — The learned law degrades gracefully to a parameter fit when unidentifiable.**
   - *Prediction*: where the material is in-distribution for a known law, the learned law matches the parameter fit (no over-flexibility cost), and only on novel materials does it pull ahead — so the learned law dominates without a downside.
   - *Test*: compare learned-law vs parameter-fit on in-distribution materials; report any over-flexibility penalty, defining "matches the parameter fit" against [[2510.22975|VoMP]]'s volumetric Young's/Poisson/density estimation benchmark (166 objects, the in-distribution parameter-recovery reference).
   - *Row*: NCLaw (the law) / VoMP (in-distribution parameter-recovery reference) vs DOT-Sim (fixed-form fit).
   - *Falsifier*: the learned law underperforms the parameter fit on in-distribution materials → flexibility costs accuracy where the law is known.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Recovery is solved; the robot loop is not** — the learn-the-law case now has multiple recovery existence proofs ([[2304.14369|NCLaw]], [[2508.01112|MASIV]], [[2505.16971|UniPhy]], [[2511.06299|Physics-Informed-Deformable-GS]]), all demonstrating *generalizable law recovery from video* but none a closed sim2real loop on a robot. The remaining novelty is *not* "learn the form" (consensus) but the geometry-extrapolation delta (H1) and the missing robot loop (H2) — the most speculative slice in Cluster B (consistent with A2's "no existence proof yet" hedge). → Treat 1M-particle generalization as transfer-of-recovery evidence, not sim2real-loop evidence; H2 (learned law into [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]]'s loop) is the go/no-go that supplies the missing proof.
> - **Learned laws can violate physics off-distribution** despite priors — a neural stress-strain map may extrapolate non-physically. → Keep [[2304.14369|NCLaw]]'s structural priors as hard constraints and gate on conservation-law residuals (H4), not just reconstruction loss.
> - **Differentiable MPM is mature for soft bodies, not rich contact** — the learn-the-law regime is deformables. → Scope the 1M-particle generalization bet to elastoplastic/fluid materials where MPM differentiability holds; treat rigid-contact laws as a separate, harder problem. [[2602.18707|CLASH]] is an early answer on that separate problem — but it sidesteps differentiability rather than solving it, distilling the collision solver into a frozen surrogate and gradient-identifying only the contact parameters from ~10 real collisions, so it does not close H1/H2's learned-*law*-vs-parameter-fit test on rigid contact, only demonstrates a cheaper way to adapt the parameter side.
> - **Differentiable-deformable gradients can be non-smooth even where the engine exists** — [[2606.04206|DLO-Lab]]'s differentiable DER simulator finds DLO landscapes have "gradient inaccessibility / non-smooth landscapes" from discontinuous contacts, so CMA-ES beats first-order RL on its own benchmark. → Treat H2's "drop the learned law into a differentiable inversion loop" as gradient-fragile on contact-rich deformables; validate that BPTT through the law is stable on a DLO-Lab-class task before claiming the loop closes, and fall back to sample-based optimization where gradients vanish.
> - **Real-video-only recovery is under-constrained** — a single 2D view may not identify the full law. → Use multi-view or RGB-D interaction (H3) and report identification variance; fall back to a fixed-form parameter fit ([[2604.27367|DOT-Sim]]/[[2503.17973|PhysTwin]]-style) when the learned law is unidentifiable (H5).

---

## Cluster C — Reality-Gap Measurement as Statistical Inference

*Stop asking "is the sim accurate?" Ask "what can I provably infer about real performance from imperfect, possibly-adversarial sims?" — treating a correlation number as a validity claim and a portfolio of biased sims as a variance-reduction estimator.*

### C1 — Per-Factor Correlation Validity as a Deployment Gate: Stress-Test, Then Route

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Reality-Gap Measurement as Inference |
| **Thesis** | That a sim-real correlation holds only on its measured conditions is now consensus — controlled per-factor variation against real is already run, and the statistics literature treats the gap as a *distribution*, not a scalar. What no one does is re-measure the *correlation itself* per perturbation factor and turn that per-(sim, factor) trust map into a deployment *router* that picks which biased sim to trust under each shift. The first principle: trustworthiness is factor-resolved, so a per-factor validity map can route deployment where one global r cannot. The field measures per-factor *success drops* but never re-measures per-factor *correlation*, nor routes on it. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2402.08191\|THE-COLOSSEUM]] (benchmark), [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] (benchmark), [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] (benchmark), [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] (survey), [[2512.19562\|REALM]] (benchmark), [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]] (benchmark), [[2505.09698\|ManipBench]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | Diagnose current high-r sims ([[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] r≥0.85, [[2605.06311\|VISER]] r=0.92, [[2604.21686\|WorldMark]] ρ>0.9) as falling below r<0.7 under deliberate OOD shift over [[2402.08191\|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s 14 factors (30–50% single-perturbation SR drop, R̄²=0.614); route [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s biased-bank weights per factor to beat its 70–100% single-global-edge win rate; halve the real-trial spend [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] needs (20–25%) by trusting only validated-per-factor sims; [[2111.00765\|VSDR]] 64 real observations (the data-efficient sim-derived-score selection precedent H5's real-trial reduction leans on) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: two questions share one mechanism. First — is a benchmark's high r a real property of the sim or an artifact of the nominal conditions it was measured on? Second — once you have a per-factor table, can you *act* on it, routing which sim to trust per shift rather than deploying with one global scalar?
- **Today's answers**: [[2405.05941|SIMPLER]] reports r≥0.85/0.890, [[2605.06311|VISER]] r=0.92, [[2604.21686|WorldMark]] ρ>0.9 — every one an *in-distribution* correlation; [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]] shows single perturbations cause 30–50% SR drops (R̄²=0.614), *differently per factor*. But the diagnosis half is now partly run: [[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]] does one-factor-at-a-time controlled variation against 752 real tasks and reports a *high* global correlation (R=0.96) with per-factor SR sensitivities — landing on the falsifier side of "does r survive shift" — and [[2512.05024|Simulator-Fidelity-Quantile-Curves]] and [[1912.06321|Sim2Real-Predictivity]] make the "gap is a distribution / correlation is a fragile measured property" point rigorous.
- **The opening**: nobody re-measures the *correlation* per factor (MolmoSpaces reports per-factor SR sensitivities, not per-factor r) and nobody turns the result into a *router*; [[2512.19562|REALM]]'s per-perturbation real-to-sim validation supplies pre-validated rows the gate can ingest, so the per-(sim, factor) routing gate — the unattacked half — is buildable from existing data.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A sim-real correlation number only vouches for the sim under the exact conditions it was measured on; whether it holds out-of-distribution is a *different* number you have to measure, not assume. And a sim's trustworthiness is one number *per perturbation factor* — different physics shortcuts break under different shifts, so checking each factor separately catches structure one overall score hides. [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s 30–50% spread across factors (R̄²=0.614) is the direct evidence that the per-factor detail decides which sim to deploy.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "one in-distribution r proves the sim usable" — that the validity is conditional is conceded ([[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]] runs controlled per-factor variation; [[1912.06321|Sim2Real-Predictivity]] showed correlation is fragile/tuning-dependent in 2019; [[2512.05024|Simulator-Fidelity-Quantile-Curves]] formalizes the gap as a distribution). The live assumption is that the per-factor diagnosis ends at *success-drop sensitivities* and a *global* edge — that nobody re-measures the *correlation* per factor or *routes* on it. The portfolio work ([[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]) still weights sims by one aggregate edge; VSDR routes *policies*, not sims-per-factor.
- **The bet**: A per-(sim, factor) trust map — the *correlation* re-measured under each [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]] factor, ingesting [[2512.19562|REALM]]'s validated rows — used as a deployment *router* lifts [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s **70–100% win rate** over its single-global-edge baseline on shift-mixed deployment, and recovers [[2510.04354|SureSim]]'s **20–25%** real-trial reduction by skipping do-not-trust cells, with the gain largest on the factors where r collapses hardest. Falsifiable: if per-factor routing ties the single global edge (and [[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]]'s R=0.96 robustness holds per factor so there is nothing to route around), the per-factor detail is not deployment-relevant.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the sim's trustworthiness is characterized* (single in-distribution r / per-factor SR drop / per-factor validated / proxy-disagreement / cross-sim failure discovery / portfolio router) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How trustworthiness is characterized | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2402.08191\|THE-COLOSSEUM]] | per-factor SR drop across 14 axes | 30–50% single / ≥75% combined, R̄²=0.614 | the OOD shift harness + factor decomposition — but never re-measures *correlation* per factor |
| [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] | single in-distribution r + MMRV | r>0.85 / 0.890, low MMRV | one in-distribution scalar tuned via sysID — the number whose validity is stress-tested |
| [[2605.06311\|VISER]] | single in-distribution r, self-flags OOD | r=0.92, "drops drastically" under distractors | shows the in/OOD split exists but reports one global r, not a per-factor gate |
| [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] | aggregate predictive edge over a biased bank | 70–100% win with a *single* aggregate edge | the portfolio router the gate weights per factor — currently one global edge |
| [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] | context-dependent effectiveness + negative adaptation | shift can *invert* a relationship | warns validity is per-axis but supplies no per-factor map |
| [[2512.19562\|REALM]] | per-perturbation real-to-sim validation (15 factors) | r=0.92 overall / 0.88 default | pre-validated per-factor rows the gate ingests — not yet a routing mechanism |
| [[2604.21686\|WorldMark]] | proxy disagreement vs human judgment | ρ>0.9, control-alignment ≠ visual-quality leaders | proxies disagree, so the deploy decision is multi-dimensional — but no gate |
| [[2603.22126\|ROBOGATE]] | cross-simulator boundary failure discovery | LIBERO 97.65% → 0/68 Isaac Sim industrial (97.65 pp gap), AUC 0.780 | sharpest proof one benchmark's r doesn't transfer across sims — diagnosis, not routing |
| [[2606.10366\|Sim-Real-VLA-Eval]] | ranking correlation re-measured under 4 *perturbation types*, across 3 sims | [[2512.19562\|REALM]] alone preserves severity hierarchy (ρ=0.700→0.875 w/ post-train); 10-demo data optimum | ranks *which sim* survives perturbation (the per-factor diagnosis C1 needs), but does not turn the result into a per-(sim,factor) routing gate |
| [[2510.23571\|RobotArena-Infinity]] | real-to-sim-translated eval, perturbed along background/color/pose | 6 VLAs drop sharply under OOD shift; VLM scores align with 8,500+ human judgments | a scalable per-factor OOD harness (real-to-sim), but reports per-policy drops, not a re-measured *correlation* gate per factor |
| [[2501.16389\|Sim2Real-Encoder-Eval]] | offline per-component transfer diagnostic | manipulation-pretrained encoders top Domain-Invariance + Action scores | predicts transfer before training, per-component — a validity check, not a deployment gate |
| [[2512.16881\|PolaRiS]] | high-r evaluator from real-video scans | r=0.9 avg / 0.98 best, <20 min/scene | a high-r evaluator the gate must still stress-test per factor |
| [[2602.11337\|MolmoSpaces]] | one-factor-at-a-time controlled variation vs 752 real tasks (per-factor SR sensitivity) | R=0.96 global correlation, per-factor SR sensitivities | the strongest pre-run of H1, but lands on the *falsifier* side (r robust) and reports per-factor *SR*, never per-factor *correlation* or a router |
| [[1912.06321\|Sim2Real-Predictivity]] | sim-real correlation as a predictivity property (the lineage root, 2019) | shows correlation is fragile and tuning-dependent | the root that makes "correlation is a fragile measured property" consensus — diagnosis, not a per-factor gate |
| [[2512.05024\|Simulator-Fidelity-Quantile-Curves]] | the sim-real gap as a *distribution* (quantile curves), model-free | model-free fidelity via quantile curves | makes the first-principle rigorous (gap is a distribution, not a scalar) but LLM-survey domain, no robotics routing gate |
| [[2111.00765\|VSDR]] | combined sim-validation × real-OOD trust score, acted on to *select* policies | ranks policies by Spearman correlation with ground-truth in 86–100% of cases from as few as 64 real observations | the nearest prior that *acts* on a sim-derived validity score to make a deploy decision — but scores *one* sim with a *single combined* number to rank policies, never re-measuring per-factor *correlation* ([[2602.11337\|MolmoSpaces]]'s SR-sensitivity gap) nor routing *which sim* to trust per factor ([[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s portfolio inversion) |
| [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]] | single *global* sim/world-model-as-evaluator correlation (self-consistent video generation, forward-inverse + cross-view consistency) | r=0.984 in-dist / 0.929 closed-loop, MMRV=0.022; reproduces policy failure modes (0.56 avg) + uncertainty/early-termination from forward-inverse consistency error | a strictly stronger high-r evaluator (0.984 > 0.92) the gate must still stress-test per factor (H1 input) — but reports one *global* correlation, no per-factor r and no router, so it strengthens the diagnosis half, not the routing half |
| [[2505.09698\|ManipBench]] | measured proxy-real correlation (VLM-MCQ accuracy vs real-robot SR), broken down by task *category* | Pearson 0.889 (p=0.003) overall / 0.950 (p=0.001) fabric; per-category accuracy (pick-place 0.525, articulated 0.396, dynamic 0.357) | a granular-correlation-as-validity data point peer to [[2604.21686\|WorldMark]]'s proxy-disagreement row — but the correlation is per *task-category* not per *perturbation-factor*, and there is no router |
| [[2510.01708\|PolySim]] | (training-side) *Simulator Router* harmonizing multiple heterogeneous sims to train *one* policy, Wasserstein-mixed-kernel-closer-to-real | +52.8% / +44.3% zero-shot on unseen MuJoCo over IsaacSim/IsaacGym; deployed zero-shot on Unitree G1 | the closest portfolio-of-sims precedent besides [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]], but its router is a *training-time* dynamics-randomization API harmonizer — it never measures sim-real correlation nor gates deployment per factor, so it contrasts route-to-*randomize* against C1's route-to-*trust* |
| [[2601.16578\|Zero-Shot-MARL-CPM-Lab]] | a *coarse 3-tier realism ladder* (sim/grey-box digital twin/physical testbed), not a per-perturbation-factor correlation — decomposes degradation into *environment shift* vs *control-architecture mismatch*, with agent-agent collision rate as the consistent indicator | collision rate 0.37→2.10→4.49 events/100 m across the three tiers; twin-to-physical avg speed drop 0.771→0.728 m/s; the mid-level MPC layer, not the policy, explains most of the lane-keeping degradation | a *source*-level decomposition (which layer causes the drop) rather than C1's *factor*-level one (which perturbation causes it) — a different granularity on the same diagnosis-not-yet-routed problem, and the connected-vehicle domain is off-axis from this doc's manipulation/locomotion substrate, but the finding that the *control-architecture* layer (not the policy) explains the gap directly parallels B1's H5 control-dynamics-misalignment residual, in a domain C1's table otherwise lacks |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (per-factor validity stress-test + routing gate beats one global r), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (diagnosis, MolmoSpaces frames the null) — Per-factor *correlation* falls below r<0.7 for the worst factors, even where global r is high.**
   - *Prediction*: re-measuring the *correlation* (not just SR sensitivity) under each [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]] factor applied to *both* sim and real, at least the worst factors (distractors, color, lighting) drop below 0.7 — i.e. [[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]]'s high *global* R=0.96 masks per-factor collapse that only a per-factor *correlation* re-measurement exposes.
   - *Test*: per-factor paired sim+real correlation re-measurement; report the per-factor r table and contrast it with MolmoSpaces's per-factor SR sensitivities.
   - *Row*: MolmoSpaces (high global R, per-factor SR) / SIMPLER (in-distribution r) / THE COLOSSEUM (per-factor harness).
   - *Falsifier*: per-factor *correlation* stays >0.7 under every factor (MolmoSpaces's robustness holds at the correlation level) → there is nothing to route around and the gate's input is empty (still publishable as a null).
2. **H2 — A per-(sim, factor) routing gate beats single-global-edge selection.**
   - *Prediction*: populating a trust map from H1 + [[2512.19562|REALM]]'s validated rows and routing each deployment query (task, factor) → trusted sim(s) lifts [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s win rate over its single-aggregate-edge baseline on shift-mixed deployment.
   - *Test*: feed per-factor weights vs one aggregate edge to the betting estimator; report win rate on multi-shift deployments.
   - *Row*: Sim2Real Betting (aggregate edge) / REALM (validated rows).
   - *Falsifier*: per-factor routing ties the global edge → the per-factor detail is not deployment-relevant.
3. **H3 — Under some perturbation, sim r goes *negative* (ranks policies backward).**
   - *Prediction*: following [[2502.10694|UDA-Simulation-Study]]'s negative-adaptation warning, there is at least one [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]] factor under which a high-r sim's correlation goes negative — the sim ranks policies *backward*, the strongest case for the gate.
   - *Test*: scan all 14 factors for sign-flips in the re-measured correlation.
   - *Row*: UDA Simulation Study (negative adaptation) / THE COLOSSEUM (factors).
   - *Falsifier*: r never goes negative → the failure is only degradation, not inversion, and routing matters less.
4. **H4 — Cross-sim transfer of r is the failure ROBOGATE already shows, generalized.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2603.22126|ROBOGATE]]'s 97.65 pp LIBERO→Isaac-Sim gap is not an outlier — re-measuring any sim's r on a *different* simulator's task distribution drops it sharply, so the gate must be per-(sim, factor, target-sim), not per-sim.
   - *Test*: measure r-transfer across simulator pairs; report the cross-sim r drop distribution.
   - *Row*: ROBOGATE (cross-sim failure) / PolaRiS (high-r evaluator).
   - *Falsifier*: r transfers across sims → cross-sim is not a separate axis and per-sim gating suffices.
5. **H5 — The gate halves real-trial spend by trusting only validated-per-factor sims.**
   - *Prediction*: routing real trials only to sim-factor cells the stress-test validated (and skipping do-not-trust cells) recovers [[2510.04354|SureSim]]'s 20–25% real-trial reduction *on top of* the portfolio, because untrusted-cell trials are wasted.
   - *Test*: compare real-trial budget for {gate-routed} vs {uniform} at matched CI coverage.
   - *Row*: REALM (validated rows) / Sim2Real Betting (router).
   - *Falsifier*: gating doesn't cut real-trial spend → untrusted-cell trials were not wasted and the gate adds no efficiency.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Per-cell correlation r is unestimable at REALM-class N** — a Pearson/Spearman r computed inside a single (sim, factor) cell at N~50 has a wide sampling distribution (its own SE is ≈ 1/√N ≈ 0.14), so a raw per-cell r<0.7 reading can be noise, and the "worst factors drop below 0.7" claim (H1) sits below the resolvable effect. → Do *not* route on raw per-cell r. Estimate the per-(sim, factor) trust map with a **shrinkage / partial-pooling (hierarchical) estimator** that borrows strength across factors and sims, and use the *rank-correlation* of pooled trust scores against realized transfer as the gate-metric; pre-register the MDE for "a factor's pooled r is distinguishable from the global r" *before* the per-factor correlation experiment, so the router acts on shrunk estimates whose uncertainty is quantified, not on noisy point estimates.
> - **A null result (r survives shift) is still informative** — but reframes the diagnosis half. → Pre-register the r<0.7 bet (H1); if r holds, "high-r sims are robust to shift X" is itself publishable, and the gate routes on the validated-robust cells.
> - **Routing on a stale gate mis-deploys the portfolio** — sims and factors drift, so a once-trusted cell can go bad. → Tie gate refresh to [[2509.15273|Embodied-Arena]]'s evolving-eval cadence; expire trust cells past a staleness window before they route a deployment.
