### C2 — Sim-to-Real as Provable Statistical Inference: Banks of Biased Simulators

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Reality-Gap Measurement as Inference |
| **Thesis** | That a portfolio of cheap biased sims beats one accurate sim — on provable confidence intervals and fewer real trials — is no longer the contrarian inversion: control-variates over biased auxiliary sources, with a provable variance bound and the fewer-real-samples result, is already published on robotics. What no one has characterized is *what makes the bank informative* — whether bias *diversity* tightens the bound more than bias *count*, and where on the compute-budget curve the portfolio wins. The field treats the portfolio result as the contribution. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] (benchmark), [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] (benchmark), [[2509.15273\|Embodied-Arena]] (benchmark), [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] (survey), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2603.13616\|N-SCORE]] (benchmark), [[2405.05439\|BC-Policy-Eval]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] 70–100% win rate over Monte Carlo from a *bank* of biased sims; [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] 20–25% real-trial reduction + 14.4% CI tightening (700 sims); finite-sample-valid CI coverage at equal compute vs a single accurate sim; [[2603.13616\|N-SCORE]] 70% fewer sim / 45% fewer hardware trials with anytime-valid Type-1 control (nonparametric metrics, 450+ real trials saved on RoboArena) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: real-performance estimation is variance reduction, and a bank of cheap biased sims beating a single accurate one is no longer the open question — it is published. The open question is *bank composition*: which property of the bank (diversity vs count) tightens the bound, and when the portfolio is worth it.
- **Today's answers**: [[2506.20553|Sim2Val]] (NVIDIA/Pavone) does control-variates over cheap abundant *biased* sources for variance-reduced *real-world* metric estimation, with a *provable variance bound* and the "significantly fewer real samples for a confidence bound" outcome, validated on quadruped robotics — exactly C2's headline; [[2507.20068|PERRY]] uses doubly-robust prediction-powered inference for valid CIs from biased simulator data; [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]] (70–100% win over Monte Carlo) and [[2510.04354|SureSim]] (20–25% fewer real trials, 14.4% CI tightening) are the betting/PPI instances; [[2206.05165|MFMCRL]]'s multifidelity control variates is the 2022 machinery predating all of them.
- **The opening**: *none* of these characterizes the bank — [[2502.10694|UDA-Simulation-Study]] only says no single method is universally best (so a bias is a signal to *weight*); whether bias *diversity* beats bias *count* and where the compute crossover sits are untouched, and C1's per-factor trust map is the natural selector for *informative* bias.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Estimating real performance is a statistics problem on a small number of real trials — what matters is how much the estimate jitters and whether its confidence interval is trustworthy, not how accurate any single sim is on its own. A biased sim is fine to use, as long as the bias carries useful signal and you correct for it. [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s 70–100% win from a *bank* of biased sims is the direct evidence that averaging-to-reduce-jitter beats squeezing-accuracy-from-one.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "the goal is one high-fidelity sim" — the statistical-inference camp ([[2506.20553|Sim2Val]], [[2507.20068|PERRY]], [[2510.04354|SureSim]], [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]], [[2206.05165|MFMCRL]]) already won that argument. The live assumption is that the portfolio *result* is the contribution, so nobody asks what *property of the bank* drives the bound. The unattacked claim: bias *diversity* (different physics approximations), not bias *count*, is the lever, and there is a compute crossover below which one accurate sim still wins.
- **The bet**: (i) Diversifying simulator bias tightens [[2510.04354|SureSim]]'s PPI bound / widens [[2506.20553|Sim2Val]]'s variance reduction **more than adding copies of the same biased sim** at matched bank size — a measurable CI-width gap Δ from diversity at fixed count. (ii) There is a compute-budget **crossover** below which one accurate sim gives tighter CIs and above which the biased portfolio wins, which the experiment maps. Reproduce [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s **70–100% win** and [[2506.20553|Sim2Val]]'s fewer-real-samples result *only as the regime where diversity is present*. Falsifiable: if redundant copies tighten the bound as much as diverse bias (no Δ), or the portfolio wins/loses at every budget (no crossover), bank composition is not the lever.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how real performance is estimated* (portfolio of biased sims / PPI from imperfect sims / single high-fidelity / cross-sim TTA / multi-source platform / real-sample source) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How real performance is estimated | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] | sequential betting over a *biased-sim bank* (Cover/Kelly) | 70–100% win over Monte Carlo, tolerates bias w/ edge | the portfolio-estimator anchor — provable-CI characterization vs single-sim at equal compute is open |
| [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] | PPI + Waudby-Smith-Ramdas, finite-sample-valid CIs | 20–25% fewer real trials, 14.4% CI tightening (700 sims) | provable bounds from imperfect sims — bias-diversity vs count trade-off unmapped |
| [[2506.20553\|Sim2Val]] | *control variates* over cheap biased auxiliary sources, provable variance bound | variance-reduced real metric estimation, fewer real samples for a confidence bound (quadruped) | the topThreat — does C2's headline (biased portfolio, provable bound, fewer real trials) on robotics, but never sweeps bias-diversity vs count or maps the compute crossover |
| [[2606.05159\|X4Val]] | neural-surrogate *control variates* over *heterogeneous, non-paired* auxiliary sources (Sim2Val's successor) | CI tightening 15–38.4% on autonomous-driving + robot manipulation (ManiSkill non-paired sim) | the closest substrate to the bias-*diversity* lever (naturally diverse-bias, no sample pairing) and the baseline the diversity-vs-count sweep must beat — but does not itself sweep diversity-vs-count or map the compute crossover |
| [[2507.20068\|PERRY]] | doubly-robust prediction-powered inference from biased auxiliary data | valid CIs from biased simulator data | DR-PPI confidence intervals from biased sims — confirms the headline, leaves bank composition unexamined |
| [[2206.05165\|MFMCRL]] | *multifidelity* RL with control variates (the 2022 machinery root) | variance reduction via multifidelity control variates | predates Sim2Val/SureSim — proves the estimator class, but RL-internal, no bank-composition or crossover map |
| [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] | (no method) names no universally-best estimator | context-dependent effectiveness | the rationale for portfolios over single estimators |
| [[2604.10856\|BridgeSim]] | cross-simulator CL evaluation + TTA | +19.1 DS | multi-sim evaluation infrastructure, not a provable-CI estimator |
| [[2509.15273\|Embodied-Arena]] | unified evolving multi-benchmark eval | 22+ benchmarks / 30+ models | the multi-source substrate a portfolio extends, not an estimator |
| [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] | real fleet (10 robots, Table30) | 30 tasks, contact/soft-body splits | the scarce real-sample source PPI/betting pair with |
| [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] | (no method) world models as evaluators | survey | the inference-over-fidelity rationale |
| [[2512.19562\|REALM]] | real-to-sim-validated paired real/sim outcomes | r=0.92 overall | supplies the paired outcomes the estimators consume |
| [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] | Pearson r as a single-sim ranking proxy | r>0.85, low MMRV | the single-sim baseline the portfolio must beat at equal compute |
| [[2603.13616\|N-SCORE]] | nonparametric *sequential* policy comparison: supermartingale evidence integrator with online-tuned scaling, anytime-valid Type-1 control | 70% fewer sim / 45% fewer hardware trials (discrete partial-credit), 20% sample-complexity gain over WSR, 450+ real trials saved on RoboArena | the robotics-grounded anytime-valid *estimator core* the CI-width comparisons run on — extends Sim2Real Betting's fewer-real-trials result from binary success to general nonparametric metrics, but evaluates *one* sim/hardware channel: never composes a *bank* of biased sims or sweeps bias-diversity vs count |
| [[2505.18000\|Anytime-Valid-PPI]] | PPI extended to *anytime-valid* confidence sequences (AsympCS) with Bayes-assisted, bias-quality-aware rectifier weighting | smaller confidence regions than classical inference with cumulative miscoverage held below α; heavier-tailed priors stay robust as prediction bias grows | the sequential, bias-aware PPI engine sharper than the cited [[2507.20068\|PERRY]]/[[2510.04354\|SureSim]] rows (those are fixed-time) — its prior-on-bias-quality is the natural lever for *informative*-bias weighting (H2/H4) and H3's adaptive real-trial routing, but it never instantiates a robotics sim bank or maps the diversity-vs-count Δ |
| [[2603.17925\|SPRUCE]] | UCB-style **across-arm** sample allocation maximizing Kelly wealth-growth against a *global null* (e-processes as convex combinations of P-e-values), with matching lower/upper bounds on **log-optimality** (Thm 3.7, oracle growth without knowing the best arm) *and* **expected rejection time** (Thm 4.2 as α→0) | multi-armed Q-log-optimality (asymptotic growth matches the best arm/portfolio); portfolio-oracle allocation regret O(Σ_a log(n)/Δ_a,Q) (Thm 5.3) | the missing **across-arm allocation** theory H3 lacks — [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] is a *single-policy* Kelly estimator reweighting simulators *within* the bank to estimate one policy's SR, [[2603.13616\|N-SCORE]] evaluates *one* sim/hardware channel, [[2505.18000\|Anytime-Valid-PPI]] is a fixed-bank rectifier — *none* provides across-arm/across-policy sample-allocation with optimality guarantees; SPRUCE turns H3 from a hand-waved "use Kelly weights" into a falsifier-grade adaptive-vs-uniform allocation experiment with provable oracle-matching budget routing; a general-statistics method, so it strengthens H3's *theory* rather than replacing a robotics row (add, not mismatch) |
| [[2510.16166\|Conformal-PPI]] | PPI via conformal set-imputation, inheriting validity under *continuous distribution shift* (mean / Z- / M-estimation / e-values) | tightest CIs vs PPI++ and FAB on mean estimation; prediction-powered e-values reject false nulls faster under time-series shift | the shift-robust PPI variant the in-distribution [[2507.20068\|PERRY]]/[[2510.04354\|SureSim]]/[[2505.18000\|Anytime-Valid-PPI]] rows leave open — keeps the provable bound valid when the bank's biased sims drift (H4 shift-mixed deployment), but offers no bank-composition or compute-crossover characterization |
| [[2503.05696\|MFPG]] | *multi-fidelity control variates* for policy-gradient estimation: cheap low-fidelity sims as a control variate, validated under dynamics mismatch + reward misspecification | unbiased variance-reduced gradients, beats high-fidelity-only in 8/8 mild-gap scenarios, holds even with anti-correlated low-fidelity rewards | the training-side descendant of [[2206.05165\|MFMCRL]] showing the same control-variate lever (C2's mechanism) survives *biased* low-fidelity sources — but reduces RL-internal gradient variance, not real-performance *evaluation* CIs, and never sweeps bank diversity-vs-count |
| [[2405.05439\|BC-Policy-Eval]] | *real-rollout-only* estimation: finite-sample-valid CI bounds on policy SR from *minimal* real rollouts — UMA (binary) / tighter DKW (continuous CDF) / MES (tightness), computing the minimum real rollouts for a target confidence+tightness | pouring SR lower-bound 3.5% (harmful OOD) vs 64.1% (benign) at 95%; RT-2 vs VC-1 declared with disjoint 95% CIs | the rigorous real-rollout-only estimator *baseline* the bank rows extend by *adding biased sim sources* to cut that real-trial count — sits beside [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]]'s single-sim *proxy* baseline but on the real-rollout side, and is the real-trial-count reference point for H1 (portfolio vs single) and H5's compute crossover |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a biased-sim portfolio gives tighter provable CIs than a single accurate sim at equal compute), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (confirmatory — Sim2Val ran it) — Portfolio beats single-accurate-sim CI width at equal compute.**
   - *Prediction*: replicating [[2506.20553|Sim2Val]]'s control-variate result under [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s estimator, a bank of cheap biased sims yields tighter CIs at equal coverage than one high-fidelity sim at matched compute — confirming the now-consensus headline as the baseline H2/H5 build on.
   - *Test*: equal-compute allocation; compare CI width + coverage on held-out real SR for {biased bank} vs {single accurate sim}.
   - *Row*: Sim2Val (control-variate portfolio) / Sim2Real Betting (betting estimator) vs SIMPLER (single-sim proxy).
   - *Falsifier*: the single accurate sim matches the portfolio's CI at equal compute → even the conceded headline fails to reproduce and the direction collapses.
2. **H2 — Bias-diversity tightens the bound more than bias-count.**
   - *Prediction*: diversifying simulator bias (different physics approximations) tightens [[2510.04354|SureSim]]'s PPI bound more than adding copies of the same biased sim — diversity, not count, is the lever.
   - *Test*: sweep {bias-diverse bank} vs {redundant copies} at matched count; report CI width — using [[2606.05159|X4Val]] as the baseline that already demonstrates CI tightening (15–38.4%) specifically from heterogeneous (diverse) auxiliary sources, the right anchor for the diversity claim and the baseline the controlled diversity-vs-count sweep must beat (X4Val itself never sweeps diversity-vs-count).
   - *Row*: SureSim (PPI bound) / X4Val (heterogeneous-source CI tightening).
   - *Falsifier*: redundant copies tighten the bound as much → diversity is not the value and count suffices.
3. **H3 — Adaptive Kelly allocation beats uniform real-trial spending — with provable oracle-matching routing.**
   - *Prediction*: using [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]]'s Kelly weights to route real trials to policies where the bank is least certain beats uniform real-trial spending (link to C1's per-factor trust map); [[2603.17925|SPRUCE]] supplies the missing *across-arm* allocation machinery — a UCB-style allocator with matching log-optimality + rejection-time bounds — so the adaptive arm is not a heuristic but an oracle-matching budget router, making the adaptive-vs-uniform comparison falsifier-grade.
   - *Test*: adaptive ([[2603.17925|SPRUCE]] across-arm Kelly allocation) vs uniform real-trial allocation at matched budget; report CI width and expected-rejection-time on the uncertain policies against SPRUCE's theoretical lower bound.
   - *Row*: Sim2Real Betting (within-bank Kelly estimator) / SPRUCE (across-arm Kelly allocation with optimality guarantees).
   - *Falsifier*: uniform allocation matches adaptive → routing real trials by bank-uncertainty adds nothing; or SPRUCE's across-arm allocator gives no edge over Sim2Real Betting's within-bank reweighting → the across-arm structure is inert.
4. **H4 — Informative bias beats adversarial bias; C1's gate selects it.**
   - *Prediction*: a bank of uniformly-wrong sims gives no edge, so the portfolio's gain depends on *informative* bias — using C1's per-factor trust map to select sims with informative bias (and exclude those failing under the relevant shift) recovers more of the 70–100% win than an unfiltered bank.
   - *Test*: {C1-gated bank} vs {unfiltered bank} on shift-mixed deployment; report win rate.
   - *Row*: Sim2Real Betting (portfolio) / UDA Simulation Study (no universally-best).
   - *Falsifier*: the unfiltered bank matches the gated one → bias informativeness doesn't matter and any bank works.
5. **H5 — The portfolio wins only past a compute-budget crossover.**
   - *Prediction*: there is a compute-budget crossover below which one accurate sim wins and above which the biased portfolio wins — mapping it tells you when to use which.
   - *Test*: sweep total compute; report the portfolio-vs-single CI-width crossover.
   - *Row*: Sim2Real Betting (portfolio) vs SIMPLER (single-sim).
   - *Falsifier*: the portfolio wins (or loses) at every budget → there is no crossover and the choice is unconditional.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **PPI/betting need a few paired real outcomes** — provable bounds still require some real data. → Pair with [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]]'s remote fleet to keep the real-sample cost minimal while preserving validity.
> - **Bias must be informative, not adversarial** — a bank of uniformly-wrong sims gives no edge. → Use C1's per-factor trust map to *select* sims with informative bias and exclude those that fail under the relevant shift (H4).
> - **Portfolio overhead** — managing many sims adds engineering cost. → Quantify the compute-allocation frontier (H5) so the portfolio is only used where it provably beats the single-sim baseline.

### C3 — Cross-Unit Exchangeability: The Real Robot Is a Population, Not a Point

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Reality-Gap Measurement as Inference |
| **Thesis** | Every provable bound this cluster builds — Pearson $r$, PPI intervals, conformal FPR guarantees — is computed against a single physical unit's trajectory distribution and then silently generalized to "the robot." Manufacturing tolerance, gearbox backlash, and per-joint calibration drift give each unit its own distribution, and no cited method indexes anything by unit. The field treats the real robot as one calibratable point. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2506.18123\|RoboArena]] (benchmark), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2512.19562\|REALM]] (benchmark), [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] (benchmark), [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] (benchmark), [[2502.00893\|ToddlerBot]] (method), [[2605.00416\|LWD]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | The Duckietown reproducibility study's inter-robot numbers (MPD std 3.4 cm / MOD std 5.2° across 3 units vs 1.3 cm / 2.8° same-unit repeatability — a 2.6×/1.9× SD inflation; arXiv:2009.04362, not yet in KH) as the only measured real-world anchor that between-unit variance is non-zero; [[2506.18123\|RoboArena]]'s 4,284 rollouts pooled across 7 institutions' physical DROID units with zero unit terms in its θ/τ/ν/ψ ranking model; [[2512.19562\|REALM]]'s r=0.92/0.88 and [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]]'s r>0.85/0.890, each fit to one canonical unit per platform; [[2602.11337\|MolmoSpaces]]'s R≈0.96/ρ≈0.98 correlating a single-unit sim against RoboArena's institution-pooled 752 real episodes; [[2502.00893\|ToddlerBot]]'s 90%=90% unit-A→unit-B point estimate at N=20 (SE≈6.7 pp); the pre-registered decision threshold this direction proposes — ICC (intraclass correlation attributable to unit identity) > 0.10 — as the bar for "non-trivial" between-unit variance |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: every gap-object this doc defines — $\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}$, $\text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}}$, $\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$, Pearson $r$, MMRV, and every conformal FPR bound in Clusters C and E — is a property of a single, implicitly homogeneous $p_{\text{real}}(\tau\mid\pi)$. No cited benchmark indexes any of these quantities by physical unit; manufacturing tolerance, gearbox backlash, and per-joint calibration drift get no index anywhere in this cluster's inference machinery.
- **Today's answers**: [[2506.18123|RoboArena]] pools 4,284 rollouts across 7 institutions' physical DROID arms into one global ranking via a task-aware Bradley-Terry model (θ ability + τ task-difficulty + ν bucket-prior + ψ policy-task offset) — and its own Introduction names "manufacturing differences between said robots" as an obstacle to scaling, then ships a model with zero terms for it. [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]] runs a literal 10-machine, 4-type real fleet and catalogues tester bias and lighting/camera-intrinsics drift as "intended perturbations" a robust VLA should absorb — but never extends that framing to the inter-unit hardware variance the same fleet could measure. [[2512.19562|REALM]] and [[2405.05941|SIMPLER]] each fit their sysID to one canonical unit per platform and report the resulting $r$ as if it characterized the platform, not the unit.
- **The opening**: [[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]] makes the mismatch concrete in a single number — its sim is system-identified to one Franka FR3, then correlated (R≈0.96) against 752 real episodes pooled across [[2506.18123|RoboArena]]'s multiple institutions' units. [[2502.00893|ToddlerBot]] is the one paper that runs a direct cross-unit transfer test (90% SR on unit A matches 90% on an independently-built unit B) after an explicit per-unit calibration protocol — the only existence proof in this evidence base that the effect is even measurable, and possibly controllable. The Duckietown reproducibility study measured a real, non-zero inter-robot variance (MPD std 3.4 cm vs 1.3 cm same-robot) six years ago on a different platform class; nobody has re-run that methodology on the manipulation/VLA literature this cluster surveys.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: This doc's own Formal Framing defines $\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}$, $\text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}}$, and $\text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ as functionals of a single $p_{\text{real}}(\tau\mid\pi)$. Physically, "the real robot" is $N$ distinct manufactured units, each inducing its own trajectory distribution $p_{\text{real}}^{(i)}(\tau\mid\pi)$, so every $p_{\text{real}}(\tau\mid\pi)$ this doc's cited benchmarks actually estimate is an unindexed mixture $\sum_i w_i\, p_{\text{real}}^{(i)}(\tau\mid\pi)$ over whichever units happened to generate the trials — a fact about how the data was collected, not a modeling choice. This does not break the doc's own bound: $|\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}| \geq \text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}} + \text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ still holds qualitatively per unit; it means every term currently reported as one number needs a unit index it does not have. [[2506.18123|RoboArena]], [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]], [[2512.19562|REALM]], and [[2405.05941|SIMPLER]] are the direct evidence the index is absent; [[2602.11337|MolmoSpaces]]'s own correlation is the sharpest single number where the mixture becomes visible.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Every method in C1/C2 and every benchmark in this doc's own Survey Landscape treats a single canonical sysID fit as sufficient — not a decided sufficiency, a tooling artifact: simulators are parameterized from one URDF per product line, sysID pipelines fit one physical unit's trajectories, because that is what the tooling gives you, not because anyone argued a single fit generalizes. [[2506.18123|RoboArena]]'s authors are the sharpest case: they name "manufacturing differences between said robots" in their own Introduction, then their task-aware Bradley-Terry model's four parameter families (θ, τ, ν, ψ) carry no unit or institution term despite pooling 4,284 rollouts across 7 institutions, and their own Limitations section (cross-embodiment, controlled experimentation, adversarial evaluators, Goodhart's law) never revisits it. Even this doc's own most careful exchangeability-adjacent reasoning doesn't reach it: E3's [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] bound-preservation proof (its own Assumption 3) treats *policy-induced* distribution shift as the only threat to conformal validity, and E2's [[2409.19190|RAIL]] computes forward occupancy from the robot's URDF-declared geometry with no per-unit uncertainty term — neither considers "calibrated on unit A, deployed on unit B" as a second, independent way the same guarantee goes void.
- **The bet**: Decomposing outcome variance (sim-real $r$, empirical collision rate, or SR) across **3 or more** physical units of the nominally same platform — three Franka Panda / DROID arms, say — under matched task, environment, and policy conditions will show a between-unit variance component with intraclass correlation **ICC > 0.10** (a pre-registered bar for "non-trivial," chosen independently of any single platform's own number) — not the ≈0 the field's pooled, unit-blind estimators ([[2506.18123|RoboArena]]'s θ/τ/ν/ψ, [[2512.19562|REALM]]'s and [[2405.05941|SIMPLER]]'s single-unit sysID fits) implicitly assume. The Duckietown reproducibility study's own measured inter-robot effect (arXiv:2009.04362) — MPD std **3.4 cm** vs **1.3 cm** same-robot, a **2.6×** SD inflation, on a different, low-cost mobile platform — is the only existing real-world number showing this kind of variance is not zero *somewhere*; nobody has re-measured it on precision-manufactured manipulation hardware. A second, independent falsifier: a conformal detector ([[2506.09937|SAFE]]/[[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]-style) calibrated on unit A's successful rollouts shows empirically degraded coverage on unit B with no domain, task, or policy shift involved. Falsifiable: if the between-unit ICC is indistinguishable from 0 at adequate power, or a conformal detector's coverage transfers cleanly A→B, the population structure is negligible and the field's point-estimate convention is vindicated.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the paper treats "the real robot's" unit identity* (single canonical unit / pooled fleet with no unit index / names the confound but doesn't model it / measures inter-unit variance directly / tests cross-unit transfer without a variance decomposition) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How "the real robot's" unit identity is treated | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2506.18123\|RoboArena]] | names "manufacturing differences between said robots" in its own Introduction; pools 4,284 rollouts across 7 institutions' DROID units into one task-aware Bradley-Terry ranking (θ ability + τ task-difficulty + ν bucket-prior + ψ policy-task offset) — zero unit or institution term | r=0.98 vs oracle, MMRV=1.8%, converges in ~100 comparisons | names the confound in its own prose, then ships a statistical model with no term for it — its Limitations section (cross-embodiment, controlled experimentation, adversarial evaluators, Goodhart's law) never revisits it |
| [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] | operates a literal fleet of 10 machines across 4 robot types; §3 catalogues tester bias and lighting/camera-intrinsics drift as "intended perturbations" a robust VLA should generalize over | Table30, 30-task real suite, soft-body split 8% SR / 27% progress | never extends the "intended perturbation" framing to inter-unit hardware variance, despite running the one fleet in this evidence base that could measure it directly |
| [[2512.19562\|REALM]] | system-identifies its high-fidelity sim against "the DROID robot platform," singular | r=0.92 overall / 0.88 default, 15 perturbation factors | one calibration fit stands in for every physical DROID unit the correlation is later asserted to hold for |
| [[2405.05941\|SIMPLER]] | fine-tunes controller parameters via sysID against the Google Robot and the WidowX/BridgeData arm, one fit each | r>0.85 (Google Robot) / 0.890 (BridgeData) | the canonical single-unit $r$ this whole cluster (C1, C2) treats as ground truth is itself a one-unit measurement |
| [[2602.11337\|MolmoSpaces]] | sim side system-identified against one Franka FR3; real side validated against 752 real pick-task episodes drawn from RoboArena's institution-pooled fleet | R≈0.96, ρ≈0.98 | correlates a single-point sim against a population-pooled real distribution — the point-vs-population mismatch this direction targets, made concrete in one number |
| [[2502.00893\|ToddlerBot]] | the one paper that runs a direct cross-unit transfer test — a manipulation policy trained on unit A evaluated on an independently-built unit B — after custom zero-point calibration jigs + per-motor SysID validated "between robot instances" | 90% SR on unit A matches 90% SR on unit B, N=20 trials each (SE≈6.7 pp) | a single A→B pair at N=20 is a point estimate of one unit-pair's gap, not a population variance decomposition across ≥3 units, and the match may reflect engineered per-unit calibration rather than a small population variance to begin with (H5) |
| [[2605.00416\|LWD]] | pools "heterogeneous" experience from a 16-robot dual-arm fleet into one Distributional Implicit Value Learning critic with no unit index; "heterogeneous" is defined as task/environment diversity | 0.95 avg SR across 8 tasks | the freshest (2026), largest-fleet instance of the same omission — heterogeneity is modeled on every axis except which physical arm produced the data |
| Duckietown reproducibility study (arXiv:2009.04362, not yet in KH) | the only paper in this evidence base that directly measures inter-unit variance, on a different (low-cost mobile) platform class, with no per-unit calibration protocol reported | inter-robot MPD std 3.4 cm / MOD std 5.2° across 3 Duckiebots vs 1.3 cm / 2.8° same-robot repeatability (2.6×/1.9× SD inflation); 2.5 cm / 3.9° across-lab | never re-applied to the manipulation/VLA literature this cluster surveys; the only real-world anchor that "this variance is not zero" is six years old and off-domain |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (the real robot is a population of physical units with a measurable between-unit variance component), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (core) — Between-unit variance decomposition on ≥3 physical units.**
   - *Prediction*: measuring a fixed policy's outcome (sim-real $r$, SR, or a continuous trajectory metric) on 3 or more physically distinct units of the nominally-same platform, under matched task/environment/operator conditions, shows a between-unit variance component with ICC > 0.10 — not the ≈0 the field's pooled estimators assume.
   - *Test*: run identical rollouts of one fixed policy on N≥3 physically distinct units of the same platform (same task, same environment, same protocol); fit a random-effects model decomposing total outcome variance into within-unit (repeat-trial) and between-unit components; report ICC with a bootstrap CI.
   - *Row*: RoboArena (pools institutions/units into one ranking) / RoboChallenge (10-machine fleet) / Duckietown reproducibility study (the precedent methodology, non-KH).
   - *Falsifier*: ICC is statistically indistinguishable from 0 at the pre-registered power level → unit identity contributes negligible variance and pooling across units is harmless.
2. **H2 — Conformal coverage does not transfer across units.**
   - *Prediction*: a conformal failure detector ([[2506.09937|SAFE]]/[[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]-style success-only calibration, per E3) calibrated on unit A's successful rollouts shows empirical FPR measurably above its nominal target when deployed unchanged on unit B, with task, environment, and policy held fixed.
   - *Test*: calibrate the conformal threshold on unit A only; measure empirical FPR on unit A (should match nominal) and on unit B (unchanged threshold, no domain/task/policy shift); report the coverage gap and check it against the Lipschitz-continuity regime [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]]'s bound-preservation proof assumes (its own Assumption 3, currently checked only against policy-induced shift, never unit-identity shift).
   - *Row*: SAFE / FAIL-Detect (E3's success-only conformal detectors) / Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF (E3's bound-preservation theorem) / RoboArena (multi-unit real substrate).
   - *Falsifier*: empirical FPR on unit B matches unit A within sampling noise → the conformal guarantee transfers across units and cross-unit exchangeability holds in practice.
3. **H3 — B1's fidelity→r law changes when re-fit per-unit vs pooled.**
   - *Prediction*: re-fitting B1's reconstruction-fidelity→forward-$r$ regression separately per physical unit yields per-unit slopes that differ by more than sampling noise predicts — the "law" B1's own H1 bets on is, structurally, a within-unit slope wearing a within-object label.
   - *Test*: for a fixed object class, gather reconstruction-fidelity/forward-$r$ pairs from ≥2 distinct physical units (reusing B1's within-object controlled-degradation design, its H1); fit the regression per unit and pooled; test the unit×fidelity interaction term.
   - *Row*: Real-to-Sim GS (B1's joint-inversion anchor) / REALM (single-unit sysID) — cross-referenced to B1's H1.
   - *Falsifier*: the fidelity→$r$ slope is statistically identical across units → the law is unit-invariant and B1's within-object framing already captures everything relevant.
4. **H4 — RoboArena's ranking model, re-analyzed with a unit term (the cheapest test).**
   - *Prediction*: adding a per-unit (or per-institution, if unit-level IDs are unavailable) random-effect term to RoboArena's task-aware Bradley-Terry model changes at least one pairwise policy ranking relative to the published unit-blind model, on the same released dataset.
   - *Test*: a re-analysis, not new data collection, contingent on RoboArena's released episode logs carrying evaluator/institution identity — the distributed double-blind protocol implies each session is tied to a specific evaluator, but robot-unit-level granularity within an institution is unconfirmed and may require a collaboration ask; refit θ/τ/ν/ψ plus a unit or institution offset on the existing 4,284-episode dataset; compare rankings and MMRV with vs without the term.
   - *Row*: RoboArena (task-aware BT model — the cheapest test in this card since it needs no new hardware).
   - *Falsifier*: adding the term leaves rankings and MMRV unchanged within noise → the omission, while real, is not consequential at RoboArena's current scale.
5. **H5 — Calibration protocol, not raw manufacturing cost, moderates the between-unit variance.**
   - *Prediction*: platforms with an explicit per-unit calibration protocol at build time ([[2502.00893|ToddlerBot]]'s zero-point calibration jigs + per-motor SysID test bed, whose transferability across instances the paper validates) show a smaller between-unit variance component than platforms without one (the Duckietown reproducibility study, which reports no equivalent per-unit calibration step) — even though ToddlerBot is the cheaper, 3D-printed platform that would be expected to have looser tolerance by raw cost alone.
   - *Test*: compare the between-unit/within-unit variance ratio (from H1's decomposition) on a platform with an explicit calibration protocol vs one without; treat calibration-protocol presence as the primary moderator under test and raw manufacturing cost as a confound to control for.
   - *Row*: ToddlerBot (engineered per-unit calibration, 90%=90% transfer) vs Duckietown reproducibility study (unmitigated inter-robot variance, 2.6× SD inflation).
   - *Falsifier*: ToddlerBot-class engineered calibration shows the same or larger variance ratio as Duckietown's unmitigated case → calibration effort is not the moderator, and the field's silence on unit identity is not fixable by better calibration alone.
6. **H6 — Unit-conditioning a fleet-RL critic changes per-unit outcome variance.**
   - *Prediction*: adding a per-unit conditioning term to an [[2605.00416|LWD]]-style Distributional Implicit Value Learning critic reduces per-unit SR variance across the fleet relative to the unit-blind pooled critic, with the largest gains on the fleet's outlier units.
   - *Test*: on a multi-robot RL fleet (LWD-style), compare a unit-blind value function to one conditioned on unit ID or a learned per-unit embedding; report per-unit SR variance and aggregate SR for both.
   - *Row*: LWD (fleet-scale RL, unit-blind baseline).
   - *Falsifier*: unit-conditioning shows no measurable change in per-unit SR variance or aggregate SR → the pooled critic already absorbs whatever unit-level signal exists, and the omission is harmless in this regime.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **H4's cheapest test depends on metadata RoboArena may not have released at unit granularity** — the paper's own dataset release doesn't confirm per-episode robot-unit IDs, only evaluator/institution association is structurally implied by the distributed protocol. → Treat H4 as a collaboration ask to the RoboArena team for unit metadata if institution-level re-analysis proves insufficient; run H1's direct hardware test in parallel rather than blocking the whole direction on data access.
> - **A null result at H1 could be an underpowered null, not evidence of absence** — REALM/SIMPLER-class benchmarks already operate at N~50 per condition, where C1's own risk analysis shows per-cell estimates carry SE≈7 pp; an ICC test with too few units or too few trials per unit will fail to detect a real but modest effect. → Pre-register a minimum-detectable-effect power analysis for the ICC>0.10 threshold before H1 runs, following C1's precedent for handling small-N noise.
> - **If confirmed, the fix is a doc-wide retrofit this direction alone cannot make** — RoboArena's ranking model, REALM's/SIMPLER's sysID pipelines, and every conformal detector in E3 would each need a unit term, and C2's PPI/betting machinery would need its exchangeability precondition re-examined (its own note describes only "a small dataset of paired real-world/simulation outcomes," with no statement of which physical unit produced them — an unstated, unverified precondition, not a claimed one). → Treat a positive H1/H2 result as an update to C2's and E3's own Risk callouts and B1's Key-targets scope, not a standalone footnote; H5 exists to bound where the retrofit is actually worth the engineering cost before committing broadly.
> - **The effect may be real but small on precision-manufactured hardware specifically** — Duckietown's 2.6× SD inflation is measured on a low-cost mobile robot; Franka Panda/DROID-class arms are manufactured to far tighter tolerance, so the between-unit component could be negligible on exactly the platforms this literature's provable bounds target. → H5 is designed to bound this directly; if the Franka/DROID-class ratio comes back near 1×, scope the bet's practical urgency to lower-cost/higher-volume platforms (humanoids, mobile manipulators) rather than the precision arms most benchmarks currently use.

---

## Cluster D — Deployment-Time Adaptation: Closing the Residual Gap Online

*Close the residual $\delta(t)$ at deploy-time — a time-varying disturbance observable only on hardware, that survives every train-, reconstruct-, and measure-time fix. The three directions split by which model is on hand: privileged extrinsics, an analytical model, or only a learned one.*

### D1 — Latent-Extrinsics Online Adaptation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Deployment-Time Adaptation |
| **Thesis** | Inferring a deployment latent from proprioceptive history and conditioning on it is now a built architecture — and the latest version, facing the question of what happens *outside* the training range, *gives up* and falls back to a robust feature. The contrarian bet is the inverse: inference *extends accurately* past the randomization range, so the gain concentrates exactly where robustification stalls. The first principle: the true dynamics are a deploy-time latent revealed only by on-robot history. The field assumes inference becomes unreliable OOD and you should robustify there instead. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2107.04034\|RMA]] (method), [[2412.04323\|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]] (method), [[2409.16578\|FLaRe]] (method), [[2606.02280\|LDG]] (method), [[2212.07740\|TERT]] (method), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2412.18781\|Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation-Eval]] (benchmark), [[2508.20294\|DALI]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2107.04034\|RMA]] zero-real-fine-tune adaptation across sand/mud/rocky/slippery + 12 kg payload (100% of body weight); [[2409.16578\|FLaRe]] real Stretch RE-1 SR 50%→80.7% (+30.7%), +23.6% sim, 72% LoCoBot embodiment transfer (6 h new-behavior); latent-extrinsics inference at deploy-time where fixed-DR plateaus, with the dynamics-OOD-severity axis anchored on [[2412.18781\|Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation-Eval]] (graded actuator-failure + friction-variation + adversarial-action perturbation protocol on MuJoCo legged control, 3 offline × 3 online RL algorithms) and the *distance-past-the-range* instrumentation on [[2508.20294\|DALI]]'s Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed regimes over mass/friction/gravity/actuator-strength context (extrapolation gains up to +96.4% over context-unaware Dreamer-DR, +33.8–63.9% over GT context-aware baselines — sim-only, the extrapolation-regime benchmark for the H2 envelope / H3 front-line bet) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a fixed domain-randomized policy is one bet placed at train-time — pick a range, hope deployment falls inside it — but the dynamics revealed on hardware don't exist until deployment, so no train-time randomization observes them.
- **Today's answers**: [[2107.04034|RMA]] trains a base policy on a privileged "extrinsics vector," then an adaptation module that *infers* it online from proprioceptive history alone (10 Hz, base policy at 100 Hz) — walking on sand/mud/rocky/slippery with a 12 kg payload (100% of body weight), *no* real fine-tuning; [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] makes the manipulation case — large-scale RL fine-tuning lifts a pre-trained BC policy from 50% real Stretch RE-1 SR to 80.7% (+30.7%) and transfers to LoCoBot (72% ObjectNav) by action-masking.
- **The opening**: D1's surviving edge is the *inverse* of the now-litigated boundary — that continued inference *beats* robust-fallback *outside* the randomization range. [[2412.04323|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]] builds the same proprioception-history latent-context architecture and answers the boundary question on a real quadruped but *concedes* inference becomes unreliable OOD and falls back to a robust latent; the latent family has the headroom to overturn that — [[2606.02280|LDG]] makes the context *outcome-centric* (robust to unmodeled / time-varying / disabled-actuator shifts that break parameter-centric methods) and [[2212.07740|TERT]] skips it entirely (history→action, 100% sand / 60% stairs where the RMA/TCN baseline scores 0%). On manipulation, [[2210.04887|In-Hand-RMA]] and [[2011.11270|COCOI]] already close the contact residual *with no real reward* — a baseline to beat, not an open target.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The deployment environment's true dynamics are a latent variable revealed only by the robot's own proprioceptive history — no train-time randomization observes them, because they don't exist until deployment. The estimable quantity is the *posterior over extrinsics given on-robot history*, a deploy-time object by construction. [[2107.04034|RMA]]'s zero-fine-tune adaptation across radically different terrains is the proof that inferring the latent online beats marginalizing over it at train-time.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "infer-then-condition beats robustify" inside the range — that is settled ([[2107.04034|RMA]], [[2409.16578|FLaRe]]). The live assumption is [[2412.04323|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]]'s: that inference becomes *unreliable* outside the randomization range, so you should give up and fall back to a robust latent there. D1 bets the inverse — that the latent-extrinsics estimate *extends accurately* some measurable distance past the range, so continued inference beats robust-fallback exactly where GRAM hands off. [[2606.02280|LDG]] supports the headroom (outcome-centric latents survive shifts no parameterization represents).
- **The bet**: Outside the train-time randomization range, a proprioception-only latent-extrinsics estimator **beats a [[2412.04323|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]]-style robust-fallback** head-to-head — measured as the *absolute closed-loop SR-vs-distance curves* for continued-inference-only vs robust-fallback on the same task, with the **crossover located** (the distance past the range where one curve overtakes the other), not a single asserted "gap grows with distance." GRAM's reported 0.79→0.58 sim decay and 100% far-OOD *hardware* SR are absolute returns with no baseline OOD curve subtracted, so the margin (inference-minus-fallback) must be *measured*, and the experiment is publishable either way — locating a crossover, or confirming fallback dominates. Reproduce [[2107.04034|RMA]]'s zero-real-fine-tune adaptation (sand/mud/12 kg) and [[2409.16578|FLaRe]]'s 50%→80.7% (+30.7%) *as the in-range baseline*. Caveat (instrument validity): a single physical payload-sweep on one quadruped confounds payload mass, CoM shift, and rig friction, and GRAM/LoadAdapt already show payload is the *easy* axis where fallback and inference both near 100% — so H3 must be run on a *harder* axis (friction/actuator) where fallback is conservative-but-suboptimal, after a pilot confirms the chosen axis can be swept far enough to *reach* a crossover regime at all. Falsifiable: if GRAM's robust-fallback matches or beats continued inference across the swept distance (no crossover in favour of inference), GRAM's give-up-OOD conclusion holds and the inverse bet fails.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the deployment latent is obtained* (privileged-extrinsics inference / RL fine-tune / outcome-centric latent / history-to-action direct / world-model supervision / parameter-level estimation) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How the deployment latent is obtained | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2107.04034\|RMA]] | proprioception-only inference of a *privileged extrinsics vector* (10 Hz) | zero real fine-tune across sand/mud/rocky/slippery + 12 kg | locomotion-shown; never tests whether inference *extends past* the DR range vs robust-fallback — the GRAM boundary question |
| [[2507.07825\|LoadAdapt]] | proprioception-only *Load Characteristics Estimator* (mass/position/velocity/friction) via teacher-student + privileged-encoder distillation — the *dynamic-load CoM-shift* extrinsics instance | zero-shot real Unitree Go2 across 4 kg / 6 kg lead balls on rough terrain, near an Oracle with direct load info, beating robustness baselines | the cleanest real-quadruped *adaptation-beats-robustness* contrast on a distinct shift type (dynamic-load CoM-shift, not terrain), substantiating H3 — but does not run the graded distance-past-the-range sweep, so it strengthens the flank rather than closing it |
| [[2409.16578\|FLaRe]] | large-scale RL fine-tuning of a pre-trained BC policy | 80.7% real (+30.7%), 79.5% sim, 72% LoCoBot (6 h) | needs RL fine-tuning + reward — heavier than proprioception-only inference |
| [[2606.02280\|LDG]] | *outcome-centric* latent dynamics context (contrastive VI) | robust to unmodeled / time-varying / disabled-actuator shifts | RMA's context from parameters → outcomes; the parameter-free generalization |
| [[2508.20294\|DALI]] | *outcome-centric* latent context from a forward-dynamics-loss encoder + cross-modal alignment to the world-model state (DreamerV3) | +96.4% over context-unaware Dreamer-DR; +33.8–63.9% over GT context-aware baselines in *extrapolation* | the extrapolation-regime *benchmark* that formalizes Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed over physical context — instruments the H2 distance-past-range envelope and substantiates H4's outcome-centric split, but sim-only (DMC/DreamerV3), so it is the protocol, not a real-robot method to beat |
| [[2212.07740\|TERT]] | history → action *directly* (no latent-vector estimation) | 100% sand / 60% stairs where RMA/TCN scores 0% | skips the explicit extrinsics — the no-latent alternative to RMA |
| [[2509.23745\|LocoFormer]] | history → action via a Transformer-XL whose context spans *episode boundaries*, forming robot-specific dynamics representations online with no explicit morphological input | 0.96 normalized displacement on 10 unseen robots; real Unitree zero-shot to OOD-dynamics/morphology (locked limbs, wheel failure, added payload, lower-leg removal — walks on knees/stilts) | the scaled implicit-long-context sibling of [[2212.07740\|TERT]]'s H5 route — adds multi-episodic cross-trial memory and demonstrated OOD-*morphology* extrapolation on real hardware, the strongest recent "skip the explicit extrinsics, read long history" instance, but still no graded distance-past-range envelope (H2) |
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | world-model prediction error (4 Hz) | OOD recovery, LoRA test-time updates | a *learned*-model adaptation engine (the D3 regime), not proprioception-only |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | online inverse-variance-weighted *parameter* adaptation | 57% vs 23% OOD | parameter-level cousin — estimates named physics, not a latent extrinsics posterior |
| [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] | prediction-residual OOD detection → online fine-tune | [[2301.04104\|DreamerV3]] residual trigger | the trigger a latent-extrinsics loop can gate on, not the estimator |
| [[2412.04323\|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]] | proprioception-history latent context, *unified adapt + robust* | sim 0.92 ID / 0.79 near-OOD / 0.58 far-OOD return; **100% real-hardware SR far-OOD (9 kg payload, slippery) with the robust-fallback engaged**, where baselines largely failed | the topThreat — builds D1's architecture and answers the boundary question, but *concedes* inference is unreliable OOD; D1 bets the inverse. Note the 0.79→0.58 sim decay and the 100% far-OOD *hardware* SR are absolute returns with no baseline OOD curve to subtract, so neither "growing gap" nor "shrinking margin" is computable from GRAM's numbers — a margin must be measured, not read off |
| [[2210.04887\|In-Hand-RMA]] | proprioception-history extrinsics inference for *in-hand rotation* (no real reward) | zero-shot in-hand object rotation across objects | pre-answers H1 on manipulation — extrinsics inference already closes the contact residual without reward |
| [[2011.11270\|COCOI]] | *contact-aware* online context inference from interaction history | generalizable non-planar pushing via online context | the manipulation contact-residual baseline — context inference, not the OOD-envelope question D1 isolates |
| [[2410.20357\|Dynamics-as-Prompts]] | *amortized* in-context parameter posterior — a causal transformer (CAPTURE) maps multi-episodic interaction history → sim-parameter distribution in one forward pass, no iterative SysID loop | 0.27 m trajectory difference at 9 iterations on real air hockey (~20% better than baselines), converges to correct CoM in ~4 sim iterations | amortizes parameter estimation but stays *parameter-centric* like [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] — estimates named sim parameters, never tested whether the in-context estimate tracks *past* the DR range (the H2 envelope) where the parameterization itself breaks |
| [[2605.21330\|Joint-Sensor-In-Hand]] | proprioception-only (joint pos/vel history) extrinsics inference via teacher-student distillation + auxiliary object-pose reconstruction, *no real reward* | 100% rotation accuracy / drop-free real in-hand reorient, 13.70 mm object-pose recon RMSE (23.4% better than MLP), 3.1× RPM over proprio-PPO | the sharper [[2210.04887\|In-Hand-RMA]]/[[2011.11270\|COCOI]] sibling — isolates proprioception-only inference + the reconstruction-supervision variant they don't, but still an *in-range* manipulation baseline; never pushes the contact task outside the DR range (H1's open delta) |
| [[1812.03399\|Latent-Variable-MBRL]] | online *Bayesian posterior* over an auxiliary dynamics latent, updated from the latest transition (ensemble PNNs + uncertainty) | adapts to mid-episode gravity-direction change; learned latent *extrapolates* underlying physical parameters beyond training in low-data HalfCheetah | the explicit-Bayesian-posterior mechanism [[2107.04034\|RMA]]'s distilled extrinsics and [[2606.02280\|LDG]]'s contrastive latent both lack — its uncertainty estimate directly instruments H2's envelope, but it is sim-only continuous control, never shown zero-shot on hardware |
| [[2602.04412\|HoRD]] | History-Conditioned Dynamics Representation infers latent environment dynamics from recent state-action history — RMA-style proprioception-history → latent-extrinsics, on the *humanoid whole-body torque-control* regime | 90.7% in-domain; 86.0% zero-shot SR in an *unseen physics engine* (Genesis) with no retraining; 85.2% zero-shot push-recovery | the cross-simulator OOD data-point none of the cited rows carries (a humanoid whole-body instance distinct from the quadruped RMA/GRAM/TERT/LocoFormer and manipulation In-Hand-RMA/COCOI rows), supporting the H2/H3 'inference holds past the training distribution' flank — but a *single* OOD point, not the graded distance-past-range sweep nor the head-to-head-vs-robust-fallback the bet requires |
| [[2606.15469\|Context-ODE]] | infers a latent environment context from state-action history (1D-CNN adapter, two-phase privileged-then-history training) feeding a *Neural-ODE forward-dynamics model* + MPPI, with online fine-tuning — the *outcome-centric learned-dynamics* latent variant | 0.022 m goal-reach RMSE under dissipating wind; robust up to **3x** OOD wind beyond the training range where [[2310.09053\|DATT]] fails at **1x**; real Sphero-BOLT (varying friction) + Fanuc CoM-pushing | the cleanest *graded-distance-past-range* extrapolation data point the H2 envelope wants on a real platform — its 3x-vs-1x OOD-wind margin is exactly the inference-extends-past-the-range claim, and like [[2606.02280\|LDG]]/[[2508.20294\|DALI]] the context is outcome-centric (forward-dynamics, not parameter) — but the latent conditions a *dynamics model for MPC*, not a policy, and it never runs the head-to-head-vs-robust-fallback ([[2412.04323\|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]]) that locates H3's crossover |
| [[2606.28476\|FADA]] | *few-shot factored-policy* adaptation: a dynamics-invariant Planner + dynamics-dependent Inverse-Dynamics-Model, adapting only LoRA adapters in the IDM from ~2 min of reward-free self-generated target data | humanoid hardware SR 20%→90% (Unitree G1 + Booster T1), −27.4% normalized error; sim-to-sim −24.7% error (largest gains on force-adaptive tasks); LoRA beats full-IDM finetune, saturates at ~2 min | a reward-free few-shot cell between [[2409.16578\|FLaRe]]'s RL fine-tune (needs reward) and [[2107.04034\|RMA]]'s zero-shot latent inference, factors out the dynamics-dependent component to adapt in isolation, but stays few-shot *in-range* target adaptation, not the past-the-DR-range envelope D1 isolates |
| [[2606.31043\|Warp RL]] | *expressive post-training reshaping*: an invertible state-conditioned RQ-spline flow warps the frozen base policy's action distribution for dynamics shift, generalizing additive residual RL beyond mean-translation | up to 70.6% PegInsertionSide SR vs 57.4% Residual-PPO; full recovery on ~50% of task-seeds; real peg-insertion −30% median completion time at matched SR | reshapes the *action distribution* rather than obtaining a deployment latent, a strictly-more-expressive correction than the additive residual, but identity-initialized post-hoc adaptation, not the latent-extrinsics inference (nor its past-the-range envelope) D1 turns on |
| [[2602.20871\|GeCo-SRT]] | a *perception-side* residual (Geometry-aware Mixture-of-Experts over point-cloud features), *continually accumulated across successive tasks* via expert-usage-prioritized replay — not a latent-extrinsics posterior, and not conditioned on a single deployment's dynamics | single-task avg SR 50.0% vs 38.3% human-correction baseline; continual 4-task sequence 63.3% avg SR / 26.5% normalized negative backward transfer, beating EWC-based continual baselines (38.3%) | a distinct axis D1's table has no cell for — every other row adapts to *one* deployment shift; GeCo-SRT accumulates *across successive tasks* without catastrophic forgetting, empirically doing (for observation-side correction, with no safety constraint) exactly what E1's H1 bets a *policy*-side Cluster-D engine can do when wrapped in a safety constraint — a candidate E1 composition target, not itself a latent-extrinsics estimator |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (online latent-extrinsics inference beats a fixed-DR policy outside the range), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Latent-extrinsics *extends past the range* on manipulation, beyond the HORA/COCOI in-range baseline.**
   - *Prediction*: extrinsics inference already closes the *in-range* contact residual without real reward ([[2210.04887|In-Hand-RMA]], [[2011.11270|COCOI]]); the open delta is that it keeps tracking *outside* the train-time range on contact-rich manipulation, recovering SR where [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] needs RL fine-tuning — so the claim moves from "closes the residual" (done) to "extends the envelope".
   - *Test*: latent-extrinsics inference vs In-Hand RMA/COCOI baselines vs RL fine-tune, pushing the contact task *outside* the DR range; report SR vs distance from the range.
   - *Row*: In-Hand RMA / COCOI (in-range manipulation baseline) vs FLaRe (RL fine-tune).
   - *Falsifier*: inference matches the in-range baseline but does not extend past the range → latent-extrinsics adds nothing beyond what HORA/COCOI already do on manipulation.
2. **H2 — The estimate stays accurate only within a measurable envelope past the DR range.**
   - *Prediction*: the latent-extrinsics estimate tracks the true dynamics some measurable distance outside the train-time DR range, then stops — mapping the envelope tells you when to trust it.
   - *Test*: push deployment progressively outside the DR range; report estimation error vs distance from the range, using [[2508.20294|DALI]]'s graded Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed regimes (over mass/friction/gravity/actuator-strength) as the named distance-past-range protocol (sim-only substrate) — and treat [[2606.15469|Context-ODE]]'s 3x-vs-1x OOD-wind margin (where [[2310.09053|DATT]] fails at 1x) as the existing *real-platform* partial instance of the envelope (a single OOD axis on quadrotor/Sphero/Fanuc, not the full graded sweep, which on a real robot remains a field gap).
   - *Row*: RMA (extrinsics inference) / DALI (Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed extrapolation-regime protocol) / Context-ODE (real-platform 3x-OOD-wind envelope instance).
   - *Falsifier*: the estimate is accurate everywhere or nowhere → there is no envelope and the deploy-time gain is illusory.
3. **H3 (front line) — The continued-inference vs robust-fallback SR-vs-distance curves cross, and the crossover is locatable.**
   - *Prediction*: matched policies — one [[2412.04323|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]]-style adapt-in-range/robust-fallback-OOD, one continued-inference — trace two *absolute* SR-vs-distance curves that *cross* somewhere past the range; the bet is that continued inference is on top in a measurable band before fallback overtakes, the inverse of GRAM's give-up-OOD conclusion. Inside the range they tie. The deliverable is the *located crossover*, not an asserted "gap grows" — a baseline (DR / contextual-RL) OOD curve is pre-registered so the margin (inference-minus-fallback) is computable, since GRAM's absolute 0.79/0.58 and 100% far-OOD hardware SR alone support neither reading.
   - *Test*: both heads on the same task on a *friction/actuator* axis (not payload, the easy axis GRAM/LoadAdapt show both heads pass); pilot first to confirm the axis can be swept far enough to reach a crossover regime; sweep deployment distance; report both absolute SR curves, the located crossover, and where GRAM hands off to its robust feature.
   - *Row*: GRAM (robust-fallback OOD, 100% far-OOD hardware on the easy payload axis) vs RMA (continued extrinsics inference).
   - *Falsifier*: the fallback curve dominates across the whole swept distance (no inference-favouring band) → inference *is* unreliable OOD and GRAM's conclusion stands; *or* the chosen real axis cannot be swept far enough to expose any crossover → the envelope-map deliverable is unbuildable on that instrument and a different axis is needed.
4. **H4 — Outcome-centric latent beats parameter-centric on unmodeled shifts.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.02280|LDG]]'s outcome-centric latent recovers more SR than [[2107.04034|RMA]]'s parameter-extrinsics on shifts no parameterization represents (disabled actuator, time-varying disturbance), and ties on in-model shifts.
   - *Test*: {outcome-centric} vs {parameter-extrinsics} on disabled-actuator + time-varying shifts, instantiated on [[2412.18781|Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation-Eval]]'s graded actuator-failure + friction-variation perturbation protocol (MuJoCo legged control — the closest standardized dynamics-OOD-severity substrate for the unmodeled-shift split) and [[2508.20294|DALI]]'s outcome-centric extrapolation regimes (its forward-dynamics-loss latent is the outcome-centric instance, +33.8–63.9% over GT context-aware in extrapolation); report SR per shift type.
   - *Row*: LDG (outcome-centric) / DALI (outcome-centric latent, extrapolation regimes) / Context-ODE (outcome-centric Neural-ODE dynamics latent, real-platform OOD-wind) / Offline RL Action Perturbation Eval (actuator-failure + friction-variation severity) vs RMA (parameter extrinsics).
   - *Falsifier*: parameter-extrinsics matches on unmodeled shifts → the outcome-centric latent adds nothing.
5. **H5 — History-to-action matches explicit extrinsics without the latent vector.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2212.07740|TERT]]'s direct history→action recovers RMA-class adaptation (100% sand) without an explicit extrinsics vector, and [[2509.23745|LocoFormer]]'s episode-spanning long context scales the same route to cross-embodiment OOD (0.96 displacement on 10 unseen robots, real-hardware morphology extrapolation), so the latent representation is optional where the policy can read history directly.
   - *Test*: {history→action} vs {explicit extrinsics} on the same terrains; report SR and adaptation latency; check whether [[2509.23745|LocoFormer]]'s multi-episodic memory buys OOD-morphology headroom the single-episode latent vector cannot.
   - *Row*: TERT (history-to-action) / LocoFormer (scaled implicit long-context, OOD-morphology) vs RMA (explicit extrinsics).
   - *Falsifier*: the explicit extrinsics beats history→action → the latent vector is load-bearing, not optional.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Proprioception under-determines extrinsics for some tasks** — vision-dominant manipulation may not expose dynamics in proprioceptive history (though [[2210.04887|In-Hand-RMA]]/[[2011.11270|COCOI]] show force/proprio history suffices in-range). → Augment the estimator with force/tactile history (links to A2's GRF reward) where proprioception alone is uninformative (H1); report the observability boundary.
> - **Online adaptation can chase noise** — a too-fast estimator may track sensor noise as dynamics change. → Use [[2107.04034|RMA]]'s slow-module (10 Hz) / fast-policy (100 Hz) separation and gate updates on [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting-RL]]'s residual magnitude.
> - **Unsafe adaptation during exploration** — online updates can drive unsafe actions before convergence. → Hand off to E1's safety-constrained continual adaptation rather than adapting reward-only.

### D2 — Differentiable-Sim Test-Time Adaptation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Deployment-Time Adaptation |
| **Thesis** | Online gradient-based test-time adaptation beating sampled RL on speed is, for an *analytical* controller, already done — single-trajectory non-episodic tuning of a geometric controller through an analytical model adapts to wind+payload more data-efficiently than model-free RL. But that route can only *tolerate* model error; it cannot *capture* it, because it has no learned residual and no neural policy. The first principle: if the dynamics model is differentiable, adapting is gradient descent on a known loss. The field assumes the analytical-controller-tuning result settles the question. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] (method), [[2507.10914\|M-GAPS]] (method), [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] (method), [[2310.09053\|DATT]] (method), [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] (survey), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2510.03471\|Quadcopter-Control-Eval-Suite]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] 81% hover-error reduction vs L1-MPC (55% vs [[2310.09053\|DATT]]) under large OOD disturbance, adaptation in 3 steps / 4.5 s wall-clock; beat [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]]'s minutes-long [[2301.04104\|DreamerV3]] fine-tune latency at equal safety risk; run the aerial head-to-head on [[2510.03471\|Quadcopter-Control-Eval-Suite]] (standardized RotorPy adaptation suite: gusty-wind / off-center-payload / rotor-fault / latency disturbances, L1-MPC/Geometric/INDI baselines, position-RMSE 0.030–0.074 m ideal + automated stress-test metrics) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: when a quadrotor hits unmodeled wind or added mass, the residual must be corrected *now* — seconds, not minutes — but the standard recipe (RL fine-tuning of a learned world model) is slow because it re-learns from sampled rollouts.
- **Today's answers**: [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting-RL]] detects OOD via [[2301.04104|DreamerV3]] prediction residuals and fine-tunes online, but a model-based RL fine-tune is slow; [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] shows the alternative — a *differentiable* hybrid dynamics model (low-fidelity analytical core + learned residual MLP) lets policy gradients flow by Back-Propagation-Through-Time, so adaptation is a first-order step, not an RL loop: 81% hover-error reduction vs L1-MPC, 55% vs [[2310.09053|DATT]], meaningful improvement after only 3 steps (4.5 s wall-clock).
- **The opening**: D2's distinctive regime is BPTT through a *learned-residual hybrid* differentiable model (analytical core + residual MLP) to adapt a *learned neural* policy, where the residual *captures* the disturbance rather than tolerating it. The general claim is *taken* — [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]] does single-trajectory online gradient adaptation on a *real* quadrotor (heavy wind+payload) and runs the DiffTune head-to-head that *is* D2's H1 ([[2202.09834|Differentiable-Physics-Online-MPC+SysID]] since 2022) — but only through a *purely analytical* model ("unchanged from nominal"), so it is robust-*to* model error, not learning it. [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] owns the learned-residual version M-GAPS structurally cannot reach (81% hover-error reduction, 3-step / 4.5-s correction), the gap [[2604.04974|Video-to-Control-Survey]] names "control-loop closure."

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: If the dynamics model is differentiable, adapting the policy to a new disturbance is just gradient descent on a known loss — the disturbance shows up as an error term the gradient fixes directly, so a few gradient steps suffice without the many trial-and-error rollouts model-free or model-based RL require. [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s 3-step / 4.5-s correction is the evidence that exact gradients shrink adaptation to a handful of steps.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "online gradient adaptation is unavoidably slow" — that fell to the [[2210.12320|GAPS]] / [[2209.10021|DiffTune]] / [[2202.09834|Differentiable-Physics-Online-MPC+SysID]] lineage by ~2022, and [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]] settles it for an analytical controller. The live assumption is that an *analytical-model* gradient route settles the whole question. It does not: a learned-residual hybrid model can *capture* a disturbance the analytical controller can only *tolerate* — and then BPTT adapts a *learned neural* policy, not a gain vector, so it works where no clean analytical form exists.
- **The bet**: BPTT through a *learned-residual hybrid* differentiable model adapting a *learned neural* policy corrects an OOD disturbance **in ≤3 steps / 4.5 s with 81% hover-error reduction** (vs L1-MPC; 55% vs [[2310.09053|DATT]]) — *and* recovers SR on a disturbance the residual captures but [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]]'s analytical-only model cannot represent, at matched wall-clock. Falsifiable: if M-GAPS's analytical-gradient route (no learned residual) matches the learned-residual loop on a disturbance off the nominal model, the learned residual buys nothing and the analytical route suffices.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *how the online adaptation gradient is obtained* (exact via differentiable sim / sampled via model-based RL / world-model self-supervision / robust-control baseline / parameter estimation / differentiable machinery reused) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | How the adaptation gradient is obtained | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] | *exact* BPTT through a hybrid analytical+residual model | 81% vs L1-MPC, 55% vs [[2310.09053\|DATT]], 3 steps / 4.5 s | needs an analytical core — contact/friction transients hard to differentiate stably |
| [[2507.10914\|M-GAPS]] | *exact* online gradient, but tunes a geometric controller's gains through a *purely analytical* model | adapts to wind+payload on a real quadrotor, "more data-efficient than model-free RL", DiffTune head-to-head | the topThreat — settles online-gradient-beats-RL for an analytical controller, but no learned residual / no neural policy, so it *tolerates* not *captures* model error |
| [[2202.09834\|Differentiable-Physics-Online-MPC+SysID]] | *exact* online differentiable-physics gradient (adaptive MPC + sysID) | real-time online sysID + MPC via differentiable physics (RA-L'22) | the 2022 root proving online gradient adaptation is fast — analytical/parametric, no learned residual or neural policy |
| [[2603.27313\|MetaTune]] | adjoint-based *meta-tuning* via differentiable robot dynamics | meta-tuned adaptation through differentiable dynamics | adjoint gradients for parameter/gain meta-tuning, not BPTT adapting a learned neural policy with a learned residual |
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | world-model prediction-error self-supervision (4 Hz) | OOD recovery, LoRA updates | a *learned*-model engine (D3 regime), no differentiable analytical dynamics |
| [[2310.09053\|DATT]] | adaptive control baseline (no differentiable sim) | the trajectory-tracking baseline Learning-on-the-Fly beats by 55% | classical adaptive control, not gradient-based test-time adaptation |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | online IVW *parameter* estimation | 57% vs 23% OOD | gradient-adjacent online estimation, not policy adaptation by BPTT |
| [[2604.27367\|DOT-Sim]] | differentiable MPM (for *parameter recovery*) | 96.55% zero-shot | the differentiable machinery TTA reuses at deploy-time — not yet a TTA loop |
| [[2604.10856\|BridgeSim]] | flow-matching observational calibrator (OL→CL) | +19.1 DS | test-time *observational* calibration, adjacent to policy-gradient TTA |
| [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] | (no method) names control-loop-closure gap | survey | the loop a fast differentiable TTA closes |
| [[2505.15589\|Reflexive-World-Models]] | *exact* gradient on a *frozen* world model's predicted-vs-actual latent error — adjusts a lightweight adaptive controller's actions (not model params) toward the WM's predictions used as implicit reference trajectories | 0.6003 vs 0.3600 median reward under perturbation vs domain-randomized [[2301.04104\|DreamerV3]]/TD-MPC2; online adapt 1.8 h vs 16.5 h full retrain | the D2/D3 hinge — exact gradients like [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] but the reference comes from a *learned* WM not a differentiable analytical core, so it inherits [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s WM-quality dependence; runs in DMC sim, no real-robot 4 Hz, no hardware OOD proof |
| [[2606.09640\|Physics-Aware-Sparse-EL]] | selective *Bayesian* online update (not gradient) of a structure-preserving learned force-*residual* on an analytical Euler-Lagrange core — updates only the disturbance-sensitive generalized-force component in its active sparse latent subspace, keeping the mechanically structured inertia/Coriolis terms fixed | lowest normalized prediction error + best figure-eight tracking RMSE across all 5 platforms (diff-drive / 6-DoF arm / quadrotor / mobile + aerial manipulator); faster error reduction and smoother convergence after payload release | the residual sibling of [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] that *captures* payload-release the analytical core cannot (H1) — but Bayesian-not-BPTT, corrects the *model* not a *learned neural policy*, so it never closes the policy-adaptation loop M-GAPS and Learning-on-the-Fly contest |
| [[2505.24068\|DiffCoTune]] | *exact* gradient co-tuning of a differentiable simulator AND a differentiable controller via real-world rollouts (split-alternate: refine sim params on real rollouts, then tune controller in the refined sim) | 60% sim-to-sim quadruped velocity-tracking gain; Go1 +1.04 m in 5 s, bipedal 34 cm hardware jump where untuned + controller-only tuning fall | also tunes the *sim*, unlike [[2507.10914\|M-GAPS]] (fixed nominal model) and [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] (residual, no sim update) — but tunes controller *parameters/gains*, not a learned neural policy via BPTT, and the loop is offline-iterative (a few rollouts), not 3-step in-flight test-time correction |
| [[2602.02331\|TTT-Parkour]] | *sampled* RL fine-tuning of a locomotion policy inside a reconstructed sim (full-parameter fine-tune on RGB-D-reconstructed real-terrain meshes, offline-then-redeploy) | 99–100% sim SR; 0% → 60–100% zero-shot on Unitree G1; ~10 min full pipeline (capture + reconstruct + TTT, converges to 97% in ~10 min) | the model-based-RL-fine-tune branch the exact-gradient differentiable route must beat on *speed* (H4) — supplies a clean, quantified REAL sim-to-real wall-clock contrast (10 min, 0%→100%) sharper than the cited sampled exemplar [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]]; a sampled-branch contrast row, not a differentiable-gradient method |
| [[2606.24552\|Sim-in-Loop-Cloth]] | *sampled* simulator-in-the-loop refinement at *inference time*: a prior-guided MPPI controller refines an offline base policy's actions via parallel rollouts in the FLASH cloth simulator, with an RGB-native real-to-sim state estimator (DINOv2 + canonical tokens) closing the real→sim→real loop per step | 9/10 single-arm / 8/10 dual-arm folding; 2.33 mm² recon MSE at 7.4 ms; 7–8/10 disturbance recovery with no dynamics retraining | the *sampled*-refinement counterpart of [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s exact-gradient correction, on the deformable/cloth regime where differentiable contact is hardest (H3) — it *corrects online via the simulator* but by MPPI sampling not BPTT, and corrects *actions* not a learned residual, so it is the per-step sampled-online-refinement contrast the exact-gradient bet must beat on latency where an analytical core is unavailable |
| [[2607.26370\|Self-Adaptive Learning and MPC]] | *no* differentiable sim: an ensemble of predictors is trained online from scratch (Random Fourier Features + online gradient descent), a bandit (Hedge) selects the best-performing one each step, and MPC plans against it — a formally regret-bounded alternative to exact-gradient BPTT | O(T^{3/4}) dynamic regret guarantee; outperforms baselines on switching-dynamics tracking; validated on Crazyflie hardware with online-switching disturbances | a *non-differentiable, theory-grounded* competitor D2's table lacks — no analytical or learned-residual model at all, just an online-selected ensemble, with a formal no-regret bound the exact-gradient rows don't supply; untested on the manipulation/contact regime H3 scopes, and no head-to-head against [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s 3-step/4.5 s wall-clock |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (exact-gradient differentiable TTA beats sampled model-based-RL fine-tune on speed at equal risk), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (front line) — Learned-residual BPTT captures a disturbance M-GAPS's analytical route can only tolerate.**
   - *Prediction*: on a disturbance *off* the nominal analytical model (e.g. an unmodeled aerodynamic or contact effect), [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s learned-residual-hybrid BPTT recovers more SR than [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]]'s analytical-only gain tuning, because the residual *represents* what the analytical model cannot — and both still beat [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting-RL]]'s sampled fine-tune on wall-clock.
   - *Test*: inject a disturbance off the nominal model — using [[2510.03471|Quadcopter-Control-Eval-Suite]]'s configurable RotorPy disturbances (rotor-fault / off-center payload / latency, the regime off the nominal analytical model) as the standardized substrate; compare SR + steps-to-correct for {learned-residual BPTT} vs {M-GAPS analytical gradient} vs {model-based-RL fine-tune} (the suite benchmarks robust-adaptive *control* methods, so the learned-residual-vs-analytical comparison itself is the unrun bet it hosts).
   - *Row*: Learning on the Fly (learned-residual BPTT) / AdaptiveQuadBench (RotorPy disturbance substrate) vs M-GAPS (analytical-only gradient) vs Self-Adapting RL (sampled RL).
   - *Falsifier*: M-GAPS's analytical route matches the learned-residual loop off the nominal model → the residual buys nothing and the analytical gradient suffices.
2. **H2 — Residual expressiveness trades adaptation speed against disturbance range.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] backprops only through the analytical core; enlarging the learned residual captures a wider disturbance range but slows adaptation — a measurable speed/range frontier.
   - *Test*: sweep residual-network size; report adaptation speed vs the range of disturbances captured, mapping the range on [[2510.03471|Quadcopter-Control-Eval-Suite]]'s disturbance library (wind/payload/rotor-fault/latency at configurable severity).
   - *Row*: Learning on the Fly (exact BPTT) / AdaptiveQuadBench (disturbance-severity range).
   - *Falsifier*: residual size changes neither speed nor range → expressiveness is irrelevant.
3. **H3 — The 3-step correction degrades on contact-rich ground manipulation.**
   - *Prediction*: the BPTT speed advantage holds where the analytical core is accurate (aerial) and erodes on contact-rich ground manipulation where richer contact forces more adaptation steps — so the bet is scoped to analytical-core regimes.
   - *Test*: compare steps-to-correct on aerial vs contact-rich tasks; report where the latency advantage shrinks — using [[2606.24552|Sim-in-Loop-Cloth]] as the evidence that on the deformable/cloth end of the contact-rich axis the field already resorts to *sampled* (MPPI) sim-in-the-loop refinement rather than exact gradients, marking where the analytical-core/differentiable route gives way.
   - *Row*: Learning on the Fly (exact BPTT) / DOT-Sim (differentiable contact machinery) / Sim-in-Loop Cloth (sampled sim-in-loop on the contact-rich deformable end).
