   - *Falsifier*: the 3-step correction holds on contact-rich tasks → the analytical-core boundary is not real.
4. **H4 — Exact gradients beat world-model self-supervision on latency where both apply.**
   - *Prediction*: where an analytical model exists, [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s exact BPTT corrects faster than [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s learned-world-model prediction-error supervision — exactness beats self-supervision on speed, marking the D2/D3 boundary.
   - *Test*: on a task with an analytical model, {exact BPTT} vs {world-model supervision}; report steps-to-correct.
   - *Row*: Learning on the Fly (exact BPTT) vs AdaWorldPolicy (world-model supervision).
   - *Falsifier*: world-model supervision matches exact BPTT on speed → the analytical model adds no latency advantage and D2 collapses into D3.
5. **H5 — A bounded per-step update keeps fast adaptation safe.**
   - *Prediction*: a bad gradient step can destabilize the platform, but bounding the per-step update (and handing execution-safety to E2's shield during the adaptation window) keeps the 3-step correction within the safety spec at no meaningful speed cost.
   - *Test*: {unbounded} vs {bounded per-step update + E2 shield}; report stability + adaptation speed.
   - *Row*: Learning on the Fly (exact BPTT).
   - *Falsifier*: bounding the update destroys the speed advantage → fast and safe trade off and the bet over-claims safety.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Differentiable dynamics may not exist for the regime** — rich contact / friction transients are hard to differentiate stably. → Start in [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]]'s aerial/analytical-core regime where the hybrid model is accurate (H3); expand to contact via B3's differentiable-MPM machinery cautiously.
> - **Fast overfitting to transient noise** — a 3-step adapt can lock onto a momentary disturbance. → Gate adaptation on [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting-RL]]'s residual-magnitude trigger and decay the residual when the disturbance clears.
> - **Adaptation during flight is safety-critical** — a bad gradient step destabilizes the platform. → Bound the per-step update (H5) and hand execution-safety to E2's reachability shield during the adaptation window.

### D3 — World-Model-Supervised Online Policy Correction

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Deployment-Time Adaptation |
| **Thesis** | Driving reward-free online policy correction from a world model's prediction error is no longer novel — per-step prediction-error supervision of a VLA, with an adaptive update filter, already recovers OOD without reward in sim. What survives is the *real-robot* instantiation: a unified world-model + force + action backbone that adapts at 4 Hz on hardware and adds a *force*-prediction-error term for contact OOD that an image-foresight head cannot supply. The first principle: physical consistency and task reward optimize different things, and prediction error is observable on hardware while reward is not. The field assumes prediction-error correction is the contribution. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] (method), [[2605.08215\|T3VF]] (method), [[2007.04309\|Self-Supervised-Deploy-Adapt]] (method), [[2604.18107\|PDF]] (method), [[2605.00080\|WM-Robot-Learning-Survey]] (survey), [[2601.07823\|Video-Generation-in-Robotics-Survey]] (survey), [[2606.12690\|EWAM]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] 0.96 [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]]-10 + 48.0% [[2112.03227\|CALVIN]] 5-task, online adaptation at 4 Hz on real robots via world-model prediction error (LoRA), recovering performance under visual+physical OOD where offline-only policies degrade; ground OOD-recovery on [[2510.13626\|LIBERO-Plus]] (7 dims × 21 sub-dims, brittle drop >95%→<30%, post-trained OpenVLA-OFT 79.6%) and the real-robot + contact endpoint on [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (Table30 real fleet, soft-body/contact split 8% SR / 27% progress) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the blocker on deploy-time correction is that *real reward is not available* — you cannot run RL on hardware against a reward you can't compute.
- **Today's answers**: [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] resolves it by making the world model an *active supervisor* — it unifies a world model, an action expert, and a force predictor under a flow-matching DiT, and uses the world model's *prediction error* as a self-supervised signal to drive test-time LoRA updates, at 4 Hz on real robots, with no environment reward — holding 0.96 on [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]-10 and recovering under visual and physical OOD where an offline-only policy degrades.
- **The opening**: D3's surviving territory is the *real-robot* 4 Hz regime where a *force*-prediction-error term sharpens the contact OOD image foresight misses, via [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s unified WM+force+action DiT. The mechanism is now *consensus* — [[2605.08215|T3VF]] drives reward-free per-step VLA updates from a prediction-error pair (predicted vs actual future image) with an adaptive *filter* that is precisely D3's H5 calibration-gate ([[2007.04309|Self-Supervised-Deploy-Adapt]] broke "needs reward" back in 2021) — but only in *sim* (LIBERO-Plus) with a *visual-foresight* head. [[2604.18107|PDF]] shows the reward-free family extends even without prediction-error supervision (+8 pp LIBERO over OpenVLA), the route H4 compares against.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Training a policy toward physical consistency and toward task reward optimize *two different things*. A world model's prediction error measures how far the policy has drifted from the dynamics the model knows — and you can see that error on hardware, whereas you cannot see the task reward there; when the dynamics shift, the prediction error grows, so following it down corrects the right thing without any reward. [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s 4 Hz real adaptation with *no* reward is the evidence that prediction error can stand in for the reward you can't observe.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "online correction needs reward" — [[2007.04309|Self-Supervised-Deploy-Adapt]] broke that in 2021, and [[2605.08215|T3VF]] makes prediction-error-as-reward consensus in sim. The live assumption is that an image-foresight prediction-error head is the whole signal. It is not on a real robot under *contact* OOD: a *force*-prediction-error term (which [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s unified WM+force+action DiT supplies and T3VF's visual-foresight head cannot) carries the contact-drift direction visual prediction misses.
- **The bet**: An [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]-style unified WM+force+action loop drives **4 Hz real-robot** online adaptation to unseen dynamics with no real reward, holding **0.96 [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]-10** under OOD where a static policy degrades, *and* its **force-prediction-error term beats a [[2605.08215|T3VF]]-style image-foresight-only head on contact-rich OOD** (the regime image foresight is blind to). Falsifiable: if image-foresight prediction-error supervision matches the force-augmented loop on contact OOD (or a static policy holds 0.96 under the same OOD), the force term — and the real-robot unified backbone — buys nothing over T3VF's sim result.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what supplies the adaptation signal with no real reward* (world-model prediction error / prediction-residual trigger / learned-residual / verifier-free perturbation / parameter estimation / twin-grounded offline) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Adaptation signal (no real reward) | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | world-model *prediction error* (flow-matching DiT) → 4 Hz LoRA | 0.96 [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]]-10, 48.0% [[2112.03227\|CALVIN]], OOD recovery | prediction-error-supervised proof — whether it matches a true-reward loop is open |
| [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] | prediction-*residual* OOD detection → online fine-tune | [[2301.04104\|DreamerV3]] residual trigger | the precursor signal; uses the residual to *trigger*, not to supervise the whole update |
| [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] | learned-*residual* dynamics (the D2 differentiable cousin) | 3-step correction | analytical-model regime (D2), not learned-world-model supervision |
| [[2605.08215\|T3VF]] | *visual-foresight* prediction-error (predicted vs actual future image) → per-step VLA update + adaptive filter | OOD recovery on LIBERO-Plus, no reward, explicitly vs RL-route | the topThreat — owns the sim/image-foresight case incl. D3's H5 update-filter, but no real-robot 4 Hz, no unified WM+force DiT, no force-prediction-error term |
| [[2007.04309\|Self-Supervised-Deploy-Adapt]] | self-supervised auxiliary loss at deploy-time (the 2021 root) | self-supervised policy adaptation during deployment on a real robot | broke "online correction needs reward" in 2021 — but a hand-chosen auxiliary task, not world-model prediction-error supervision |
| [[2510.11689\|Phys2Real]] | online *parameter* estimation | 57% vs 23% OOD | parameter-level online correction, not prediction-error supervision |
| [[2511.07416\|PhysWorld]] | residual RL on a *reconstructed* world model (offline) | 82% real | world-model-grounded correction, but offline, not online prediction-error |
| [[2604.10856\|BridgeSim]] | truncated Q-estimator + observational calibrator (OL→CL) | +19.1 DS | closed-loop correction adjacent, not prediction-error self-supervision |
| [[2601.07823\|Video-Generation-in-Robotics-Survey]] | (no method) names hallucination/physics-violation risk | survey | the supervision risk a prediction-error loop must gate against |
| [[2606.18589\|DREAM-Chunk]] | *WM-latent match* — predict each candidate VLA chunk's dreamed latent future, reactively *select* the chunk whose dream best matches observed reality (no weight update) | 10%→65% can-insertion on Franka (+55 pp), ~7ms WM latency | a new endpoint vs [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] (LoRA) and [[2605.08215\|T3VF]] (per-step weight update) — corrects by *chunk selection* without any parameter update, so it cannot recover OOD outside the pre-sampled chunk pool the way a weight-updating loop can |
| [[2505.15589\|Reflexive-World-Models]] | WM-predicted latent trajectory as an *implicit reference*; adaptive controller minimizes squared error vs observed latents by gradient-correcting *actions* (not weights), in real time | 0.6003 vs 0.3600 median normalized reward under perturbation (vs DR'd TD-MPC2); 1.8 h vs 16.5 h adaptation | the control-theoretic sibling of [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s LoRA route — corrects actions for fast formally-grounded recovery, but on DMC latents (no VLA, no real-robot 4 Hz, no force-prediction-error term H3 turns on) |
| [[2606.12690\|EWAM]] | Neural Anomaly Detection + Action Correction over a frozen WAM, with an *experience filter* admitting only safe/successful trajectories for intermittent online parameter updates | 83.7% fewer faults/episode (13.5→2.2), 87.2%→94.4% SR (BlockStacking), 63.8% faster | concretely instantiates H5's calibration-gate / update-filter (rejecting unsafe trajectories the [[2601.07823\|Video-Generation-in-Robotics-Survey]] flags abstractly) — but sim-only, with a heuristic experience filter, not a conformal-calibrated gate, and no WM-prediction-error supervision driving the update |
| [[2602.21198\|Reflective-Test-Time-Planning]] | *hindsight reflection* — retrospective verbal critiques of past actions converted to self-supervised signals → LoRA parameter update at deploy time, no reward | 33.65% vs 11.13% (3DLLM-Mem) on Long-Horizon Household; 19.5% zero-shot on HM3D | the reflection-driven sibling of D3's reward-free deploy-time LoRA — a *distinct* supervision source (hindsight reflection vs WM prediction error) with explicit long-horizon credit assignment, but an embodied-LLM planner, not a low-level WM+force action loop |
| [[2601.06748\|TT-VLA]] | *learned dense reward* — value-free PPO driven by step-wise task-progress reward from a pretrained [[2509.15937\|VLAC]] critic, updated every 8 steps at inference | up to 44.9% relative SR gain (OpenVLA) across 15 unseen sim tasks + 9 real pick-place | the explicit learned-reward-model online-RL route D3/T3VF position *against* — supplies the dense-reward foil H1 needs, but depends on a learned critic's reward (a proxy that can drift OOD) where prediction error is directly observable on hardware |
| [[2511.14178\|VLA-Pilot]] | *learned MLLM-judge* — frozen-backbone inference-time steering: an MLLM-as-black-box-scorer (EPS-CoT) reward drives Evolutionary Diffusion that mutates/selects action candidates, *no weight update*, no world model | +0.31 real MSR (DiVLA), 0.50 OOD MSR vs 0.12/0.19 baselines, parity with 50-demo fine-tuning, zero-shot cross-embodiment | the *learned-judge* foil distinct from [[2601.06748\|TT-VLA]]'s VLAC-critic+weight-update and [[2606.18589\|DREAM-Chunk]]'s WM-latent chunk-selection — its MLLM scorer is exactly the learned-proxy route D3 bets against (it can drift OOD), sharpening the contrarian case that a directly-observable WM/force prediction-error signal beats a learned judge; no force/contact term, so it reinforces the surviving wedge rather than preempting it |
| [[2606.18247\|VERITAS]] | *learned visual verifier* — gradient-free verifier scores sampled action chunks at inference + verifier-curated self-collected rollouts autonomously fine-tune the base policy offline | +12.6% sim / +35% real from inference-time steering (no fine-tune); +9.7% sim from offline fine-tune; verifier-curated data as effective as human demos | extends the learned-judge/verifier foil family (alongside [[2511.14178\|VLA-Pilot]]'s MLLM-judge and [[2601.06748\|TT-VLA]]'s VLAC critic) with a new endpoint — a visual verifier *that can drift OOD* steers chunks then self-fine-tunes — so it strengthens the contrarian case that a directly-observable WM/force prediction-error signal is the right one; no force/contact term, reinforcing the surviving wedge |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (world-model prediction-error supervision drives reward-free online correction), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Prediction-error supervision matches a true-reward loop (or corrects a different objective).**
   - *Prediction*: where both are computable, [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s prediction-error self-supervision either matches a true real-reward RL loop or corrects a measurably *different* (consistency, not task) objective — quantifying the gap tells which.
   - *Test*: {prediction-error} vs {true-reward RL} where reward is computable; report the SR gap and what each optimizes.
   - *Row*: AdaWorldPolicy (prediction error).
   - *Falsifier*: prediction-error supervision matches the reward loop exactly with no objective gap → consistency and task reward coincide and the distinction is moot.
2. **H2 — Prediction error detects some OOD shifts and is blind to others.**
   - *Prediction*: world-model prediction error gives a usable correction gradient for physical/dynamics OOD but is blind to visual-only OOD that doesn't change predicted dynamics — mapping which shifts it catches bounds the method.
   - *Test*: visual vs physical vs dynamics OOD on [[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]]'s 7×21 perturbation dimensions (the suite T3VF runs on, isolating the visual-OOD axis) and [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s friction/mass physical perturbation (R̄²=0.614 sim↔real, the physical-OOD axis); report where prediction error gives a usable gradient.
   - *Row*: AdaWorldPolicy (prediction error) / LIBERO-Plus (visual-OOD axis) / THE COLOSSEUM (physical-OOD axis).
   - *Falsifier*: prediction error catches all OOD equally → there is no blind spot and the signal is universal.
3. **H3 (front line) — Force-prediction error beats a T3VF-style image-foresight head on contact-rich OOD.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s unified world-model + force predictor lets a *force*-prediction-error term (links to A2's GRF reward, [[2604.27367|DOT-Sim]]'s tactile sysID) recover contact-rich OOD that [[2605.08215|T3VF]]'s visual-foresight prediction-error head is blind to — the force signal carries the contact-drift direction image foresight cannot see.
   - *Test*: {T3VF-style visual prediction error} vs {visual + force-prediction error} on contact-rich OOD; report correction quality and recovered SR on [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]]'s soft-body/contact split (Table30 real fleet, 8% SR / 27% progress — the real-robot contact endpoint, though the force-correction-quality metric itself stays method-internal).
   - *Row*: AdaWorldPolicy (force-augmented prediction error) / RoboChallenge (real-robot contact split) vs T3VF (image-foresight only).
   - *Falsifier*: the image-foresight head matches the force-augmented loop on contact OOD → visual prediction error suffices and the force term (D3's surviving wedge) buys nothing.
4. **H4 — Prediction-error supervision beats verifier-free perturbation *and* a learned-judge scorer on dynamics OOD.**
   - *Prediction*: where the dynamics shift, [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s prediction-error supervision recovers more SR than both [[2604.18107|PDF]]'s verifier-free perturbation (blind search) and [[2511.14178|VLA-Pilot]]'s learned-MLLM-judge steering (a proxy that drifts OOD), because the prediction error carries the directly-observable *direction* of the dynamics drift that neither a blind perturbation nor a learned judge supplies.
   - *Test*: {prediction-error supervision} vs {verifier-free perturbation} vs {learned-MLLM-judge steering} on dynamics OOD, instantiated on [[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]]'s perturbation dimensions (7×21, five difficulty levels — the named OOD substrate T3VF and PDF both run on); report SR and steps-to-recover.
   - *Row*: AdaWorldPolicy (prediction error) / LIBERO-Plus (OOD perturbation substrate) vs PDF (verifier-free perturbation) / VLA-Pilot (learned-MLLM-judge, no WM).
   - *Falsifier*: verifier-free perturbation or the learned judge matches prediction-error supervision → the directional signal is not worth the world model.
5. **H5 — World-model hallucination poisons the gradient unless calibration-gated.**
   - *Prediction*: when the world model is itself OOD, its prediction error supervises toward the *wrong* correction — gating updates on prediction-error *calibration* (links to E3's conformal detector) and rejecting corrections when the model is OOD prevents the poisoning [[2601.07823|Video-Generation-in-Robotics-Survey]] flags.
   - *Test*: inject world-model OOD; {ungated} vs {calibration-gated} updates; report corrupted-correction rate, using [[2604.19092|RoboWM-Bench]]'s executability + unstable-contact-prediction failure-mode evaluation (greater discriminative power than perceptual plausibility metrics) to surface where the world model hallucinates.
   - *Row*: AdaWorldPolicy (prediction error) / RoboWM-Bench (WM-executability / unstable-contact-prediction failures) / Video Generation in Robotics Survey (physics-violation flag).
   - *Falsifier*: the ungated loop never degrades under model OOD → hallucination doesn't poison the gradient and gating is unnecessary.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **World-model hallucination poisons the gradient** — a wrong prediction supervises toward the wrong correction. → Gate updates on prediction-error *calibration* (links to E3's conformal detector, H5) and reject corrections when the world model is itself OOD per [[2601.07823|Video-Generation-in-Robotics-Survey]]'s physics-violation flag.
> - **Prediction error ≠ task error** — minimizing consistency may not improve task success. → H1 measures the consistency-vs-task gap; pair prediction-error supervision with sparse real success checks where available.
> - **Unsafe correction under no-reward adaptation** — without reward, the loop may drift into unsafe regions. → Hand off to E1's safety-cost-constrained continual adaptation so the no-reward update stays inside safe limits.

---

## Cluster E — Risk-Bounded Sim-to-Real Deployment: Safety Under the Irreducible Gap

*Bound the irreducible residual gap at runtime — an un-handled gap is a safety failure, not just a performance loss, so it must be bounded, not assumed away. Three distinct surfaces: bound the update (E1), bound the action (E2), flag the failure (E3).*

### E1 — Zero-Violation Continual Adaptation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | E — Risk-Bounded Sim-to-Real Deployment |
| **Thesis** | That reward-only continual adaptation goes unsafe, and that EWC alone fails to preserve safety, is now a measured published result. What no one has shown is whether a zero-violation continual-adaptation constraint *composes* — whether it wraps an arbitrary Cluster-D online engine on real hardware while preserving both the adaptation gain and the safety guarantee across successive domains. The first principle: an unsafe action on hardware has a consequence the reward cannot undo, so safety is a constraint set, not a reward term. No cited method tests this composition; it is this direction's first attempt. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] (method), [[2604.19737\|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] (benchmark), [[2509.18648\|SPiDR]] (method), [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] (method), [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] (benchmark), [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] (survey), [[2507.10087\|Foundation-Robotics-Review]] (survey), [[2407.04942\|FOSP]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | Hold [[2503.10949\|SCDA]]'s real grasp SR 20%→60% at a violation-rate upper-confidence-bound below a pre-registered threshold (≤6% at N≈50, rule-of-three — not literal zero) *when wrapping a [[2107.04034\|RMA]]/[[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] Cluster-D engine* (not just SCDA's own grasp loop); the safety-budget / adaptation-rate knee; bounded forgetting × violation-UCB across *successive* domains where [[2604.19737\|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] (Damaged HalfCheetah/Ant Velocity + Safe Continual World, 8-algorithm cost+normalized-forgetting protocol) shows online-EWC violates and CPO catastrophically forgets, with the violation/budget axis grounded on [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] (canonical safe-RL suite, SafePO 16 algorithms, PPO-Lag 98% cost reduction — sim-only/single-domain) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: Cluster D's online engines all share a hazard — an exploratory update on real hardware can drive an unsafe action before it converges to a useful one — and the field's default (reward-only online RL fine-tuning) has no mechanism to keep exploration safe.
- **Today's answers**: [[2503.10949|SCDA]] documents that reward-only adaptation "led to unsafe behaviors" and makes safety a *constraint* — Policy-Constrained Reward and Cost Policy Optimization (PCRPO) for safe RL plus Elastic Weight Consolidation (EWC) for continual learning, with a Fisher Information Matrix computed in randomized-sim pretraining to protect important parameters, then adaptation under *stricter* safety limits on hardware: real grasp SR rises 20%→60% *at zero safety cost*, the only strategy that improves across an entire domain without catastrophic forgetting.
- **The opening**: E1's front line is *composition* — whether the zero-violation constraint *wraps arbitrary Cluster-D engines* on hardware. The two-axis tension is now *measured* — [[2604.19737|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] (predecessor [[2502.15922|Safe-EWC]]) shows online-EWC *violates* constraints while CPO *catastrophically forgets*, and [[2604.09452|SafeAdapt]] restates the EWC critique near-verbatim — so "is unconstrained adaptation unsafe" is settled. [[2509.18648|SPiDR]] shows safety can also be enforced at *transfer-time* (DR + pessimistic-ensemble cost penalty as a CMDP, safe transfer to a real race car + Go1), so transfer- and adaptation-time faces *compose* — the gap [[2507.10087|Foundation-Robotics-Review]] names "safety from model hallucinations."

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: On hardware, an action's safety cost is a hard constraint with no recovery — an unsafe exploratory update has a consequence the reward cannot undo. Adaptation must optimize reward *subject to* a safety-cost bound; safety is a constraint set, not a reward term. [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s direct comparison (reward-only goes unsafe; cost-constrained hits zero violations at the same 60% SR) is the evidence that safety must be an explicit constraint.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "unconstrained adaptation is unsafe" — [[2604.19737|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]]/[[2502.15922|Safe-EWC]]/[[2604.09452|SafeAdapt]] made that a measured result. But [[2604.09452|SafeAdapt]] does hold a belief on the composition question itself, stated in its own abstract: "one can provide formal, provable guarantees for **arbitrary RL algorithms** used to update a policy by projecting their updates onto the Rashomon set." That claim is broader than its evidence — the method text scopes it to "any gradient-based method," "provided that after each update the parameters are projected back onto" the certified region, and the empirical validation is PPO fine-tuning alone, on two gridworld tasks (Frozen Lake, Poisoned Apple). The claim presupposes the update *is* a gradient step that touches the protected parameters — exactly the presupposition Cluster-D's engines break: [[2107.04034|RMA]] updates only a latent-extrinsics encoder, with no gradient touching the protected policy weights at all (H3 predicts the projection is structurally *vacuous* for it), and [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] updates a low-rank LoRA subspace that may sit near-orthogonal to the Rashomon set's protected directions (H3 predicts protection is *mis-targeted*, not simply absent). So the open question is not whether composition has been tried — it's whether SafeAdapt's own "arbitrary RL algorithms" claim survives contact with an update shape its abstract's guarantee never validated: a real Cluster-D engine on real hardware.
- **The bet**: [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s PCRPO+EWC, **wrapping a Cluster-D engine** ([[2107.04034|RMA]]/[[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]), holds the **20%→60% gain** at a violation-rate UCB below a pre-registered threshold (≤6% at N≈50, rule-of-three, not literal zero) *and* keeps both bounded forgetting and bounded violation-UCB across **N successive domains** — where [[2604.19737|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] shows online-EWC violates and CPO forgets when run alone. Falsifiable: if wrapping a Cluster-D engine destroys the gain or drives the violation-UCB above threshold, or successive-domain forgetting/safety trade off, the constraint does not compose and SCDA is a standalone result, not a wrapper.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *when and how safety is enforced during adaptation* (constrained continual adaptation / pessimistic transfer-time CMDP / execution-time shield / unconstrained engine / negative-adaptation warning) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | When/how safety is enforced | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] | constrained *continual adaptation* (PCRPO + EWC + Fisher) | 20%→60% at *zero* cost, no forgetting | the adaptation-time anchor — wrapping arbitrary Cluster-D engines is open |
| [[2604.19737\|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] | *continual* safe RL in non-stationary envs (method-comparative) | online-EWC *violates* constraints while CPO *catastrophically forgets* | the topThreat — makes E1's two-axis tension a measured result, but MuJoCo/control-bench and comparative, not a real-hardware wrapper over Cluster-D engines |
| [[2502.15922\|Safe-EWC]] | safe continual RL design for nonlinear control (ICRA'25 predecessor) | characterizes the safety×forgetting tension for control | the lineage root that settles "is unconstrained adaptation unsafe" — diagnosis, not a composition result |
| [[2604.09452\|SafeAdapt]] | *provably safe* policy updates in deep RL | provably safe policy-update scheme, restates the EWC critique | provable safe updates in isolation — never wraps an arbitrary online engine across successive domains |
| [[2509.18648\|SPiDR]] | *transfer-time* CMDP (pessimistic ensemble cost penalty) | safe transfer to real race car + Go1 where DR violates | transfer-time safety, not continual on-hardware adaptation — the complementary face |
| [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] | *execution-time* reachability shield | 0% collisions vs 5–35% | bounds the *action* (E2), not the *update* — complementary surface |
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | none (4 Hz online LoRA under OOD) | OOD recovery | the unconstrained engine E1 hardens with a safety cost — its update is a low-rank LoRA subspace, so H3 predicts EWC's Fisher penalty may be *mis-targeted* rather than protective for this engine |
| [[2107.04034\|RMA]] | none (fast online latent-extrinsics) | zero-fine-tune, 12 kg payload | the D1 loop that needs a safety bound — it updates only a latent-extrinsics encoder with no gradient touching policy weights, so H3 predicts EWC protection is structurally *vacuous* for this engine |
| [[2409.16578\|FLaRe]] | none (reward-driven RL fine-tuning) | +30.7% real | the reward-driven adaptation E1 makes safe |
| [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] | none (residual-triggered fine-tune) | DreamerV3 residual trigger | the trigger that should fire under a safety constraint |
| [[2502.10694\|UDA-Simulation-Study]] | (no method) names "negative adaptation" | adaptation can do *worse* than none | the warning that unconstrained adaptation is unsafe |
| [[2407.04942\|FOSP]] | *model-based* safe offline→online adaptation (DreamerV3 world-model + cost-decoder + Reachability-Estimation-Function + safe-policy-expansion) | near-zero violations on real Franka 'SafeReach', adapts to unseen safety regions/objects in *40 gradient steps* | the model-based twin of [[2503.10949\|SCDA]]'s parametric face — a world-model safe-adaptation engine on hardware, but demonstrated on its own loop, not wrapping an arbitrary Cluster-D engine across successive domains |
| [[2412.04426\|Marvel]] | safe *offline-to-online transition* (Value-Pre-Alignment re-aligns reward/cost Q + adaptive-PID Lagrangian) | costs below safety threshold at ~*120,000 interactions* vs millions for from-scratch baselines | the safe O2O *handoff* mechanism — bounds the offline→online seam [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] (continual) and [[2509.18648\|SPiDR]] (transfer-time) leave implicit, but sim-only (Bullet/Safety-Gym), no hardware composition |
| [[2601.19612\|SOOPER]] | *online-learning-time* prior-fallback (pessimistic conservative policy prior + optimistic world-model planning, cost-budget switch) | no violations throughout learning with *sublinear regret*; ~2× the prior's reward fine-tuning a real race car | the throughout-learning *prior-fallback* face with formal regret bounds — distinct from [[2509.18648\|SPiDR]]'s transfer-time pessimistic ensemble, but its safety rests on a fixed prior, not a Fisher-protected continual policy across domains |
| [[2404.09080\|ATACOM]] | *throughout-learning* constraint-geometry projection (actions projected onto the tangent space of a constraint manifold, no backup policy) | safe online fine-tuning on real KUKA iiwa14, SR *12%→71%* with near-zero violations over 3000 episodes | the geometric safe-exploration mechanism that needs no backup policy — bounds *every* exploratory action where [[2503.10949\|SCDA]]'s PCRPO bounds the cost in expectation, but single-task, not continual across successive domains |
| [[2506.11033\|Adaptive-Shielding]] | *train-time × execution-time* composition under dynamics shift (Safety-Regularized Optimization + adaptive conformal-prediction shield + online function-encoder dynamics inference) | lower cost rates with competitive returns OOD, only *~1.1s* added execution overhead (3.94s→5.05s, Car-Goal) | the direct demonstration that train-time and execution-time safety *compose* under unobserved friction/mass shift — the mechanism behind E1's H4/H5, but sim-only Safety-Gym, not a real-hardware Cluster-D wrapper |
| [[2201.13248\|SafeAPT]] | *repertoire-based* safe adaptation (diverse sim policies via MAP-Elites + constrained Bayesian-optimization selection, no gradient fine-tune) | *0.0±0.0* safety violations on a real Kuka-arm hockey task over 8 replicates, high-reward policy found within minutes | the no-gradient safe-adaptation paradigm — selects among pre-learned policies where [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] gradient-updates a Fisher-protected one; data-efficient but bounded by the repertoire's coverage of real dynamics |
| [[2201.08355\|Sim-to-Lab-to-Real]] | *staged transfer-time* dual-shield (Hamilton-Jacobi reachability backup + PAC-Bayes generalization certificate across sim→lab→real) | violations cut *4–77%* vs prior safe RL; safety bound 0.367→0.794, real quadruped 0.867 safety rate | the staged-transfer face with a *formal generalization certificate* — sharpens H4 (transfer-time composes with adaptation-time) where [[2509.18648\|SPiDR]] uses a pessimistic ensemble, but certifies the transfer, not ongoing on-hardware adaptation |
| [[2010.15920\|Recovery-RL]] | *throughout-learning* recovery-policy decoupling (separate task + recovery policies, offline-pretrained safety critic defines recovery zones) | *2×–20×* better success-vs-violation trade-off in sim, *3×* on a physical dVRK robot | the foundational recovery-zone paradigm that decouples task from safety — distinct from [[2503.10949\|SCDA]]'s single cost-constrained policy and [[2409.19190\|RAIL]]'s reachability shield, but pre-deep-WM, no continual cross-domain guarantee |
| [[2605.09387\|NEXUS]] | *continual learning of the safety specification* (LLM + PDDL feasibility / LTL safety constraints that evolve from execution feedback) | safe-task SR *+20.07pp* and unsafe-task refusal *+10.37pp* via evolving the spec; 0.67% violation rate under jailbreak | the contrasting continual-safety paradigm — evolves the symbolic safety *spec* where [[2503.10949\|SCDA]]'s EWC protects capability *weights*; the symbolic-planner layer, not low-level RL, so it bounds task selection, not exploratory actions |
| [[2601.21845\|Constrained-Meta-RL]] | *provable test-time* safe adaptation (mixture policy blends a universally-safe policy with a high-reward candidate under Simultaneous-Slater) | ~*50%* lower test-time reward regret with no violations, plus a matching sample-complexity lower bound | the provable safe-adaptation-to-novel-domains mechanism with regret bounds — a meta-RL route to E1's successive-domain safety where [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] uses Fisher protection, but gridworld-only empirics, no hardware |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (cost-constrained continual adaptation hits zero violations where reward-only goes unsafe), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Composition holds across a scale sweep from a single wrapped engine to N successive domains.**
   - *Prediction*: wrapping [[2107.04034|RMA]]-style (D1) or [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]-style (D3) adaptation in [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s PCRPO+EWC holds the zero-violation result (violation-rate UCB below a pre-registered threshold, ≤6% at N≈50, rule-of-three) and preserves the 20%→60% adaptation gain at the single-domain level; scaling the same wrapped engine to N=k *successive* domains, the per-domain violation-UCB and a pre-registered normalized-forgetting bound both hold *simultaneously* through domain k, not traded off — single-engine and N-domain are two levels of one sweep, not two separate claims.
   - *Test*: (a) {unconstrained D-engine} vs {SCDA-wrapped} on a single OOD domain, report violations and SR; (b) chain N=k domain shifts on the SCDA-wrapped engine, report the per-domain violation-UCB and [[2604.19737|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]]'s normalized-forgetting metric per domain, with the per-domain trial budget pre-registered.
   - *Row*: SCDA (constrained adaptation) over AdaWorldPolicy / RMA (unconstrained engines) / Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP (normalized-forgetting protocol).
   - *Falsifier*: wrapping destroys the gain or admits violations at the single-domain level, **or** either the violation-UCB or normalized-forgetting exceeds its pre-registered threshold by domain k → composition fails outright or as domains accumulate.
2. **H2 — There is a safety-budget / adaptation-rate Pareto frontier.**
   - *Prediction*: tightening the safety-cost limit erodes the 20%→60% gain past a measurable point — a budget/rate frontier where the loosest limit that still guarantees zero violations maximizes SR.
   - *Test*: sweep the safety-cost limit; report SR vs limit, find the zero-violation knee, instantiated on [[2310.12567|Safety-Gymnasium]]'s violation-cost + budget protocol (SafePO's 16-algorithm baselines, MuJoCo — the canonical safe-RL violation/budget substrate, sim-only/single-domain so it backs the cost-knee axis, not the real-hardware composition).
   - *Row*: SCDA (constrained adaptation) / Safety-Gymnasium (violation/budget protocol).
   - *Falsifier*: SR is flat in the budget → there is no trade-off and the limit can be set arbitrarily tight.
3. **H3 — Composition failure, if it occurs, concentrates at the Fisher-vs-update-subspace interface, not uniformly across engines.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2107.04034|RMA]] adapts via a latent-extrinsics encoder with no gradient touching the Fisher-protected policy weights at all, so EWC protection is *vacuous* for it — there is no mechanism for it to matter. [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] adapts via 4 Hz LoRA, a low-rank subspace that may be near-orthogonal to the high-Fisher directions EWC penalizes, so protection is *mis-targeted* rather than vacuous. If H1 finds a composition failure, it concentrates in the LoRA case (mis-targeted protection lets violations through) rather than the RMA case (vacuous protection, but also nothing for EWC to break) — a specific mechanism prediction, not a generic "does it fail" question.
   - *Test*: measure the overlap between the EWC-penalized (high-Fisher) parameter subspace and each engine's actual update subspace (RMA's encoder-gradient direction; AdaWorldPolicy's LoRA subspace); correlate overlap with H1's per-engine violation/forgetting outcome.
   - *Row*: SCDA (Fisher-protected adaptation) / RMA (latent-extrinsics, no protected-weight update) / AdaWorldPolicy (LoRA, low-rank update).
   - *Falsifier*: violation/forgetting outcomes are uncorrelated with Fisher-subspace overlap → the failure mode (if any) is not a subspace-targeting problem and EWC's mechanism is not the explanation.
4. **H4 — Transfer-time and adaptation-time safety compose.**
   - *Prediction*: combining [[2509.18648|SPiDR]]'s transfer-time CMDP with [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s continual safe adaptation yields fewer total violations than either alone, because they bound different windows (initial transfer vs ongoing adaptation).
   - *Test*: {SPiDR only} vs {SCDA only} vs {both}; report violations across the transfer + adaptation timeline.
   - *Row*: SPiDR (transfer-time) + SCDA (adaptation-time).
   - *Falsifier*: combining adds no safety over the better single method → the windows overlap and one suffices.
5. **H5 — A model-free backstop covers safety-cost misspecification.**
   - *Prediction*: a wrong safety-cost function permits unsafe actions it doesn't penalize, but pairing with E2's reachability shield as a model-free backstop catches them — so safety doesn't rest on the learned cost alone.
   - *Test*: inject cost-model misspecification; {SCDA only} vs {SCDA + E2 shield}; report residual violations.
   - *Row*: SCDA (constrained adaptation) / RAIL (execution-time shield).
   - *Falsifier*: SCDA alone never admits violations under misspecification → the learned cost is sufficient and the backstop is redundant.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **"Zero violations" is unfalsifiable at small N** — a rare-event rate cannot be shown to be zero from a handful of real episodes; at N~50 the upper 95% bound on a *zero-observed-violation* rate is still ~6% (rule of three: 3/N), and the 20%→60% SR gain itself sits near the binomial noise floor. → Report the *upper confidence bound* on the violation rate, not "zero," and pre-register the per-domain trial budget / MDE so the budget/rate knee (H2) and the across-domain safety claim (H1's N-domain sweep) are powered before they are asserted.
> - **The safety-cost calibration drifts under domain shift** — SCDA's Fisher matrix and cost limits are calibrated in randomized-sim pretraining, but a successive-domain shift can move the real cost distribution away from that calibration, so a "zero-violation" guarantee calibrated on domain *k* need not hold on domain *k+1*. → Re-calibrate (or conformalize) the safety-cost threshold per domain and gate on a residual/coverage check before trusting the prior calibration; pair with E2's model-free shield (H5) as the backstop when calibration is stale.
> - **Tight safety cost can stall adaptation** — an over-strict limit may freeze the 20%→60% gain. → H2 maps the budget/rate frontier; set the limit at the loosest value that still guarantees zero violations.
> - **EWC protection can over-rigidify** — too-strong Fisher penalties block needed adaptation. → Tune the EWC weight per [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s schedule and monitor the forgetting/adaptation balance across domains (H1's N-domain sweep); H3 tests whether over-rigidification is a subspace-targeting artifact specific to the wrapped engine rather than a uniform EWC property.
> - **Cost model misspecification** — a wrong safety-cost function permits unsafe actions it doesn't penalize. → Pair with E2's reachability shield as a model-free backstop (H5) so safety doesn't rest on the learned cost alone.

### E2 — Reachability-Filtered Sim-to-Real Execution

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | E — Risk-Bounded Sim-to-Real Deployment |
| **Thesis** | That a hard reachability filter beats a soft penalty — at preserved success rate, even raising SR for weak policies — is now executed and won, on diffusion policies, on a real arm. What no filter does is shield a policy that is *itself changing*: a Cluster-D online-adapting policy whose actions shift mid-deployment, where a fixed-geometry occupancy model provably misses residual-gap hazards. The first principle: collision-freeness is a reachability property of forward occupancy, verifiable independent of the policy. The field assumes the static-policy hard-filter result settles safe execution. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] (method), [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] (method), [[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] (method), [[2303.04137\|Diffusion-Policy]] (method), [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] (survey), [[2507.10087\|Foundation-Robotics-Review]] (survey), [[2507.00435\|RoboEval]] (benchmark), [[2509.19555\|AnySafe]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | Hold [[2409.19190\|RAIL]]'s 0%-collision guarantee (vs 5–35% baseline IL) over a *changing* [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]]/[[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] policy mid-adaptation, at bounded SR cost; match [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]]'s +68% SR over soft CBF on a real Franka as the *static* baseline; close the residual-gap hazards a fixed-geometry filter misses ([[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]]); measure the collision-vs-SR frontier on [[2507.00435\|RoboEval]] (Safety/Stability class reports collisions + jerk + slips jointly with SR + stage-wise progression, 8 tasks × 3–5 spatial variations — differentiates policies at the same SR via collisions, ACT 4× lower Cartesian jerk than Pi-0.5) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: when a sim-trained imitation-learning policy hits the residual gap, it produces compounding errors and OOD actions — and the field's usual safety is *soft*: penalty terms or probabilistic constraints that bound risk in expectation, not absolutely.
- **Today's answers**: [[2409.19190|RAIL]] makes safety a *hard* guarantee — a continuous-time reachability filter checks whether the IL policy's plan is collision-free (computing the robot's forward occupancy), and if not, a model-based backup planner executes a guaranteed-safe alternative: 0% collision rate across all tasks vs 5–35% for baseline IL (Pick-Place RAIL+DP 0% vs [[2303.04137|Diffusion-Policy]] 27.2%), ~10-pp cost (68% vs 78% SR), 0.42 s/plan on a real Franka.
- **The opening**: the surviving on-thesis wedge is shielding a policy that is *itself adapting* mid-deployment — no runtime filter does this yet. The static headline is now *won* — [[2511.06385|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] (RAIL's diffusion-policy successor) runs set-based reachability over the action chunk with a formal guarantee on a real Franka FR3 (+68% SR over soft CBF, path-consistent braking) and [[2502.00935|Latent-Safety-Filters]] adds the weak-policy boost — and [[2505.00779|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] shows a *fixed-geometry* filter is *insufficient* under the residual gap, but none shields the adapting policy, the "physical feasibility / loop closure" gap [[2604.04974|Video-to-Control-Survey]] names.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: Collision-freeness is a reachability property of the robot's forward occupancy — verifiable in continuous time independent of the policy, so safety can be a hard runtime filter, not a learned objective. A guarantee that holds requires verification, not a penalty in expectation. [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s 0%-vs-5–35% result is the evidence that only verification eliminates collisions under OOD actions.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "learned soft safety suffices" — [[2511.06385|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]]'s +68% SR over CBF and [[2505.00779|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] already won the hard-vs-soft argument over IL/diffusion policies. The live assumption is that the *static-policy* hard-filter result settles safe execution. It does not: a Cluster-D online-adapting policy changes its actions mid-deployment, and a *fixed-geometry* occupancy model provably misses residual-gap hazards ([[2505.00779|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]]) — so the guarantee must hold over a *moving* policy and an *uncertain* geometry.
- **The bet**: A reachability shield holds the **0%-collision guarantee over a *changing* policy mid-adaptation** ([[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]]/[[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]), at a bounded SR cost during the adaptation window — where the static-policy baseline ([[2409.19190|RAIL]] 0% vs 5–35%; [[2511.06385|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] +68% over CBF on a real Franka) is the confirmatory floor — *and* an uncertainty-aware occupancy model closes the residual-gap hazards a fixed-geometry filter misses. Falsifiable: if collisions appear while the policy adapts (the guarantee assumed a fixed policy), or a fixed-geometry filter already catches the residual-gap hazards, the adapting-policy / uncertainty-aware extension buys nothing.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what kind of safety guarantee is provided* (hard reachability verification / unshielded soft IL / detect-then-shield conformal / adaptation-time constraint / pre-action verification / changing-policy target) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | Kind of safety guarantee | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] | *hard* continuous-time reachability filter + backup planner | 0% collisions (vs 5–35% IL), ~10-pp cost, 0.42 s/plan real Franka | the hard-guarantee anchor — shielding a *changing* (adapting) policy is open |
| [[2303.04137\|Diffusion-Policy]] | none (soft IL) | 78% Pick-Place SR / 27.2% collisions | the unshielded IL whose OOD actions the shield bounds |
| [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] | *hard* set-based reachability over the diffusion-policy action chunk + path-consistent braking | formal guarantee on real Franka FR3, +68% SR over soft CBF | the topThreat — wins hard-vs-soft on E2's own anchor policy class, but shields a *static* policy; the adapting-policy case (H1) is untouched |
| [[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] | *uncertainty-aware* latent-space reachability for OOD failures | avoids OOD failures a fixed-geometry filter misses | the on-thesis residual-gap version — shows fixed-geometry reachability is insufficient under the gap, but still a static policy |
| [[2506.09937\|SAFE]] | conformal runtime *detection* (not prevention) | <1 ms, zero-shot to unseen tasks | flags failure (E3), doesn't prevent collision — the detect half of detect-then-shield |
| [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] | adaptation-time safety *constraint* | 20%→60% at zero cost | bounds the *update* (E1), not the *action* — complementary surface |
| [[2605.22446\|Pre-VLA]] | *pre-action* verification of candidate chunks | F1 0.8303, +6.83 pp LIBERO closed-loop | pre-action verification complementing post-hoc shielding |
| [[2604.05484\|CoEnv]] | *twin-grounded* swept-collision verification before execution | 49% across 5 real multi-agent tasks; verification ablation 50%→20% | a geometric pre-check in a reconstructed twin — softer than [[2409.19190\|RAIL]]'s continuous-time guarantee, and twin-fidelity-bound (links to B1) |
| [[2604.17896\|Physical-Feasibility-VLA]] | *training-time* differentiable geometric feasibility loss (no runtime check) | SSR 22.00%→43.50% under perturbation; 40 demos beat 120 | bakes feasibility into the policy as a soft inductive bias — the train-time contrast to a hard runtime shield, with no deploy-time guarantee |
| [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] | none (fast online adaptation) | 3-step correction | the changing policy E2 must shield mid-adaptation |
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | none (online-adapting policy) | OOD recovery | shield target during no-reward updates |
| [[2604.04974\|Video-to-Control-Survey]] | (no method) names physical-feasibility gap | survey | the gap E2 fills with a hard guarantee |
| [[2509.19555\|AnySafe]] | *runtime-adaptive* latent reachability filter — constraint re-parameterized from images at deploy time, conformal-calibrated | adapts to novel/interpolated image constraints where fixed-constraint filters fail; α tunes conservativeness on a real Franka, no retraining | adapts the *constraint set* at runtime — the missing cell beyond [[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]]'s fixed-constraint latent filter — but the *policy* it shields is still static; H1's changing-policy target is the orthogonal axis it doesn't move |
| [[2602.23478\|refineCBF]] | *online-refined* hard HJ-reachability shield — **refines the value function itself in-the-loop** (REFINECBF converges to a safe-CBVF, Thm 1; SAFEADAPT-REFINECBF gives *monotonic* safety improvement / monotonically-decreasing false-positive rate, Thm 2), warm-started from an approximate value function | re-converges safe value functions in **under 2 s** on real ground robots + quadcopters; HJ-PATCH 9/10 robust safe rollouts (CPU); adapts to new obstacles / modified actuation limits / changed disturbances | the missing *shield-itself-adapts-online-with-a-formal-recovery-guarantee* cell — same Sylvia-Herbert HJ-reachability lineage as [[2409.19190\|RAIL]], landing in the bet's *second half* (an uncertainty-aware / adaptive-geometry occupancy model closing residual-gap hazards a fixed-geometry filter misses); distinct from [[2509.19555\|AnySafe]] (reparameterizes the constraint *set* from images, no value-function-recovery theorem), [[2505.00779\|Uncertainty-Latent-Safety-Filter]] (fixed-constraint latent), and [[2511.07899\|Ensemble-Safety-Filters]] (statistical conformal-switching, not value-fn refinement); add-not-replace — it adapts to *environment/dynamics* changes, **not a changing policy** (so it strengthens the uncertain-geometry half, not H1's changing-policy half), and runs on ground vehicles + quadcopters, not a manipulator |
| [[2511.07899\|Ensemble-Safety-Filters]] | *statistical* HJ-reachability shield — one-sided conformal switching between nominal and learned safe controllers, trajectory-level Beta-certified | 96% SR / 4% violation (ensemble) vs 46% nominal violations; best single filter 2–8% on a 10-D highway sim | adds a *probabilistic* trajectory-level guarantee over [[2409.19190\|RAIL]]/[[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]]'s *binary* hard verification — but proven on AV sim, not a real manipulator, and shields a static policy |
| [[2503.06736\|OSC-CBF]] | *task-consistent* CBF QP — full second-order dynamics, minimally-invasive operational-space safety on a real arm | 2.94 kHz for 168 constraints, scales to 1000+, preserves task tracking on a real Franka Panda | the sharpest *soft*-CBF foil for H4 — a task-consistent CBF the hard reachability shield must beat, vs the card's generic 'soft CBF' [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] already beat by +68% SR; still no reachability-grade collision guarantee |
| [[2605.26452\|Koopman-CBF-SAC]] | *learned-dynamics* CBF QP — Koopman-EDMD barrier with a residual-margin robustness term, critic trained on filtered actions | 0 violations on CartPole, 96.8% reduction (12.55%→0.40%) on Quadrotor 2D; degrades when Koopman error is large | a learned-CBF sibling fixing the nominal-vs-*filtered*-action inconsistency [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]] solves via path-consistent braking — distinct from analytic [[2503.06736\|OSC-CBF]] — but model-error-bound and not a continuous-time reachability guarantee |
| [[2309.08603\|Fallback-Safe-MPC]] | *fallback* safe-recovery-set controller — conformal runtime monitor triggers a guaranteed-safe recovery policy using only non-ML measurements | near-0% false-positive at 5% risk tolerance; recursive-feasibility + safety proofs prevent crashes under degraded vision | isolates the *backup-planner half* of [[2409.19190\|RAIL]]'s shield-plus-backup design — the safe-fallback controller — but is an MPC/AV-sim fallback, not the manipulation reachability shield, and the recovery is a fixed safe set, not a moving policy |
| [[2503.07404\|Safe-Robot-Foundation-Models]] | *post-policy* constraint-manifold projection (ATACOM tangent-space) over a pre-trained VLA/RFM — a third safety-guarantee KIND, enforcing formal safety *without* retraining or safety fine-tuning | operates 'without any violations' on a physical robot; maintained and *improved* SR as fine-tuning progressed while the unsafe baseline degraded | the third safety-guarantee mechanism beside RAIL/PACS (HJ reachability) and [[2503.06736\|OSC-CBF]]/[[2605.26452\|Koopman-CBF-SAC]] (CBF-QP) — and the only candidate holding the guarantee *while the policy changes* (here offline fine-tuning progression), a data-point adjacent to H1's changing-policy clause; but its 'change' is offline fine-tuning not no-reward online adaptation, and its task is air-hockey, not the manipulation collision-vs-SR frontier |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a hard reachability shield drives collisions to 0% where soft penalties can't), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — The 0%-collision guarantee survives an *adapting* policy.**
   - *Prediction*: running [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s filter over a [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] (D2) or [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] (D3) online-adapting policy keeps the 0%-collision guarantee while the policy is *changing*, at a bounded SR cost during adaptation.
   - *Test*: shield an adapting policy; report collisions + SR cost during the adaptation window on [[2507.00435|RoboEval]]'s Safety/Stability metrics (collisions + slips reported jointly with SR + stage-wise progression, 8 tasks × 3–5 variations — the collision-vs-SR measurement substrate).
   - *Row*: RAIL (hard shield) over Learning on the Fly / AdaWorldPolicy (changing policies) / RoboEval (collision-vs-SR substrate).
   - *Falsifier*: collisions appear while the policy adapts → the shield's guarantee assumes a fixed policy.
2. **H2 — There is an intervention-rate / SR-cost frontier set by the reachability margin.**
   - *Prediction*: tightening the reachability margin trades intervention rate against the ~10-pp SR cost across task families, and intervention falls as the policy improves across epochs ([[2409.19190|RAIL]] already reports this).
   - *Test*: sweep the reachability margin; report intervention rate vs SR cost per task family on [[2507.00435|RoboEval]]'s 8 tasks × 3–5 spatial variations (per-policy collisions + SR jointly, so the intervention/SR-cost trade is measurable across task families at the same SR).
   - *Row*: RAIL (hard shield) / RoboEval (per-task collision-vs-SR).
   - *Falsifier*: intervention and SR-cost are uncoupled from the margin → the margin is not the control knob.
3. **H3 — The shield *raises* SR for weak policies, *costs* SR for strong ones.**
   - *Prediction*: characterizing the +16 pp SSucc boost, hard safety improves weak-policy SR (by pruning doomed trajectories) and costs ~10 pp for strong policies — a policy-strength-dependent sign on the SR effect.
   - *Test*: stratify by policy strength; report the SR effect (boost vs cost) per strength bin on [[2507.00435|RoboEval]] (differentiates policies at the same SR via collisions/jerk) cross-checked against [[2512.22539|VLA-Arena]]'s CBDDL Safety suite (cumulative safety cost reported jointly with SR under L0–L2 perturbation — the safety-cost-vs-SR trade-off it names directly).
   - *Row*: RAIL (hard shield) / RoboEval (collision-vs-SR) / VLA-Arena (safety-cost-vs-SR) over Diffusion Policy (unshielded IL).
   - *Falsifier*: the shield always costs SR → it never boosts weak policies and the +16 pp is an artifact.
4. **H4 (confirmatory — PACS won it) — Hard verification beats a soft penalty on collisions under the same OOD.**
   - *Prediction*: under the OOD-action generator ([[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s 14 factors), a soft safety penalty cannot reach 0% collisions while a hard filter does — reproducing [[2511.06385|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]]'s +68% SR over soft CBF as the static baseline the adapting-policy bet (H1) builds on.
   - *Test*: {soft penalty} vs {hard shield} under COLOSSEUM-perturbed deployment on a static policy; report collision rate, pairing [[2402.08191|THE-COLOSSEUM]]'s SR-drop generator with [[2512.22539|VLA-Arena]]'s CBDDL Safety suite (cumulative safety cost + SR under L0–L2 distractor perturbation — the safety-cost endpoint to COLOSSEUM's SR-drop).
   - *Row*: Path-Consistent Safety Filter (hard, +68% over CBF) / RAIL (hard shield) / VLA-Arena (safety-cost under perturbation) vs Diffusion Policy (soft IL).
   - *Falsifier*: the soft penalty reaches 0% collisions → even the conceded hard-vs-soft result fails to reproduce.
5. **H5 — Pre-action verification + reachability shield beats either alone.**
   - *Prediction*: combining [[2605.22446|Pre-VLA]]'s pre-action chunk verification with [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s reachability shield catches more unsafe actions than either alone — pre-screening reduces shield interventions and the shield catches what pre-screening misses.
   - *Test*: {Pre-VLA only} vs {RAIL only} vs {both}; report collisions + intervention rate.
   - *Row*: Pre-VLA (pre-action) + RAIL (hard shield).
   - *Falsifier*: combining adds nothing → the two catch the same actions and one suffices.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **A conformal-calibrated shield's guarantee decalibrates over an *adapting* policy** — filters like [[2509.19555|AnySafe]] and [[2511.07899|Ensemble-Safety-Filters]] calibrate their switching/violation bound on a fixed policy's action distribution, but H1's online-adapting policy shifts that distribution mid-deployment, so the conformal coverage assumed at calibration need not hold during the adaptation window. → Re-calibrate the shield's threshold online as the policy updates (or use [[2602.23478|refineCBF]]'s in-the-loop value-function recovery), and report empirical violation coverage *measured across the adaptation window*, not the static-policy calibration figure — a fixed-policy 0%-collision guarantee is not evidence for the changing-policy case.
> - **Reachability filtering adds latency** — continuous-time occupancy checks may slow the control loop. → [[2409.19190|RAIL]] runs at 0.42 s per plan on a real Franka, so it is tractable; precompute occupancy and bound the per-step check budget.
> - **Conservative shields over-intervene** — too-tight margins freeze the robot. → H2 maps the intervention/SR-cost frontier; set the margin at the loosest value that preserves 0% collisions.
> - **Shield needs a model of obstacles** — reachability requires environment geometry the residual gap may misestimate. → Ground the occupancy model in B1's reconstruction fidelity and fall back to E3's conformal detector where geometry is uncertain; or refine the shield *online* as the geometry changes — [[2602.23478|refineCBF]] supplies the formal answer, re-converging the safe value function in under 2 s as new obstacles / actuation limits / disturbances appear (SAFEADAPT-REFINECBF guarantees monotonic safety improvement), the uncertain-geometry half of the bet's second clause.

### E3 — Conformal Runtime Failure Detection

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | E — Risk-Bounded Sim-to-Real Deployment |
| **Thesis** | Detecting failures from successful runs alone, with a conformal FPR guarantee, is now consensus. What survives is narrower and *structural*, not merely untested: a bound proven for smooth (CBF/CLF) feedback controllers has a proof-level reason it might not survive a discontinuous backup switch, and a detector built on one architecture's internal representations has no natural reason to transfer where a shared-output-space score does. The first principle: a failure is an OOD event against the success manifold, so success-modeling plus a sound cutoff suffices. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2606.15366\|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] (method), [[2604.20472\|TDQC]] (method), [[2510.09459\|FIPER]] (method), [[2410.04640\|Sentinel]] (method), [[2506.09937\|SAFE]] (method), [[2503.08558\|FAIL-Detect]] (method), [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] (method), [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (benchmark), [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]] (benchmark), [[2309.08603\|Fallback-Safe-MPC]] (method), [[2212.14020\|System-Level-OOD-Robotics]] (survey) |
| **Key targets** | A detect-then-act loop (flag → [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] backup / [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] safe-adapt) that *preserves* the conformal FPR bound while cutting user-facing failures; cross-architecture transfer of [[2506.09937\|SAFE]]'s internal-feature detector backstopped by [[2503.08558\|FAIL-Detect]]'s success-only density; reproduce [[2510.09459\|FIPER]]'s no-failure-data conformal detection (beating the FAIL-Detect lineage on earlier + more accurate prediction) as the settled baseline; ground the localization→targeted-response endpoint on [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]] (9,440 error trajectories + 78,623 QA, 3-level detection/identification/locating/explanation taxonomy, sim+real test sets, real closed-loop SR 47.5%→61.25%); [[2606.16690\|PATCH]] FPR 0.04/0.07 at TPR 0.97, localized intervention + verified resume at 55.2 Hz |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: detecting failures from successes alone is settled, so a detector *trained on labeled failures* is the wrong-distribution mistake the field already corrected — the open problem is what to *do* with the flag while keeping the guarantee, and whether the strongest internal-feature detectors transfer across policies.
- **Today's answers**: [[2510.09459|FIPER]] does the bet near-verbatim — no failure-labeled data, conformal calibration on successful rollouts only, OOD + action-uncertainty scores — and explicitly beats the FAIL-Detect lineage on *earlier* and *more accurate* prediction; [[2410.04640|Sentinel]] is the success-only root (runtime monitoring of consistency + progress, CoRL'24); [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]] (~78% sim / ~72% real, success-only logpZO) and [[2506.09937|SAFE]] (internal-feature conformal, <1 ms, zero-shot, FPR guarantee) are the now-settled instances.
- **The opening**: the surviving leverage is narrower than a fully-open detect-then-act question — [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] already proves bound-preservation for CBF/CLF-structured backups, and [[2604.20472|TDQC]] already probes cross-architecture transfer of a calibrated, non-internal-feature score. What is left: (1) whether bound-preservation extends to a conformal flag wired to a *non*-CBF/CLF backup ([[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s discrete backup, [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s safe-adapt) — a control-substrate generalization of an existing theorem, not a from-scratch proof — and (2) whether [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s internal features specifically transfer, since TDQC's Q-function transfer does not settle the internal-feature case; [[2602.01515|RAPT]]'s root-cause diagnosis (AUROC 0.92 sim, 1.63 ms) and [[2605.30834|Hide-and-Seek]]'s step-level localization (0.852 bACC) point at the targeted-response half H5 probes.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A failure is just anything that looks unlike a successful run — an OOD event — so you can spot it from successful runs alone; pairing that with a conformal threshold (a calibration that caps how often you cry wolf, with a guarantee that holds even on a small calibration set) bounds the false-alarm rate. Detection needs a model of what success looks like plus a sound cutoff, not examples of failures. [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]'s ~78%/72% from successes only is the evidence that success-modeling generalizes where failure-labeling cannot.
- **Assumption being challenged**: *Not* "detection needs failure-labeled data" — the field dropped that by [[2410.04640|Sentinel]] (CoRL'24) and [[2510.09459|FIPER]] (NeurIPS'25); settled. Not a belief anyone holds about the two narrower claims either — [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] and [[2604.20472|TDQC]] never claim their results generalize beyond what they tested; they simply haven't run the broader case. But two structural reasons make the broader case genuinely uncertain, not just unrun. (1) Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF's bound-preservation proof explicitly assumes the policy $\pi_r(x)$ is Lipschitz continuous in state — its own Assumption 3, load-bearing for the Grönwall-inequality trajectory-sensitivity bound the whole method rests on. [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s backup is a discrete switch between an IL policy and an entirely different model-based planner — discontinuous by construction — so the proof's own hypothesis is violated, not merely untested, for exactly the backup this direction needs. [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s episodic PCRPO+EWC update, by contrast, is the *same kind* of across-episode policy update the theorem's regime already covers, so that half is a plausible, likely-to-hold extension, not a genuinely open one. (2) TDQC's cross-architecture transfer result is carried by its black-box variant, which scores only action probabilities/logits — a representation every VLA shares by construction. [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s detector scores internal hidden-state features, which live in each architecture's own independently-trained latent basis with no canonical cross-model correspondence — so TDQC's transfer result says nothing about whether a representation-specific detector like SAFE's transfers the same way.
- **The bet**: (i, front line) [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]]'s bound-preservation-through-the-loop theorem — proven under a Lipschitz-continuous-policy assumption — does **not** trivially extend to [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s discrete IL-policy/backup-planner switch: empirical FPR/coverage measurably degrades below the ~100% the theorem reports for smooth CBF/CLF controllers, unless the switch is explicitly re-derived as a hybrid/non-smooth extension. The confirmatory half: the same theorem *does* extend cleanly to [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s episodic PCRPO+EWC update, preserving FPR within 2 pp of nominal while cutting user-facing failures by at least 30%. (ii) [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s internal-feature detector does **not** transfer across architectures — even though TDQC's black-box Q-function/logit score does — because the two representations live in structurally different spaces (shared output space vs private per-architecture latent basis); pairing SAFE with [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]'s policy-agnostic density score recovers the detection TDQC's transfer result does not predict. Falsifiable: if the CBF/CLF bound survives the discrete RAIL switch without modification, or SAFE's internal features transfer across architectures as cleanly as TDQC's Q-function, the structural distinction is not real and both extensions were free all along.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table on the axis the direction turns on — *what the detector learns from and what it outputs* (success-only density / internal-feature conformal / step-level localization / detection + root-cause / prediction-error / action+state anomaly / pre-action verification) — plus key result and what each leaves open:

| System | What it learns from / outputs | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2510.09459\|FIPER]] | *no failure data* (conformal on successes, OOD + action-uncertainty) | beats the FAIL-Detect lineage on *earlier* + *more accurate* prediction | the topThreat — does E3's bet near-verbatim, leaving only the detect-then-act FPR-preservation (H3) and cross-arch transfer (H4) open |
| [[2410.04640\|Sentinel]] | *successes only* (runtime monitoring of consistency + progress, STAC) | success-only failure monitoring (CoRL'24) | the success-only root that settled "no failure labels needed" — diagnosis, not a closed detect-then-act loop |
| [[2506.09937\|SAFE]] | policy *internal features* + functional Conformal Prediction | <1 ms overhead, zero-shot unseen-task, FPR guarantee | features are policy-internal, living in each architecture's independently-trained latent basis with no canonical cross-model correspondence — structurally distinct from TDQC's shared-output-space score, so cross-architecture transfer (H4) is a genuinely open structural question, not merely untested |
| [[2608.04246\|SAFECAST]] | augments [[2506.09937\|SAFE]]'s probe *training* and *calibration* sets with visual + language **contrast-set** trajectories (DTW-filtered for diversity), targeting the calibration-shift assumption directly | F1/ROC-AUC beat SAFE across DROID real + LIBERO sim; OpenVLA F1 0.922→0.959 (visual-only → multimodal contrast sets); sim-trained + real-contrast-calibrated probe beats train+calibrate on a small real-only set | closes the exact gap in this cluster's own Risk-1 ("calibration rests on representative success data") with a concrete mechanism and numbers, but the fix is *re-calibration under anticipated shift types*, not a guarantee the shift at deploy-time was anticipated — doesn't touch H3's detect-then-act bound-preservation or H4's cross-architecture transfer |
| [[2503.08558\|FAIL-Detect]] | *successes only* (flow-based logpZO + conformal) | ~78% sim / ~72% real, top in 10/16 sim & 8/12 hardware | the policy-agnostic density backstop for H4 — coarser than internal-feature |
| [[2605.30834\|Hide-and-Seek]] | trajectory-only labels → step-level *localization* (LSTM + contrastive) | 0.852 bACC, +11.7% bACC / +15.0% TWA unseen real, 2,000× faster than VLM | step-level localization sharpens SAFE's label noise — coarse supervision, not success-only |
| [[2602.01515\|RAPT]] | model-predictive OOD detection + *root-cause diagnosis* | AUROC 0.92 sim / 75% real recall at zero nominal FP, 1.63 ms | detection *plus* diagnosis — humanoid sim-to-real specific |
| [[2604.16677\|ReconVLA]] | conformal action-level + Mahalanobis state-level anomaly over frozen VLA | AUC 0.922 (π₀), halts 16/20 before hardware limits | detection without touching the policy — two-level, not internal-feature |
| [[2603.11106\|RC-NF]] | *successes only* (robot-conditioned normalizing flow) | <100 ms, +8% AUC / +10% AP on LIBERO-Anomaly-10 | the success-only density detector, RC-NF's flow to FAIL-Detect's logpZO |
| [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] | world-model *prediction error* as an OOD signal | OOD recovery | the detector D3 uses, complementary to internal-feature detection |
| [[2605.22446\|Pre-VLA]] | *pre-action* chunk verification | F1 0.8303, +6.83 pp LIBERO closed-loop | pre-action verification complementing post-hoc detection (links to E2) |
| [[2603.04029\|Self-Adapting-RL]] | prediction-residual OOD detection | DreamerV3 residual | the trigger E3 generalizes with a conformal guarantee |
| [[2309.08603\|Fallback-Safe-MPC]] | conformal *runtime monitor* wired to a *fallback controller* (detect-then-act, recursive-feasibility + FNR guarantee) | FPR near 0% at 5% risk, sample-efficient (less data than retraining), prevents crashes/runway-excursions under degraded vision | the closest prior to H3's detect-then-*act* loop, but the FPR bound is asserted at the monitor — never shown to *survive through* the fallback action (the distribution shift H3 isolates) |
| [[2606.15366\|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] | episodic framework *preserving* a conformal guarantee **through** a closed feedback loop — quantifies the policy-induced distribution shift via a closed-loop-sensitivity *distribution-shift budget*, uses Adversarially-Robust Conformal Prediction, maintains CBF/CLF stability/safety across *iterative policy updates* | **100%** empirical coverage + stability (inverted pendulum); **99.8%** score-coverage / **100%** safety (multi-obstacle maze); converges where 'calibrate-once' baselines fail under shift | the formal machinery H3's strongest row ([[2309.08603\|Fallback-Safe-MPC]]) explicitly lacks — it proves the FPR/coverage bound *survives through the action* where the policy's own actions shift the trajectory distribution away from calibration, stated as a control-theoretic theorem rather than asserted at a monitor; adds to (does not replace) Fallback-Safe MPC (the fixed-fallback existence proof) — but the proof requires the policy to be Lipschitz continuous in state (its own Assumption 3); [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s discrete IL-policy/backup-planner switch violates that assumption by construction, so extending the bound to RAIL's backup (not [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s continuous episodic update) is the genuinely open half of H3 |
| [[2310.17552\|Sirius-Runtime]] | *human interventions as implicit failure labels* + cVAE dynamics → *anticipatory* future-error flag → human-gated intervention | +32% sim / +40% real SR, +16% human-effort efficiency over IIL baselines | success-side, anticipatory (pre-failure) flagging without explicit failure data — but intervention-labeled and non-conformal, so no FPR guarantee like FAIL-Detect/SAFE |
| [[2207.12380\|p-Quantile-Anomaly-Detector]] | *task-relevant* mispredictions (planning-cost p-quantile) → FPR/FNR-bounded flag | AUROC 0.946; data-free calibration holds empirical FPR to 3.4% for a 5% target | the cleanest pre-robotics instance of E3's conformal-FPR axis + benign-vs-critical distinction — but trajectory-prediction in AVs, not a manipulation/policy detector the robot rows turn on |
| [[2309.13475\|System-Level-Anomaly-Detector]] | reachability-labeled *system-level* (not component-level) OOD images → flag → safety fallback | 90.3–97.4% recall on unseen airports; shrinks Backward-Reachable-Tube (25.75%→18.24%) | the 'failure = system-level OOD' framing as a concrete detect-then-mitigate, an angle all current rows (component/policy-internal) miss — but vision-controller specific, fallback is fixed not the conformal-bounded act-half |
| [[2602.16182\|WM-Failure-Classifier]] | world-model *latent prediction error* + conformal calibration → 3-way success / known-failure / *OOD* split → corrective prompt | >90% 3-way accuracy at a 90% conformal quantile; flags 1–3 s before human observers, deployed on Spot | adds conformal calibration + the known-vs-novel partition on top of AdaWorldPolicy's bare prediction-error signal — but the act-half is a prompt, not the FPR-preserving loop H3 needs |
| [[2604.13788\|Failure-ID-Filtering]] | spatially-aware conformal anomaly + VLM *semantic filter* → benign-vs-task-critical separation (IL manipulation) | 70.50% AUROC / 62.90% F1 on BotFails (+5.30% AUROC); semantic filter prevents a 23% TNR loss | the manipulation analog of p-Quantile's task-relevance, the FPR-cutting benign-vs-critical mechanism FAIL-Detect's binary success-only detector lacks — but adds a VLM filter, not a single conformal cutoff |
| [[2606.16690\|PATCH]] | action-chunk-conditioned *latent patch innovation* (execution-corridor) → *localized* flag → intervene + state-preserving resume | balanced acc 0.96; FPR 0.04 (static) / 0.07 (transient) at TPR 0.97; 90% resume accuracy at 55.2 Hz real-robot | the sharpest false-positive-control + a concrete localize→targeted-response (H5) beyond Hide-and-Seek's *offline* localization — but no conformal FPR guarantee, and not transferred across architectures (H4) |
| [[2604.20472\|TDQC]] | episode-level delayed success labels → TD-calibrated *Q-function* failure score (white- *and* black-box) | lower sequential Brier + higher ROC-AUC across OpenVLA / UniVLA / π₀ / π₀-FAST; +13% LIBERO-10 SR via test-time action search | directly probes H4 — whether a calibrated success-signal transfers *across VLA backbones* — with a logit-only black-box variant as the policy-agnostic case SAFE's internal features leave open; but its transfer is carried by scoring action probabilities/logits, a representation every VLA shares by construction, which says nothing about whether SAFE's private-latent-basis representation transfers the same way; also needs failure outcomes for calibration, not success-only |
| [[2606.18043\|VFD]] | flow-VLA *velocity-field disagreement* (ensemble epistemic UQ) → failure flag → data-efficient adaptation (SAVE) | Spearman −0.71 with SR; failure TPR 79% / 67% detection; SAVE reaches 67.1% SR at 22–50% fewer demos | extends the success-confidence-flag axis to the flow / π₀ policy class ReconVLA touches, and instantiates detect-then-*adapt* where most rows only flag — but UQ via ensembles, not a conformal cutoff |
