| [[2407.08735\|AESOP]] | fast LLM-embedding anomaly + slow generative LLM → *reasoning-driven* recovery plan (latency-aware MPC) | 100% recovery vs 23% Fallback-Safe MPC and 15% naive; ~10 Hz real quadrotor | the reasoning-driven targeted-response (H3/H5) where the *response* realizes detection value, distinct from Fallback-Safe MPC's *fixed* fallback — but no conformal FPR guarantee on the embedding monitor |
| [[2504.11170\|Sparse-MAF-AAE]] | *successes only* (torque-only MAF + adversarial AE, unsupervised) → real-time anomaly flag | AUROC 89.23% at 100 Hz; 0.86 ms inference on embedded hardware (+15.57% over LSTM-VAE) | the concrete <1 ms success-only baseline grounding SAFE's <1 ms claim + H2's lead-time-vs-overhead leg — but torque-only and non-conformal, coarser than internal-feature or flow density |
| [[2212.14020\|System-Level-OOD-Robotics]] | *survey* — frames 'failure = OOD inside the closed robotic feedback loop' (system- vs component-level), real-time / episodic / data-lifecycle | six research questions; explicitly calls for the missing *closed-loop-OOD benchmark* | the conceptual basis of E3's first-principles thesis + the Benchmark-Gaps row — names the gap H3/H4 fall into, but supplies framing not a method or detector |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a conformal success-only detector flags sim2real failures at <1 ms with no failure labels), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 (confirmatory — FIPER/Sentinel settled it) — Success-only detection beats failure-labeled on held-out sim2real failures.**
   - *Prediction*: success-only OOD detection ([[2510.09459|FIPER]], [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]) beats a failure-labeled detector on *held-out sim2real-induced* failures (the ones the labeled set can't contain) — reproducing FIPER's edge over the FAIL-Detect lineage as the settled baseline H3/H4 build on.
   - *Test*: {success-only} vs {failure-labeled} on held-out sim2real failures; report balanced accuracy.
   - *Row*: FIPER (no-failure-data conformal) / FAIL-Detect (success-only) / Sentinel (success-only root).
   - *Falsifier*: the failure-labeled detector wins on held-out failures → even the conceded success-only result fails to reproduce.
2. **H2 — Internal-feature detection fires earlier than prediction-error at lower overhead.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s internal-feature signal detects failures earlier and at lower overhead than [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s world-model prediction error on the same failures.
   - *Test*: {internal-feature} vs {prediction-error} on the same failures; report detection lead-time and overhead.
   - *Row*: SAFE (internal-feature) vs AdaWorldPolicy (prediction-error).
   - *Falsifier*: prediction-error detects earlier/cheaper → internal features are not the better signal.
3. **H3 (front line) — Bound-preservation degrades under RAIL's discrete backup switch but survives SCDA's continuous episodic update.**
   - *Prediction*: wiring E3's conformal flag to [[2409.19190|RAIL]]'s discrete IL-policy/backup-planner switch (E2) breaks or measurably loosens the FPR/coverage bound relative to the ~100% [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]] reports for smooth CBF/CLF controllers, because the switch violates the theorem's Lipschitz-continuity assumption (its own Assumption 3) by construction; wiring the same flag to [[2503.10949|SCDA]]'s episodic PCRPO+EWC update (E1) — an across-episode update the theorem's regime already covers — preserves the bound within 2 pp of nominal.
   - *Test*: {detection only} vs {detect-then-act via RAIL} vs {detect-then-act via SCDA}; report empirical FPR/coverage for each, instrumenting the policy-induced distribution shift via [[2606.15366|Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF]]'s closed-loop-sensitivity *distribution-shift budget* (Adversarially-Robust CP) and explicitly checking whether its Lipschitz-continuity assumption holds for each backup.
   - *Row*: SAFE (internal-feature) / RAIL (discrete backup, the uncertain half) / SCDA (continuous episodic update, the confirmatory half) / Robust Conformal CBF/CLF (bound-preservation theorem + its Lipschitz assumption) / Fallback-Safe MPC (fixed-fallback, bound asserted at the flag).
   - *Falsifier*: RAIL's discrete switch preserves the bound as cleanly as SCDA's continuous update → the Lipschitz-continuity assumption was not load-bearing after all, and both extensions were free; or SCDA's bound degrades too → the theorem doesn't transfer past its original CBF/CLF demonstration at all.
4. **H4 — SAFE's internal features fail to transfer across architectures for a structural reason TDQC's transfer result does not predict.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s internal-hidden-state features don't transfer across architectures — because each architecture's hidden-state basis is independently trained with no canonical cross-model correspondence — even though [[2604.20472|TDQC]]'s black-box Q-function/logit score, which scores a representation every VLA shares by construction (action probabilities), transfers cleanly across OpenVLA/UniVLA/π₀/π₀-FAST. Pairing SAFE with [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]'s/[[2603.11106|RC-NF]]'s policy-agnostic density score recovers the detection SAFE's features miss.
   - *Test*: transfer SAFE's detector across architectures; separately transfer TDQC's black-box score across the same architectures; {SAFE internal-feature only} vs {+ density backstop} vs {TDQC black-box}; report detection per architecture pair.
   - *Row*: SAFE (internal-feature) / TDQC (shared-output-space score, transfers) / FAIL-Detect (policy-agnostic density) / RC-NF (flow density).
   - *Falsifier*: SAFE's internal features transfer as cleanly as TDQC's score → the output-space/latent-basis distinction is not the structural reason and both are equally transferable representations.
5. **H5 — Step-level localization + root-cause beats binary detection for response.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2605.30834|Hide-and-Seek]]'s step-level localization and [[2602.01515|RAPT]]'s root-cause diagnosis enable a *targeted* response (which step, which cause) that recovers more SR than binary detect-then-halt.
   - *Test*: {binary detect} vs {localize + diagnose → targeted response}; report recovered SR on [[2505.12224|RoboFAC]]'s localization + identification + correction benchmark (3-level failure taxonomy, sim+real test sets, real closed-loop SR 47.5%→61.25% with low-level correction — though it is failure-labeled, where H1's detection argues for success-only).
   - *Row*: Hide-and-Seek (localization) / RAPT (root-cause) / RoboFAC (localization+correction closed-loop SR) vs FAIL-Detect (binary success-only).
   - *Falsifier*: targeted response matches binary halt → localization/diagnosis add no actionable value.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Conformal validity needs a calibration set of successes** — the FPR guarantee rests on representative success data. → Calibrate per [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]'s success-only protocol on the deployment distribution and re-calibrate on domain shift to keep coverage. [[2608.04246|SAFECAST]] gives this a concrete mechanism — augmenting both probe training and calibration with visual + language contrast-set trajectories closes SAFE's calibration-mismatch gap (multimodal F1 0.922→0.959) and shows a sim-trained, real-contrast-calibrated probe beats a small real-only calibration set — but the fix pre-supposes the anticipated shift types cover the deployed one; an *unanticipated* shift still breaks the bound.
> - **Internal-feature detectors are model-specific** — [[2506.09937|SAFE]]'s features are policy-internal and may not transfer across architectures. → Pair with [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]]'s/[[2603.11106|RC-NF]]'s policy-agnostic density score as a model-independent fallback (H4).
> - **Detection without response is inert** — flagging a failure the system can't avoid adds no safety. → H3's detect-then-act loop wires the flag to E2's shield or E1's safe adaptation so detection drives a bounded response.
> - **Both surviving sub-claims extend very recent (2026) theorems and could be preempted before this direction runs** — this is the highest-preemption-risk direction in the doc: if either source group publishes the RAIL-style discrete-backup extension or the SAFE-cross-architecture-transfer result first, the direction closes. → The mitigation is structural, not just speed: H3's discrete-switch prediction is a *negative* result relative to the smooth-controller case (the bound degrades, it doesn't just get re-derived to hold), and a negative finding grounded in a violated proof assumption is far less preemptable than a routine confirmatory extension — run H3/H4 as falsification attempts first, not as races to confirm.

---

## Cross-Cutting Themes

> [!tip] The Reality Gap Is Bidirectional — and Real→Sim Is Now the Binding Constraint
> B1, B3, and A1 all turn on the same inversion, and the same floor: the forward gap Cluster A attacks is bounded by how faithfully reality was run *backward* into the simulator *and* by the simulator's own error at the true parameters — per [[2608.05948|GAUGE]], inversion alone cannot close that second term, so perfect inversion narrows the forward gap but does not zero it. [[2512.19562|REALM]] names the real-to-sim gap as a distinct object; [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]] measures it (r=0.915 vs [[2511.04831|Isaac-Lab]] 0.649) and its ablation shows the gap closes only when appearance ($\phi^\star$) and dynamics ($\psi^\star$) are inverted *together*; B3 pushes the inversion one level deeper, recovering the constitutive *law* not its parameters; A1's neural-rendering-in-the-loop is the same inversion applied to semantics. The field spent a decade making sims transfer forward (Cluster A); the leverage has migrated to inversion (Cluster B), and even C1's correlation stress-test and C2's portfolio depend on how well each sim inverts reality. The migration is capped, not unlimited: the Formal Framing's bound reads $|\text{Gap}_{\text{S2R}}| \geq \text{Gap}_{\text{R2S}} + \text{Gap}_{\text{floor}}$ qualitatively — GAUGE measures the floor term at an order of magnitude on dynamic contact and volumetric deformation, even given ground-truth-calibrated parameters.

> [!tip] Sim Fidelity Is Not Transfer Quality — the Proxy You Optimize Is *Anti-Correlated* With the Goal
> This is the sharpest contrarian result in the doc, and it ties A3, B3, and C1 to one claim: **optimizing fidelity actively destroys transfer.** A3's [[2604.02523|Tune-to-Learn]] is the cleanest case — the controller gains with the *lowest* system-identification error produce the *worst* sim-to-real transfer, so minimizing the standard sysID objective moves you away from the goal. B3's [[2510.11689|Phys2Real]] shows the same inversion for randomization — DR's distribution-*marginalizing* fidelity reaches only 23% on OOD mass while *estimating* the single true parameter hits 57%, so averaging over the physics you could have identified is strictly worse. C1's [[2502.10694|UDA-Simulation-Study]] names the failure outright as "negative adaptation" — under shift, the adaptation that should help can do *worse than no adaptation*, flipping the sign of the relationship. B1's [[2608.05948|GAUGE]] sharpens the mechanism behind the mismatch: across 22 measurement-grounded real task families, video world models hit R²=0.99 fitting a pendulum's oscillation *form* while inferring a period 73–93% too long, and physics engines given the *correct* calibrated parameters still show order-of-magnitude errors on dynamic contact and volumetric deformation — so the proxy fails not only because fidelity is the wrong optimization target (Tune-to-Learn, Phys2Real) but because visual/structural plausibility and physical-parameter accuracy are separable capabilities, and current tools optimize the former while the latter is what transfer needs. The cross-cutting lesson is not the mild "fidelity is a confounded surrogate" — it is the strong claim that **the realism you optimize is, on these axes, negatively coupled to the transfer you actually want; measure transfer directly, because the proxy points the wrong way.**

> [!tip] Sim-to-Real Evaluation Is Becoming Statistical Inference, Not Accuracy Engineering
> C1 and C2 form one diagnose-and-route → infer pipeline, and B2 inherits the reframing for data. C1 *diagnoses and routes* — it shows in-distribution r ([[2405.05941|SIMPLER]] >0.85, [[2605.06311|VISER]] 0.92) is a validity artifact that must be re-measured per factor under shift, then turns the per-factor result into a deployment gate that routes which biased sim to trust per perturbation. C2 *infers* — [[2604.24018|Sim2Real-Betting]] (70–100% win rate) and [[2510.04354|SureSim]] (provable CIs, 20–25% fewer real trials) extract provable real bounds from *imperfect* sims by treating estimation as variance reduction, consuming C1's per-factor weights. The question shifts from "how accurate is my sim?" to "is this correlation valid and which sim do I deploy per factor (C1), and what can I provably infer from it (C2)?" — and B2's twin becomes a data engine under the same inference logic.

> [!tip] Differentiable Rendering + Physics Collapse System-ID Into Gradient Descent
> A1, B1, and B3 all ride the same capability: appearance and dynamics are now recovered end-to-end by gradient, not hand-tuning. [[2503.17973|PhysTwin]] and [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim-GS]] jointly optimize geometry + physical parameters + appearance from video; B3's [[2604.27367|DOT-Sim]] makes the optical-tactile simulator differentiable and calibrates constitutive parameters from few demos; B3's [[2510.11689|Phys2Real]] fuses VLM priors with online estimation; B3's [[2304.14369|NCLaw]] pushes the same differentiable-MPM machinery one level deeper, learning the constitutive *law* rather than its parameters. A1's neural-rendering-in-the-loop, B1's joint reconstruction, and B3's parameter-sysID-to-learned-law progression are three faces of the same collapse — manual sysID is being replaced by differentiable recovery of $\phi$, $\psi$, and now the functional form of the dynamics itself, and D2 reuses the same differentiable machinery for exact-gradient test-time adaptation.

> [!tip] The Reality Gap Has a Temporal Axis — Train, Reconstruct, Measure, Deploy, Bound
> The five clusters are not parallel attacks on one gap; they are ordered in *time* by when each acts, and A through E pass the residual down a pipeline. A1–A3 act at **train-time** (robustify the policy before deployment); B1–B3 act at **reconstruct-time** (invert reality into the simulator offline); C1–C3 act at **measure-time** (infer what the gap is, where each sim is trustworthy, and whether the real trials feeding that inference are even drawn from one distribution); D1–D3 act at **deploy-time** (close the residual the first three leave behind, online on hardware — [[2107.04034|RMA]] infers extrinsics, [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] adapts by differentiable TTA, [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] corrects by world-model supervision); E1–E3 act at **deploy-time under a safety constraint** (bound the residual that adaptation cannot remove). Each cluster's output is the next's input: B's reconstruction feeds C's measurement and D's adaptation; C1's trust map routes the sims D and E rely on; D's online updates are exactly what E1's [[2503.10949|SCDA]] and E2's [[2409.19190|RAIL]] must keep safe. The residual gap is not eliminated — it is moved down the timeline until only a *bounded* remainder reaches the user.

> [!tip] An Un-Handled Residual Gap Is a Safety Failure, Not Just a Performance Loss
> Cluster E reframes what C measures: the residual gap that survives A/B/C/D is not merely lost success — at deploy-time it is a *risk surface*. E1, E2, and E3 are the three ways to bound it. E1's [[2503.10949|SCDA]] constrains the *adaptation* (zero safety violations while D1/D3's online updates run, 20%→60% real grasp SR); E2's [[2409.19190|RAIL]] bounds the *action* (0% collisions vs 5–35% unshielded, ~10-pp SR cost: 68% vs 78%); E3's [[2506.09937|SAFE]] and [[2503.08558|FAIL-Detect]] flag the *failure* the other two can't rule out (<1 ms overhead, ~78%/72% detection, no failure labels). All three convert C1's per-factor untrustworthiness into a runtime guarantee — and crucially, each provides safety *without* failure-labeled data or a hand-crafted dense reward, because the sim2real failures you can label are not the ones the residual gap produces.

> [!tip] Online Adaptation Beats Robustification When Deployment Leaves the Randomization Range
> D1, D2, and D3 share one bet against Cluster A's train-time orthodoxy: a policy that *infers-then-conditions* on the true deployment dynamics beats one that *marginalizes* over them by domain randomization, precisely when deployment falls outside the train-time range. [[2107.04034|RMA]] proves it in locomotion (zero-real-fine-tune across sand/mud/12 kg payload); [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] in manipulation (+30.7% real SR from adapting, not robustifying); [[2508.21065|Learning-on-the-Fly]] in aerial control (81% hover-error reduction in 3 gradient steps); [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] with no real reward at all (0.96 [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]-10 under OOD via prediction-error supervision). The three split by which model is on hand — privileged extrinsics (D1), an analytical differentiable model (D2), or only a learned world model (D3) — but the common engine is a deploy-time signal that observes the latent the train-time policy could only guess at. This is the same estimation-over-marginalization principle B3/C2 apply offline, now running online on hardware.

> [!tip] Is Fidelity Even Binding? — The Open-Loop vs Closed-Loop Reconciling Experiment
> The doc carries a genuine internal tension. Clusters A/B/C bet that **sim fidelity (and the reconstruction-fidelity→forward-r law) is the binding constraint** — B1 makes "reconstruction fidelity is the new bottleneck" its thesis, C1 routes on per-factor correlation, A1 chases semantic fidelity. Clusters D/E bet the opposite: that **fidelity per se is *not* binding** because a deploy-time loop closes the residual online (D1's inference-past-the-range, D3's prediction-error correction) and E only has to *bound* whatever remains. Both cannot be unconditionally true. One controlled experiment reconciles them: take a task and sweep simulator fidelity from perfect down to a large dynamics error (e.g. up to 10× inversion error), and at each fidelity point run two execution modes — **open-loop** (roll the policy out once, no online correction) and **closed-loop** (replan / re-infer every T steps). If the **closed-loop curve flattens** the fidelity→SR relationship (SR stays high even at low fidelity once you replan), then fidelity *per se* is not binding and the D/E view holds — the binding constraint is the *online correction budget*, not the sim. If the **open-loop curve stays steep even with replanning** (SR tracks fidelity regardless of the loop), then the fidelity-correlation *is* the bottleneck and the A/B/C view holds. The same sweep also locates the regime boundary: the fidelity level below which no realistic replanning rate recovers SR is exactly where Cluster B's reconstruction investment becomes load-bearing rather than substitutable by Cluster D's online loop.

> [!tip] The Real Robot Is a Population, Not a Point — C3's Confound Reaches Every Provable Bound in the Doc
> C3 targets an assumption every other cluster's inference machinery inherits silently: that the real trials feeding a correlation, a confidence interval, or a conformal bound are drawn from one distribution, when they are actually drawn from whichever physical units happened to be on hand. B1's fidelity→r law (its own H1) is fit from reconstruction-fidelity/forward-r pairs pooled across however many units contributed trials — C3's H3 asks whether that "law" is a within-unit slope wearing a within-object label. C2's PPI and betting machinery (its own front-line bet) proves provable bounds *conditional on* the calibration and evaluation trials being exchangeable — C3's H2 asks whether a conformal detector calibrated on one unit keeps its coverage guarantee on another, with no other shift involved. E3's Robust-Conformal-CBF/CLF bound-preservation proof checks its Lipschitz-continuity assumption against *policy-induced* distribution shift only (its own Assumption 3) — C3 names unit identity as a second, independent way the same proof's premise can fail, one nobody has checked. None of B1, C2, or E3 is wrong on its own terms; each simply inherited "the real robot" as a point rather than a population, because that is what the field's tooling — one URDF per product line, one sysID fit per platform — has always handed them.

---

## Benchmark Gaps

| Gap | Direction | Existing closest |
|---|---|---|
| *Per-episode in-loop* affordance/material semantic randomization vs *fixed-cousin* asset-level semantic randomization, and vs appearance-only DR | A1 | [[2410.07408\|Digital-Cousins-ACDC]] (asset-level semantic cousins, 90% vs 25%, fixed per scene) + [[2604.11674\|AffordSim]] (~24%-avg zero-shot ceiling) + [[2604.25459\|GS-Playground]] (in-loop 3DGS, semantics-agnostic) |
| Physics-*law*-grounded (distribution-free) reward retention across contact-dynamics shift vs a *learned* transferable reward (and action transfer) | A2 | [[2602.19313\|TOPReward]] (reward-model benchmark: progress/completion/cross-embodiment robustness across 4 platforms — the closest reward-quality suite, but manipulation/learned-reward, not locomotion GRF) + [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] (cross-embodiment morphology-distance protocol, interp/extrap/composition splits — policy not reward, manipulation). No standardized benchmark exists for sensor-free GRF-estimation accuracy or reward-retention under contact-dynamics shift — these remain field gaps (ad-hoc per-paper R², [[2604.23702\|QuietWalk]] self-numbers) |
| Gain×dynamics *interaction term* via co-optimization (super-additivity) + co-optimized DR-variable beating a *runtime* adaptive-gain residual | A3 | [[2604.02523\|Tune-to-Learn]] (gains-vs-sysID inversion, diagnoses but never co-optimizes) — measured on [[2603.15469\|RoCo-Challenge]] (sim+real assembly tracks, Sim-to-Real Cliff) / [[2305.17110\|IndustReal]] (NIST-board peg/gear transfer SR; ingest)-class contact substrates; no benchmark today isolates the gain×dynamics super-additivity term or the deployment oscillation spectrum (H2/H3 remain method-internal field gaps) |
| *Monotone* reconstruction-fidelity → forward-correlation law across rigid/articulated/deformable (where the navigation counter-example does not reach) | B1 | [[2505.17966\|Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics]] (graded Chamfer 2 mm/5 mm/1 cm → grasp-transfer SR ~50% over 12 recon models on YCB-Video + Aria — the closest concrete reconstruction-fidelity-axis suite) + [[2508.11117\|Robot-Policy-Eval-Sim2Real]] (names sim-to-real transfer-*matching* metrics + 5 perturbations + graded-success metrics as the eval protocol — supplying the dependent-variable definition the fidelity→r regression needs, though it runs no graded-fidelity sweep and reports no r itself) + [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] (joint inversion, push-T r=0.915 / rope 0.901 vs [[2511.04831\|Isaac-Lab]] 0.649 / 0.237) + [[2207.10821\|Lower-Fidelity-Sim2Real]] (lower fidelity → higher transfer in navigation — non-monotone). The graded-fidelity-sweep-vs-forward-correlation protocol itself does not yet exist — the monotone-law-across-object-classes sweep remains a field gap. The deformable inversion side of the bet (H4) is now populated by two complementary uncited keepers — [[2602.02402\|SoMA-Sim]] (real-to-sim deformable inversion, resim PSNR 33.51 vs PhysTwin 28.77) and [[2606.15338\|SimWeaver]] (deformable forward SR 91.30% from 200 sim demos) — but neither reports a fidelity→forward-r correlation, so the graded reconstruction-error→transfer DV for deformables specifically remains a measurement gap. [[2608.05948\|GAUGE]] supplies the closest thing to a graded ground-truth instrument today — 22 real task families with calibrated physical properties across rigid/textile/volumetric-deformable, exposing order-of-magnitude engine error even given correct parameters — but it scores engines/world-models against known GT, not video-reconstructed $(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ against forward robot-SR, so the reconstruction-fidelity→transfer sweep itself is still unrun |
| *Fidelity-gated* per-task fold-back loop that is monotone across rounds (drift control) + reconstruction-fidelity exchange rate, vs a single feedback pass | B2 | [[2506.18088\|RoboTwin-2.0]] (50-task / 731-object / 147-category object-twin benchmark, +24.4% real few-shot / +21% zero-shot unseen-object generalization — the closest real benchmark substrate for the generalization-SR leg) + [[2512.00076\|Arcadia]] (closed lifecycle, single feedback-on/off pass, scene-level). But NO named suite measures the per-round monotone-vs-drift trajectory or the fidelity-gated fold-back loop (field gap). [[2412.01770\|CASHER]] demonstrates the deployment-scan fold-back that previously had no in-KH instance, but only as a single unsupervised scene-scale pass — so the field gap narrows from "deployment-grounded fold-back is unmeasured" to "fold-back is shown at scene scale (CASHER, Arcadia) but the per-task object-twin round-over-round monotone-vs-drift trajectory and fidelity gate still have no named benchmark" |
| Learned constitutive *law* beating a parameter fit on *unseen geometry* (the delta MASIV's material-type recovery skips) + surviving a *closed robot* real→sim→real loop | B3 | Recovery-fidelity substrate: [[2506.02794\|PhysGaia]] (GT 3D trajectories + GT material params across liquids/gases/viscoelastic/textiles, physical-realism metrics); parameter-fit baseline: [[2510.22975\|VoMP]] (166-object material-estimation benchmark, Young's-modulus ALRE 0.0409). NO shared suite exists for the two defining axes — learned-law-vs-parameter-fit geometry extrapolation to 1M particles (only ad-hoc per-method tasks: [[2508.01112\|MASIV]]/[[2304.14369\|NCLaw]]) and the closed robot real→sim→real loop (only the [[2511.04665\|Real-to-Sim-GS]] method loop) — both remain field gaps. H3's cheap real-video-only recovery for manipulation deformables now has a robot-loop existence proof on the parameter-fit side: [[2606.16202\|EgoPhys]] recovers a generalizable stiffness prior from single egocentric RGB video and closes the real→sim→real loop on an xArm6 (77.6% config-error reduction, zero-shot to unseen objects) — but for a *fixed* spring-mass form, so it narrows the robot-loop gap for chosen-law parameter priors while leaving B3's learned-constitutive-*form*-in-the-loop (H2) still unproven |
| Per-factor *correlation* (not SR sensitivity) re-measured under OOD, routed as a per-(sim,factor) trust gate vs one global edge | C1 | [[2602.11337\|MolmoSpaces]] (per-factor SR sensitivity, high global R=0.96, no per-factor r, no router) + [[2402.08191\|THE-COLOSSEUM]] (14-factor SR drop) + [[2604.24018\|Sim2Real-Betting]] (aggregate-edge routing). [[2111.00765\|VSDR]] shows acting on a sim-derived validity score to select policies is feasible from ~64 real observations, narrowing the selection-mechanism side of the gap but leaving the per-factor *correlation* re-measurement and per-(sim,factor) routing — not per-policy selection from one combined score — as C1's unclosed half |
| Bank *composition* — bias-diversity vs bias-count CI-tightening + the compute-budget crossover map (portfolio-vs-single is taken) | C2 | [[2606.05159\|X4Val]] (CI tightening from heterogeneous non-paired sources, 15–38.4% AD+ManiSkill — closest to the diversity lever, but never sweeps diversity-vs-count or maps the compute crossover) + [[2506.20553\|Sim2Val]] (paired-source control variates, provable bound) + [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] (PPI CIs, 700 sims). No named benchmark sweeps bias-diversity-vs-count at fixed bank size or maps the compute-budget crossover (H5 crossover + the diversity-vs-count Δ remain field gaps). The defining bet axes remain unmet: no suite sweeps bias-*diversity* vs bias-*count* at matched bank size (H2) nor maps the compute-budget crossover (H5); [[2603.13616\|N-SCORE]] supplies a robotics-grounded anytime-valid CI estimator and [[2510.16166\|Conformal-PPI]] a shift-robust PPI variant, but both evaluate a single channel rather than a composed bank, so the diversity-vs-count Δ and crossover protocol are still field gaps |
| Between-unit outcome-variance decomposition (ICC attributable to physical-unit identity) + conformal-coverage transfer across units of the same platform, holding task/environment/policy fixed | C3 | [[2506.18123\|RoboArena]] (names "manufacturing differences between said robots" as an obstacle, pools 7 institutions' DROID units into one ranking with no unit term) + [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (10-machine/4-type fleet, catalogues tester bias and lighting/camera-intrinsics drift but never inter-unit hardware variance). No named benchmark logs or analyzes results by physical-unit identity; the Duckietown reproducibility study (arXiv:2009.04362, off-domain, 2020) is the only paper measuring inter-unit variance directly (MPD std 3.4 cm inter-robot vs 1.3 cm same-robot), and it has never been re-applied to the manipulation/VLA literature this document surveys — the field-acknowledged absence is total: no cited benchmark reports a between-unit variance component at all |
| Continued latent-extrinsics inference *beating robust-fallback OUTSIDE the DR range* (the inverse of GRAM's give-up-OOD conclusion) | D1 | [[2412.18781\|Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation-Eval]] (graded actuator-failure + friction-variation perturbation protocol on MuJoCo legged control — the closest standardized dynamics-OOD-*severity* substrate) + [[2508.20294\|DALI]] (formalizes Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed regimes over mass/friction/gravity/actuator-strength context — the named *distance-past-the-range* generalization protocol, sim-only on DMC/DreamerV3) — but no suite implements the inverse-of-GRAM protocol itself (matched robust-fallback vs continued-inference, SR-gap GROWING with distance OUTSIDE the DR range on the same task); [[2412.04323\|GRAM-Robust-Adaptation]] and [[2107.04034\|RMA]] are the method baselines, not a benchmark. D1's H2 envelope (where does the latent-extrinsics estimate stay accurate past the DR range?) now has two instruments — [[1812.03399\|Latent-Variable-MBRL]]'s Bayesian-posterior uncertainty estimate demarcates the trustworthy envelope, and [[2508.20294\|DALI]] supplies the graded extrapolation-regime axis to sweep against — but neither runs on real hardware, so the field gap narrows from "no distance-past-range protocol exists" to "DALI's sim extrapolation-regime sweep exists, but the *real-robot* SR-vs-distance-past-range loop and the matched inverse-of-GRAM head-to-head are still unmeasured" |
| *Learned-residual* BPTT adapting a *learned neural* policy on a disturbance the residual captures, vs M-GAPS's analytical-only gradient route | D2 | [[2510.03471\|Quadcopter-Control-Eval-Suite]] (standardized RotorPy aerial-adaptation suite: wind/payload/rotor-fault/latency disturbances, L1-MPC/Geometric/INDI baselines, tracking-RMSE + stress-test metrics — the closest STANDARDIZED substrate for the aerial headline, but it benchmarks robust-adaptive *control* methods, not learned-residual-BPTT *neural-policy* adaptation, so the learned-residual-vs-analytical comparison itself is still unrun) + [[2507.10914\|M-GAPS]] (analytical-controller online gradient, no learned residual) + [[2508.21065\|Learning-on-the-Fly]] (learned-residual BPTT, method self-numbers). The D2 gap line (AdaptiveQuadBench) can name [[2606.09640\|Physics-Aware-Sparse-EL]] as the cross-platform structure-preserving learned-residual that demonstrably captures payload-release the analytical core cannot — the H1-falsifier residual substrate — while still leaving the learned-residual-*BPTT*-adapting-a-neural-policy comparison unrun, since it updates the model via Bayesian (not gradient) steps rather than a learned policy |
| *Real-robot* 4 Hz unified-WM+force prediction-error correction with a *force*-prediction-error term for contact OOD, vs an image-foresight-only head | D3 | OOD-recovery substrate: [[2510.13626\|LIBERO-Plus]] (7×21 perturbation dims, the suite T3VF runs on) + [[2402.08191\|THE-COLOSSEUM]] (friction/mass physical perturbation, R̄²=0.614 sim↔real) for the visual-vs-physical OOD split; real-robot + contact substrate: [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (Table30 real fleet, soft-body/contact split 8% SR); WM-hallucination substrate: [[2604.19092\|RoboWM-Bench]] (executability + unstable-contact-prediction failure modes). Genuine field gap (no named suite): graded force/contact-OOD recovery sweep + the force-prediction-error correction-quality metric itself (methods-only) |
| Zero-violation continual-adaptation constraint *wrapping an arbitrary Cluster-D engine* on real hardware across successive domains (composition, not isolation) | E1 | [[2604.19737\|Safe-Continual-RL-NSCMDP]] (in-KH benchmark — Damaged-HalfCheetah/Ant + Safe Continual World cost+forgetting protocol over 8 algorithms, but sim/control-bench, isolated, not a real-hardware wrapper) + [[2310.12567\|Safety-Gymnasium]] (canonical safe-RL violation/budget suite, SafePO 16 algorithms, sim-only single-domain). No benchmark exists for the real-hardware zero-violation-wrapping-of-a-Cluster-D-engine-across-successive-domains composition the bet turns on. E1's composition bet now has a model-based foil: [[2407.04942\|FOSP]] is a world-model safe offline→online engine that hits near-zero violations on a real Franka in 40 gradient steps, but like [[2503.10949\|SCDA]] it runs on its own loop, so the open gap sharpens to whether a safe-adaptation constraint (parametric SCDA or model-based FOSP) wraps an arbitrary Cluster-D engine across successive domains — no paper yet demonstrates the wrapper on hardware |
| Hard reachability shield over a *changing* (online-adapting) policy mid-update + uncertainty-aware geometry (hard-vs-soft is taken) | E2 | [[2507.00435\|RoboEval]] (per-policy collisions/jerk/slips reported jointly with SR + stage-wise progression, 8 tasks × 3–5 variations — the collision-vs-SR measurement substrate) + [[2512.22539\|VLA-Arena]] (CBDDL Safety suite reporting cumulative safety cost jointly with SR under L0–L2 perturbation — the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier, pairs with THE COLOSSEUM's SR-drop generator). NO named benchmark exists for the defining axis — a hard reachability shield over a CHANGING/online-adapting policy with collision rate measured during the adaptation window (method-self-numbers-only: [[2409.19190\|RAIL]] / [[2511.06385\|Path-Consistent-Safety-Filter]]). [[2511.07899\|Ensemble-Safety-Filters]] supplies a probabilistic trajectory-level (Beta-certified, conformal) violation-rate metric that RoboEval/VLA-Arena's deterministic collision counts lack — the benchmark gap should add a statistical-assurance endpoint (certified violation probability vs SR), not just binary collision rate, to measure a hard shield over a changing policy |
| Detect-then-*act* loop that *preserves* the conformal FPR bound + cross-architecture transfer of internal-feature detectors (success-only detection is taken) | E3 | [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]] (named localization+identification+correction benchmark, 3-level taxonomy, sim+real closed-loop SR 47.5%→61.25% — the localization→response substrate) + [[2510.17950\|RoboChallenge]] (generic real-SR substrate for the detect-then-act user-facing-failure endpoint). The bet's two DEFINING novel axes — H3 (FPR-bound preservation through a detect-then-act loop) and H4 (cross-architecture detection transfer) — have NO benchmark in the field; their substrate must be assembled from RoboChallenge's real SR + per-method detection setups ([[2510.09459\|FIPER]] / [[2506.09937\|SAFE]] / [[2503.08558\|FAIL-Detect]]). The [[2212.14020\|System-Level-OOD-Robotics]] survey names the missing closed-loop-OOD benchmark outright, sharpening E3's gap from "no benchmark for H3/H4" to a field-acknowledged absence of any episodic closed-loop-OOD evaluation suite — but it supplies framing, not the FPR-preservation or cross-architecture protocol the bet still needs |
